# Character State

Updated after **Volume 02 Batch 0001 (Chapters 61–70)**, the opening block of Volume 02. Volume 01's entries are left in place and the Volume 02 movement is appended to each of them under a dated heading; nothing in the Volume 01 record has been altered. Keep this file to immediate state, wants, and known costs. Long-range arcs live in `bible/characters.md`; canon rules live in `state/continuity.md`.

**Where everyone is on Sunday the twenty-eighth of December.** The river core has won a case it cannot pay for and has just had the best week of its life and the worst document of its life, and the case itself has not moved: the order is **suspended** since ten o'clock on the twelfth and **not discharged**, the investigation is open, **the hearing is still not set sixteen days later, the reporting officer is still not named, and the reporting officer must still be a person other than any person named in the Prefect's letter or in the order.** Two hundred and fifty-two people are still on a per-head schedule at two and four a head a week that a woman of about thirty-four signed for. A hundred and forty feet of named frontage is still behind a fence with a settlement plate on it. **A tram of thirty-one passengers, a conductor and a driver has come back and nobody in the city can produce a record that it was ever anywhere.** Milo's last entry for the year is at 16.40 on the twenty-eighth and the last line of it is that he is not the author of any of it.

## Milo Rook

- **Where he is:** the Civic Atlas Office, the route room, the second of the two tables under the north window, at about half past four on the afternoon of the fifteenth, with the Field Book open at a blank.
- **Stage:** 1 — trace-sight. Unchanged and not advanced. **Volume 1 ends at Stage 1.**
- **Crossings: eight, all in Volume 1.** The fourth, sixth, eighteenth, twenty-first and twenty-sixth of Sazur, the tenth of the month at 14.06, **the seventh of December at 16.06** and **the eighth of December at 09.06**. Both new ones read out in a yard with Dr Sen's tally present, all four signed conditions satisfied, and **each cost a route.** Neither crossing is a page of counting: the counts live on Dr Sen's tally, which has eight lines on it, and the book carries the two passages as dated time entries — the seventh at 16.06 and 16.35 on the seventh, the eighth at 09.06 and 09.33 on the eighth.
- **Cost paid: both stages of the orientation blank, and they are two costs and not one.** **First stage: ten named remembered routes**, the original eight intact and none removed, plus **the hundred and one steps of the stair tower at the Alder Quay interchange** (given by Ola Petrossian on a card at the door, the seventh of December) and **the walk of the eighteenth of October with a lamp and a cat in a laundry bag** (marked on the ninth by a woman of sixty from the second landing, at Rue's insistence and Milo's not asking). All ten recoverable when marked, **all ten marked by somebody who is not him.** **Second stage: the written line home, which went on Monday the twenty-fourth of November at about ten past eight in the morning** — nine lines, his own hand, the oldest entry in the book, gone clean, the page there and the ink on either side the same. **It is not persistent inside the volume: he can still find home when it is marked, and the marking comes from somebody who is not him.** He chalked his own door on the twenty-eighth, was given three chalk arrows on his own building by the fourteenth of December, **asked two people to write the route for him in the last chapter and was refused twice by people who had no reason to refuse him, and the blank is still blank.** He has written down in order that it might be a cost, or a bad memory, or a page that needs another person's mark, and that he does not know which, and has not decided. **He is not cured and the loss is not a wound he closes.**
- **The token: cold, cold, and once not.** Cold on the seventeenth of November, and on the fourteenth and fifteenth of December, and he did not take it out. **On the seventh of December, on the reach, it was warm for about four minutes and the negative space in the front of it lined up with a hundred and forty feet of made ground, exactly, and he could have opened something and did not.** The reason is written on the facing page and is the whole of the batch: *if a street is open because of something I can take out of my coat, then the three answers in this district stop being three answers the moment one of them is a thing I can put back.* **He did not use it as a key, did not summon anybody, did not replace a witness, and did not make the old lane into a replica.**
- **What he gave away, and what it cost.** The sheet, four versions, the rule at the foot, the design, the authorship, the custody, and finally **the crossing itself**: the map he entered the reach with is Rue's two-column sheet with a rule at the foot, and he read the rule out loud on the reach because *the whole of the argument of six weeks is that a map with no rule at the foot is an invitation to whoever has got a pen.* He calls himself **the one who can be replaced.** He was in a corridor for ninety minutes on the fifteenth while Lena Marr stood up in a hall, and he has written that down as the sum of three months and has not been able to add a line to it.
- **His part, as he now names it:** the columns, the rule at the foot, the versions, the arithmetic, the refusal, and the map. **He is not the author, the holder, the clerk, or the custodian of any of it.**
- **What he said on the reach, in daylight, with four hundred people inside a quarter of a mile, and it is in a hearing transcript:** the order's public safety claim, the missing worker record, the current residents' presence while the crossing is open, and — because Doreen Sankey made him — *a cartographer went over the low end of this street on the eighteenth of October with a lamp and a cat in a laundry bag, and I am not going in as a man with a piece of paper in my hand and I am not going in for Lark Street.*
- **The clue chain, carried and not advanced.** Two documents carry a pair of marks, not three. The reopened order file produced a **fourth document with one mark in it** — a carbon relocation schedule, a destination with no official address, a question about the destination and not about the order — and he has written on the page that it is **not a third pair**, because a pair is two marks in two hands on one page. **He has twice, in his own book, recorded that he is not joining the carbon to the turn-sheet copy or to Pell's copying tick, and why: joining them would be him deciding that they are the same.**
- **What is on his page and is not his:** a community and memorial register entry marked contested, a district drainage book with a name and a date in it, a works maintenance schedule, a printed tram rule book, three chalk arrows on his own building, and a nine-line blank in his own book.
- **His standing in the file:** his interpretation remains excluded; the level remains under a foreman's signature and **is not back in his file and may not be put back**; the joint finding of the twentieth of October and the fourth of December are both in it; and the classification of the eleventh of the current year, which was his colleague's, is withdrawn and was made correctly on the day it was made.


### Milo Rook — after Chapter 70, the end of Volume 02 Batch 0001

- **Where he is:** the Civic Atlas Office, the route room, the second of the two tables under the north window, at about twenty to five on the afternoon of Sunday the twenty-eighth of December, with the Field Book open, and with **a second tin on the bottom shelf of the plan room under the repair schedule that he did not ask for and cannot take back and is not the custodian of.**
- **Stage: the transition into Stage 2 has begun and does not complete.** The outline places field grammar across Volumes 2–3. He has identified **Cadence** as the dominant grammar of the back length and **Keepsake** as the dominant grammar of Saltmarket Row, and he has **stabilised nothing and redirected nothing**, because a length is not a seam and there was nothing to hold. **He has not gained a power in this batch and the batch is built so that his new ability makes things worse when he tries it.**
- **Crossings: nine. Eight in Volume 01, and the ninth on Monday the twenty-second of December, in at 11.06 and out at 11.41, thirty-five minutes inside, from the north end of the back length, with the quay section gang of eleven men and a foreman.** Count held by Pell Harrow with a counter and no words for thirty-five minutes; walked in and out by Doreen Sankey; twelve minutes stated; all four conditions of October satisfied; the fourth condition at a quarter to eight by Ola Petrossian, who was not asked. **Read out in a yard to about nineteen people at about ten past twelve with Iona Sen's tally, which now has nine lines on it, and she makes him say the number, and it is the seventh time she has made him.**
- **The orientation ledger: the remembered-route list stands at TEN, the original eight intact, none removed, all ten recoverable when marked and all ten marked by somebody who is not him — and the ninth crossing did not cost a route.** He has written that down twice, in his own hand, and says he does not know why and will not decide. **The list may be added to and may not be removed from by later batches.**
- **The second stage is now persistent inside the volume, and it is the batch's real cost.** The written line home went on the twenty-fourth of November and is **the thirty-fifth day of it on the twenty-eighth of December, and it is not coming back.** It has been marked three times in three hands with a date against each — a nurse of twenty-three on a card, a woman who keeps a round on a card, a boy of seventeen with chalk on a second-floor landing with no date on it — **and it is still blank.** On the evening of the twenty-seventh at 21.40 he wrote the only sentence in the book in the imperative about himself: *The line home is not coming back. Write it down and stop asking.* **He asked nobody to fill it in for three weeks before that and nobody offered, and Iona Sen told him in a yard to notice that he had done it quietly and that a thing you do quietly is a thing that is going to get done.**
- **The Stage 2 cost, and it is exactly two things and no more: a sound and a taste.** On the twenty-second, at four seconds into a tram gong on the length, **he could not call up the pitch of the third gong of a Line 3 morning car, and he needed it**, because a conductor who has heard a person say *clear* answers; he used the rule at the foot of a sheet in a foreman's hand instead. On the twenty-third, at about four in the afternoon, he could not remember the taste of the tea he had made at five that morning, and he did not need it. **No face, no place, no person, no name. He has written the two in the order they happened and not in the order they frighten him, and he does not know whether it is one thing or two, and has decided nothing.**
- **Mara Quill wrote his return route and it is a working instrument and not a comfort.** A sheet of accountancy paper, in biro, with a heading, a date, an *out* and a *back*, **three hands on it** — her route, Rue's two corrections, his date and version number — **and four copies, in the route room, in Crown Heights, in the yard under a brick, and a fourth place she did not tell him about.** It was tested against not working: Rue walked him from the yard gate to his own front door without letting him look at it, at his own request, and it took nineteen minutes and he could not have told you which way the chandler's gate was. He is not its author, does not own it, does not want to own it, and has said in his own book that he would rather be a man kept by three other people than a man who can find his own door.
- **What he is not.** He is **not the author of the tram's route.** The sheet that records the ninth crossing is in a rack in an office four rooms above a fitter's shop, and the reason on it is in a foreman's hand, and he is not in it, and **he did not put a single word on it about the passage, because there was nowhere to put one.** He is not cured. His flaw is intact and the batch was built to worsen it, because the thing he was asked to do is the thing he wants to own, and what he did instead was change his method twice, in public, in front of strangers, and be wrong in a room with nine people in it, and not ask the one person who could have helped him.
- **The Lark token: cold, and not used, and not taken out.** Cold on the sixteenth, seventeenth, nineteenth, twentieth, twenty-first, twenty-second, twenty-third and twenty-seventh — **eight of the thirteen days of the batch — and the seventh of December is still the last time it was anything else.** He wrote down the reason on the facing page of the twenty-second and the sentence has not improved in three weeks and he is not going to improve it.
- **The Field Book: nine dated pairs — six days, nine days, three, three, two, three, three, five and eight — and a tenth interval of a single day from the tenth of December that was looked at and not written and stays looked at.** **Four threes, one six, one nine, one two, one five and one eight, and he joins none of them to any other and does not write down what he thinks nine of a thing means.** Atlas residue was used **once in ten chapters**, in Chapter 66, in **five** words: *A trade outlives its pitch.*
- **The clue chain, carried and not advanced.** The copied tick, the copied turn sheet and the carbon relocation schedule **are not named or joined to anything in Chapters 61–70.** One new copy was found — a gang's own weekly sheet, and a routing slip about four inches by six inches with *for index* in the purpose box — and **he refuses twice to put it in a column with anything, for the same bad reason as in November: a column is a case, and he has made one twice in eleven weeks and both times the column made itself into a case.**

## Mara Quill

- **Where she is:** the Registry, with a two-year note on her conduct, a public safety appeal of nineteen paragraphs on a Crown Heights envelope with a division mark on it, a hearing to come, and an attachment in a file that will be produced against her for the rest of her working life.
- **What the review on the third of December found:** that she worked the file off her own hours for six years in a records alcove without telling her review panel, and that **the fourth set of initials in the annex box of an intake routing slip of the sixth year back** was a matter she ought to have declared and did not. **She read the finding out in a corridor to about nine people and about six of them did not understand a word of it. She lost preferred access, and appealed on the fourth in one line, and it is not stayed.**
- **What she did that was the mechanism, and it cost her nothing she can get back:** on the fourth of the month she refused the department's request for a schedule of persons resident at the withdrawn alignment. **The answer is NO and it is on the file in her own hand with her own date, and it says in terms that a nil return is capable of being read as a representation that no persons are resident there, and that she does not know that, and that she knows the schedule is short by twenty-one.** The empty box in the appeal of the eighth of December is the only reason she can stand in a room and ask for an order to be suspended. **She certified the head count of two hundred and fifty-two on the fifteenth with a declaration of interest at the top of it, which is the third thing in a file about her own hand and the thing that finished the review.**
- **What she refused, and did not explain to anybody or get thanked for:** to be kind to him about the line going out of his book (*you have had four people be kind to you in two days and you are going into a room on Monday week with a folder under your arm, and if you go in there having been comforted you will lose it*); to speak for a household; to produce the turn sheet in any room (*I know of no residents' document*); to call an unsworn mechanic as a man rather than as a document; to go to the Bench before the event; and to ask a clerk in a hall of thirty people to look up a file number.
- **What she said that nobody argued with:** *on the sixth of December there is going to be an event, and an event makes a record that has to be written down by somebody, and if I am standing in front of the only record anybody has of a man who died in a pump house six years ago, then the record gets written, and it gets written in a room I am in. That is why we are going on the eighth of December and not on the sixteenth of November.*
- **The relationship, one step and no warmth, and the step is standing next to him and not being able to do anything about it.** She said the thing she has not said — *on the twenty-ninth of Sazur a man promised me that nothing of his bearing on this file would reach me in writing with a date on it, and I held that for forty-four days and then I made him give away the design instead, and he gave it away in about nine seconds and he did not complain, and that is the most decent thing anybody has done for me in six years and I have not said so to him and I am not going to* — in a yard of about eleven, and about nine of them looked at him and he looked at the ground. **Nothing romantic has happened and nothing is going to. She is not going to write his return route for him in Volume 1 and it was never hers to write, and he has not asked. The promise stands and is hers and does not get wider.**


### Mara Quill — after Chapter 70, the end of Volume 02 Batch 0001

- **Where she is:** the Registry, and a front room, with a two-year note on her conduct, an appeal in one line that is not stayed, no preferred access, **a route custody register page copied on the nineteenth of December in a drawer in her flat with a coat in it**, nineteen paragraphs and three attachments on a Crown Heights envelope, and a piece of paper on a man in a railway coat that she is not going to use.
- **The unauthorised copy, and what it cost her, in the code's own words.** She read the one line twice and did not read it a third time, because the third reading is where a person begins looking for a way round it. She then asked the clerk to bring her the page and **copied four lines and a continuation sheet onto a sheet of her own with a pencil, in ninety seconds, at a table in a records alcove, with the door open.** A man of about forty-five in Survey Enforcement walked past, looked at the page, kept walking. **She did not follow him**, because a woman who goes after a man to ask him about it has a document about a state of mind in her file, and a woman in an alcove with a door open has only a door. **She is not proud of that reasoning and has written it down anyway, because a person who will not write down the reasoning they used is a person who has a second one.**
- **What the copy says, and the milestone's second half.** *Withdrawn from running diagram: 13 Oct, service amendment 12/44, under standing instruction 11/D/6. Requesting body: PW quay section. Route custody after withdrawal: nil.* On a continuation sheet in a different hand: *Instruments, routes, or objects recorded on the above at the time of withdrawal: **one.** 1. Route token, brass, hand-stamped **LARK ST.**, no number. Issue not in current series. Held by: **ROOK.** Authority for issue: [no entry]. Returned: [no entry].* **So she now knows that the reason he is where he is is a physical object with a custody entry somebody else made, and it has no address on it, and nobody signed for it, and it was on that length on the day the length came off the map. He does not know that she knows. She has not told him, has not used it, and is not going to.**
- **The distinction she wrote out in full, because she needed to know which of two things she was doing.** *That is one thing — the fourth set of initials, a thing she has not done and has not chosen every day for four months, and the kind of not-doing that gets worse the longer it goes on, and she has a review about it. This is not that. This is a thing she read on Friday, in ninety seconds, in full knowledge of one line of a code she can recite; and a man's name in a column with nothing after it; and a fact about him that has nothing to do with the tram, and she has had it for a day and a half and has not used it.* And underneath: *if he ever finds out that I know, he will not thank me, and he will not be angry either, and that is the part I would like written down, because I have worked out in advance that I will not be able to bear it and I would like there to be a piece of paper in which I said so first.*
- **Milestone 2, and it is a professional act.** She wrote his return route on the Sunday night at about twenty past eleven, in a porch, in the rain, in about forty minutes, **and he did not ask and she did not offer, and the reason she did it is in her own mouth: *I am going to write a route, and I am not going to do it kindly… There is no instrument in this city that says a man came back. A man who cannot find the way he came is a man who has to be produced from somewhere afterwards, and if the only person who can produce him is his own memory then in about four months a department will produce him instead, out of a filing system, and it will not be the same man.*** **She is a lawyer writing a route and two of her four words were wrong and a woman who keeps a round found both in about ninety seconds**, and the corrections are in Rue's hand on the back. **Three hands on the card and four copies and it is not in his bag and not in her bag and not in Rue's satchel, and the fourth place she wrote down on the back and did not tell him about, and that was the only time in six weeks she kept something from him on purpose and did not use it.** She did not wish him goodnight and said, *Version one, and there will be a version two if it is wrong, and I would like to be the person who is wrong about this and not you, because you are the man who will get it right in a field and not know that he has got it wrong.*
- **What she told the river core, and what she is going to do about it.** Four things in a yard on the Friday afternoon: **three documents carry one standing-instruction number and three documents is three documents and she is not going to call it a pattern this week**; the amendment is not within the order's investigation, the tram authority is not the Registry, and nobody in Crown Heights can put the two in a room; **the joining is her job and not a yard's and she would rather be stopped by nine people in a yard than by a panel in a building in about four months**; and the department will say in writing, on a date, that it has no record of six hundred yards of tram route, **because the record is not lying and it was written by a man who was asked for a reason and gave a filing number.** She has now told the yard she is going to do it, and nobody has stopped her.
- **What she refused in this batch:** to follow the man in the corridor; to read the thirty-one names out in a yard in the rain; to let a twenty-four-year-old clerk be the man who asks after a record outside her access; to be named on Cass Ardery's asking; and — on the twenty-eighth, in front of about nine people — **to let the Lark Street business be discussed, and she let it be refused in four words and did not soften it afterwards.**
- **Nothing romantic has happened and nothing is going to. The promise of the twenty-ninth of Sazur stands and is hers and does not get wider, and what widened this month is not the promise, it is her knowledge, and she has not used it and is not going to.**

## Pell Harrow

- **Where he is:** the round, out at eight and in at four, and in a yard in the rain. Seventeen. A courier and a Streetkeeper. **He has no power of any kind and is not Milo's assistant.**
- **Tern: still no answer.** He has not seen him. He has not been asked about it in this batch and did not raise it, and nothing is resolved, and the list in the tin is not a finding and Ostrow confirms nothing.
- **What he did:** held the count on the **eighth crossing** with a counter and no words for twenty-seven minutes, and *that was the quietest half hour of my year and I am not going to say anything else about it*; **came back at about four miles an hour and said *Your mark is off* and then four words — *it was me on Saturday at eleven… I am not going to do it again tonight because it does not do anything at two o'clock in the morning and I have not seen it do anything at two o'clock in the morning* — and refused, unprompted, to have a mark asked of him overnight**; **chalked an arrow on the outside of Milo's own building at about five feet on the Sunday night of the fourteenth, dated, and said nothing**; and a third arrow on the wall of the second-floor landing on the same night, **with no date on it, and nobody has ever asked who put it there and nobody is going to.**
- **The limit, now tested and stated on the page:** a mark holds for a **working shift**, on the way out and on the way back in, and **does not hold overnight**, and nobody in the district is going to be asked to hold one overnight, **and that includes the boy.** Rue said it in a yard and it is the first rule anybody drops under pressure.
- **What he still refuses:** to be the reason a document is made; to make the round into a service; to be leaned on; to go and look at the tick in the fourth column; and **to walk a route for a man who cannot find his own door, on the grounds that a person who is passing is not a person who has to be there.**


### Pell Harrow — after Chapter 70, the end of Volume 02 Batch 0001

- **Tern: still not found, and it was the first thing he said on the Tuesday and he put it first so that nobody had to look at him to find out.** No word in six weeks. **He is going up the south again on the twenty-eighth, whatever happens to this**, and he said in the yard in December that the list in the tin is not a finding and Ostrow confirms nothing, and nothing in this batch changes that.
- **What he is now, in this case, and it is not a role: the man who found the man in the works jacket.** A man of about fifty, a badge with a number and not a name, on the quay on the fourteenth of December — **which Chapter 62 writes as *Monday the fourteenth*, and which the CR-019 chain and Volume 01 Chapter 60 both make a **Sunday**; that is an inherited slip in an already-audited block, it is recorded here rather than silently repaired because the chapter is the authority and the chapter was not reopened, and a later batch must use Sunday** — and on the Wednesday morning and on the Monday the twenty-second, at about forty feet from the north end for about an hour **with a notebook out, writing in it twice while the gang walked the length.** **Pell did not follow him and says that was a decision and not a forgetting, and has written down what the man was wearing.** *A man who is going to steal a thing is quick. A man who is going to look at a thing carefully, on purpose, for as long as it takes to be sure, and then put it back exactly as it was — that is a man who is going to come back for it, and that is worse, and I have not seen him do the second thing yet.* **No name, no copy, no resolution, and it must not become a new face.**
- **What he did, and it is two things and both of them are his.** He **held the count on the ninth crossing with a counter and said no words for thirty-five minutes**, and he has no power of any kind and cannot hold a route, open one, or keep one, and he was not asked to do anything else. And on the Friday the twenty-sixth he **put a thing on a foreman's desk and got a second tin**: *A thing that exists in one place is a thing that has not happened yet.* The gang's weekly duplicate has gone in a tin at the end of a corridor for years and now goes in **a second tin in the plan room of the Civic Atlas Office**, because *a copy of a record of work is not a record, and a record is a thing a department can produce against you, and no department in this city is going to be stopped by two tins* — which is Dilley Farr's sentence, given in answer, and both of them are right.
- **His other contribution, and it is the cheapest thing in the batch and the most useful: a dog.** *A dog is not a document and a dog is the only witness in this case that nobody can put in a file.* And a gong behind a cold store at half past four on three evenings, eleven seconds each, and on the Tuesday it went for two hours — and when Milo Rook said *I heard it*, Pell Harrow said *you did not.*
- **What he still refuses:** to be the reason a document is made; to make the round into a service; to be leaned on; to be on a sheet; **to follow a stranger through a yard in a district where he is the one person everybody knows; and to be thanked.**
- **The limit on his mark is unchanged and is not widened in this batch: a mark holds for a working shift and not overnight, and nobody in this district is going to be asked to hold one overnight.** His second tin is a carbon in a plan room and is not a mark and does not touch the rule.

## Rue

- **Where she is:** the round, the trestle table, the porch, and the wall on the north path.
- **What she did in this batch, and each of it is now a rule somebody else has to keep:** **refused to be handed Lena Marr's notice**, and put the passage on her round instead, out at eight and in at four, *and if you say it to anybody else then you are on somebody else's thing, and I do not want it*; **refused to be the count on the seventh** and said so at a table with thirty people in it and did not fix it in advance; **stated the overnight rule in a yard without softening it**; **made a rule in four seconds on the Sunday morning — thirty people behind a woman at eleven feet and not one of them touching her arm — and did not explain it**; **crossed her own line out of the return map** because *if I am on this then anybody can lean on me*; and **told him, in the rain at a gate, that nobody is going to ask him for a passage, and that on the sixth there would not be one person at a door who had agreed to be there** — which is the reason the district is built around other people and not around him.
- **What she refused twice in the last chapter:** to write his route home, *that is mine, and mine is three streets and a chandler's yard and a pie shop, and I do not know a step of yours, and write it down. Ask somebody who has to write it down.*
- **What she still will not do:** name the fourth-floor or the fifth-floor person, go up, report the round, read a sheet, or write a thing down that is hers. She read a card recording a Thursday as not worked and a four-page return document aloud, and **she read the woman's three lines from the frontage walk aloud and the woman had asked not to be read aloud, and Rue read them.**


### Rue — after Chapter 70, the end of Volume 02 Batch 0001

- **What she did in this batch, and each of it is now a rule somebody else has to keep.** She **refused a lamp in the first yard of the case** — *you will not find it under a map, you will find it on a clipboard* — and then, two hours later, in the same yard, changed her own answer in public: *You will not find it under a map. You will not find it on a clipboard. You will find it on a clipboard, and you will not find it under a map, and I have not got the second half of that right and I am not going to pretend I have.* **She kept the unaccounted-for sheet** — a sheet of accountancy paper, two columns, NAME and LAST SEEN, the right-hand column about an inch and a half deep and **empty for the whole of the week, because there is nowhere to write** — and she gave the thirty-one names out once in the rain and let nobody write them down. She **walked a mechanic to the depot and to a market at a quarter to ten before he had done his own eleven and did not put it on a sheet and told Milo Rook about it and then would not talk about it again.** She **read the turn sheet's new column into being**: SAID BY, added in her hand, *because the printed rule of the third of December is on the reach and the length is not the reach, and the day somebody tells me the book is on every piece of track in this city I will take the column out, and I would like that written down with a date.* And she **walked him home without letting him look at the card, on purpose, at his request and on one condition**, and said the sentence the whole volume turns on: *You can get home off that card. And you cannot get home off yourself. And the difference between those is a woman of thirty-one in biro and a woman with a round and a foreman with a grease pencil, and that is the whole of it, and it is not going to get better, and I am not going to be the one who tells you it might.*
- **The four sentences in Maggie Nye's kitchen, standing up by the sink, with nobody from the district there but her** — and the thing that followed them, which is the only thing in this batch that is anybody's own record of a person: **she gave a woman of sixty-six a sheet of accountancy paper and did not have it back, and does not know where it is and is not going to know.**
- **What she said about the rest of them, and it is uncomfortable and correct:** *Rue says they are wrong and she will not defend them and she is not going to stop them, and she is right and I would not take it back if she had not told me* — nine other people with about one name each on paper in about eight kitchens.
- **What she refused, again, in this batch:** to read a manifest's names out in a yard; to put a name on a sheet with a place beside it; to let a stranger's document be a district's; to be thanked; to cross her own name onto the length; to be on the sheet about the second walk; to be the one who wrote his route; and to soften the Lark Street business, which she refused **in one word, first, before anybody else could**, and which is unresolved and is on the page unresolved.

## The woman on the fourth floor

- **Not named on the page, and this remains structural.** Rue has carried her name on the accountancy-paper rota since the twenty-fourth of last month and has not said it in a yard and is not going to.
- **What she did, and it is the batch's largest single choice:** **she came down on the Sunday morning of the seventh at about eight, before anybody was on the path, on her own, with her coat on and a bag, and nobody had gone up for her and nobody had sent for her, and she walked the frontage once, in daylight, front to back, ninety minutes, at about two miles an hour, with about thirty people behind her at eleven feet.** She did not go in. **She chose a memorial route rather than a home, and she did not go in, and she has not gone up since.** Her three lines, in her own hand, which she would not read out: *I walked it. Once, in daylight, front to back, and I did not go in and I am not going in. / I am not coming down again for anybody. Not for the sheet, not for the door, not for a turn, not for a hearing, and not for a thing that is in a good coat. I have said that four times now and this is the fourth and I am not saying it a fifth. / The road is not mine and I have not claimed it and nobody is to put my name on it. If it is on anything that is my name on it, take it off in front of me.*
- **Nobody thanked her. Milo Rook was about four hundred yards away and has written down twice that he was about four hundred yards away. She is not healed, she is not on anything, and the deliberate space at the foot of the turn sheet went through the seventh and the eighth empty and about four people have checked it.**
- **She is not Ada Fenn and must never be counted with her.** Ada is on the third floor and has said in a yard that she has been up those stairs at six o'clock every evening since the closure; **this woman is on the fourth floor, which is Milo's own floor, and she gave her own age once, in her own mouth, on the seventh: *because I am sixty-eight*.** No other chapter gives her an age and no state file may give her one.


### The woman on the fourth floor — Volume 02

- **She does not appear in Chapters 61–70 and her name is not written once in them, and this is structural and not an accident.** She came down once in Volume 01, on the seventh of December, and said four times, in a yard, that she is not coming down again — *not for the sheet, not for the door, not for a turn, not for a hearing, and not for a thing that is in a good coat* — **and nobody in Chapters 61–70 goes up to her, asks for her, or puts her on anything.** The chalk arrow with no date on it is still on the wall of the second-floor landing, and there is now a second one on the same wall from the same boy on the Friday night of the twenty-sixth, **and there are two undated marks on that wall and nobody has ever asked who put either of them there and nobody is going to.**
- **She is not Ada Fenn and the two must never be folded together.** Ada Fenn is on the **third** floor of Cedar Court; this woman is on the **fourth**, which is Milo's own floor. Ada Fenn does not appear in Chapters 61–70 and her name is not written once in them; she is referred to three times, all of them in the established idiom and never by name, as **a woman of sixty-eight**, once in a working man's mouth in Chapter 62 and once in Mara Quill's district inventory in Chapter 65 and once inside Milo's own Field Book in Chapter 66. **No state file may give either of them an age that is not in a chapter, and no later batch may add a fourth reference without saying that it is adding one.**

## Ada Fenn

- **Where she is:** Cedar Court, third floor. **She does not appear in Chapters 51–60 and her name is not written once in them. She is not named, thanked, replaced, argued back, or put on anything, and nobody goes up three flights of stairs about her.**
- **She is referred to in five places across four chapters and in seven age tags, and never by name:** as **a woman of sixty-eight** who was not in the district on the sixth of December and did not say anything on the sixth of December, and then *she is sixty-eight and she has a fever coming*, both in Chapter 54's account of the four hundred answers; in an unknown man's mouth in Chapter 57, in the yard — *she is sixty-eight and she has a fever*; and twice in Lena Marr's mouth in the next paragraph, in the sentence *the district's answer on the eighth of December is a woman of sixty-eight*; and once each as **a name of sixty-eight** inside Milo's own Field Book, in Chapters 59 and 60. **Two of the seven are his own sentences, which is on the page and is not a reason to remove them.** **There is one more sixty-eight tag in Chapter 57 and it is not her: the woman on the fourth floor gives her own age in her own mouth on the seventh, and the two must never be folded together — she is on the fourth floor and Ada Fenn is on the third.** That is the whole of Ada's presence in the batch.
- **Chapter 57 stages the moment she was nearly reached for and was not.** A man said *then go and ask the old woman on the third floor* in a yard of about twenty-nine, and Rue said **no** first, before anybody else could, and Lena Marr gave the real reason in about sixty words: *She is not ours. If we go up and ask her, then the district's answer on the eighth of December is a woman of sixty-eight, and every one of us in this yard goes back to being a thing that happens next to a woman of sixty-eight… and the report will be true, and it will be the end of it, because a report that says a person remained is a report that says a place is kept by one person, and one person is not a district and one person is what this whole street was closed over. And I have not been up those stairs either, and I live in that building, and that is not a thing I am proud of and I am not going to pretend it is.* **Nobody went up. It is on the page that nobody went up.**
- **Unchanged and binding:** she has refused to interpret the third bell thirteen times and will not be asked a fourteenth; she is not on the rota and will not be, including by way of signing herself off it; her own name for what she is remains *a woman with a habit, and those are not the same.*
- **The district did the crossing with the three sources it built itself.** **Her withdrawal is load-bearing and it is not a problem, and Volume 1 does not solve it, thank her, or replace her.**


### Ada Fenn — Volume 02 Batch 0001

- **She does not appear in Chapters 61–70 and her name is not written once in them.** She is referred to in **three places across three chapters**, never by name and always as **a woman of sixty-eight**: Chapter 62 in Dilley Farr's mouth, as one of the things the district's method has cost; Chapter 65 in Mara Quill's flat inventory, *there is a woman of sixty-eight on nothing*; and Chapter 66 inside Milo's own Field Book, where he uses it to say why he is not going to find out what happens to a habit by stopping one. **Two of the three are inside an inventory or a working man's sentence and one is his own; the prohibition is a standing note and is not a problem to be solved, and it has not been solved, and nobody went up three flights of stairs about her in any chapter of this batch.**
- **Unchanged and binding:** she has refused to interpret the third bell thirteen times and will not be asked a fourteenth; she is not on the rota and will not be; her own name for what she is remains *a woman with a habit, and those are not the same.* **The district found a way to keep a length without her, and it was a gang of eleven men and a market of eleven habits and a conductor of thirty-eight, and nobody asked her whether that was all right.**

## Owen Fitch

- **Where he is:** Cedar Court, fourth floor, the stairwell, and the foot of the outside stairs in the rain.
- **The refusal, and it is a real refusal and it is not a wound that is healed.** On Saturday the twenty-second of November he refused the job at the foot of the outside stairs, to four people, in the rain. **He will not be the turn, hold the sheet, train anybody, be in charge of a table, sign anything, keep a list, or be the reason a thing works.** *If the return happens and it wants a turn, it goes without one, and a return without a turn is a return with a hole in it, and the hole is where I am, and it will look like a mistake and it will be deliberate, and I will not be asked about it afterwards.* **He also disclosed, for the first time in six years, that he has not been on that street since the night of the closure and did not go back when the order came.**
- **The one thing he offered, and the reason it was not a role:** on the sixth of December, in daylight, once, at the ground-floor door, he stood there for about four minutes, **said nothing, did not go in, was not a turn, and was not counted**, and he was gone before the round was in. *I have not been on that street since the night of the closure and I did not go back when the order, and I have never once said so out loud and I am saying it now because a piece of paper I did not write has got my name and my floor in it and somebody has to be somewhere near the middle of this, and it is not going to be me.*
- **His fourth position, which nobody knew was there, given in a yard on the nineteenth of November:** **nobody should go in, and the reason should be written down in the district's own book** — *a paper that says a person went in can be used against them and a paper that says a person did not go in can be used for them, and there is one of those and there is not one of the other, and every one of us has spent six weeks only ever writing down the second kind.*
- **Nobody thanked him, nobody healed him, nobody argued with him, and no character in this batch explains his position to him.** The woman of sixty said in a yard that he had not been asked, and asked for that to be written down, and nobody wrote it down.
- **The record, for the sixth time:** one household has declined to be entered; his initials are on the instrument of the fifth of Sazur; a refusal line has been read into the record against them at a hearing; his own capitals and three words are on the third-floor line of the accountancy-paper rota; his third-floor check is unchanged, once a night, going up, unwritten, revocable at will; and **his name is still on five copies of a sheet of paper with a contractor's head on it, in the space that was left empty on purpose, and it has not been taken off anything in this batch and no clerk took it off for him.**


### Owen Fitch — Volume 02 Batch 0001

- **He does not appear in Chapters 61–70 and his name is not written once in them, and nobody asks about him and nobody explains his position to him.** The one mention is a line in Mara Quill's flat inventory of the district on the nineteenth of December: *a man on the fourth floor of a building with no number who has not been on that street since the night of the closure and who has not been asked, and whose name and whose third floor are still on five copies of a sheet of paper with a contractor's head on it and have not been taken off any of them.* **Five copies, one name, and a floor on the paper that is not his: he is on the fourth, the third floor is the space somebody else filled in, and the inventory now says the floor is the paper's and does not ratify the forgery. Nothing in this batch changes any of it, and no clerk took his name off anything.**
- **Unchanged:** the refusal of the turn and of every other part; the one thing he offered, four minutes at a ground-floor door, said nothing, not a turn, not counted; the fourth position, that nobody should go in and the reason should get written down; and the record nobody explained to him.

## Lena Marr

- **Where she is:** Cedar Court, the Board school, the Hall of Wards, and four rooms behind a boatyard on the Ferry Road side.
- **The decision that is the batch's bill.** On the fifteenth of December, at about ten to twelve, in a hall of about thirty people, **she stood up and signed as the named head of a temporary accommodation household, which puts the per-head additional schedule for every person in the block onto her instead of onto the district rate — two and four a head a week, in advance, for as long as that building is a building, with no term.** She wrote both her questions down that morning and showed them to a woman of sixty-three on a step, and the woman read them and is not in the room and said she would come on Wednesday if either was wrong. **The same signature in the same minute opens the school intake for the seven**, on a Continuity referral, because a referral asks for a place and a place needs a person. **The two children lost in October are still two, and she said so twice in the room, unasked.** Nobody thanked her.
- **What she refused:** a fifth Thursday in any form; to be the one who chooses what the district gets; to hand a schedule of rooms to a rates authority; **to be the centre of the supervised return**, which she wrote a notice for on the nineteenth of November and handed to Rue inside four minutes, saying *if I run it then in three weeks there is one woman with a plastic wallet and everybody knows where to find her, and that is how this district got closed once*; and to apologise to a widow for something a department did.
- **Her three-part answer, still standing:** no fifth Thursday, no going to a room before the event, and the count. And the reason for the second: *if we go in now and say the word, then for the rest of this case the district is the people who said the word, and the department will find the classification in the file in about ninety seconds… after that the only two sentences anybody hears are ours and his.*
- **And the sentence about the woman on the third floor, in a yard, which is the reason nobody went up** — see Ada Fenn above. **And Lena's own admission: *And I have not been up those stairs either, and I live in that building, and that is not a thing I am proud of and I am not going to pretend it is.***
- **The widow:** visited on the Sunday, in a taxi, at nine pounds of her own money, with Doreen Sankey on the step. She read the six words off the disposal return and **did not make her ask one question**, and did not make her ask where to go, and did not say sorry. **The woman is fifty-four, mends other people's clothes, is alive, and is not named on the page and was not named in this batch.**


### Lena Marr — Volume 02 Batch 0001

- **Where she is:** the yard, the Board school, four rooms behind a boatyard, and a Thermos. **She has not stopped paying the levy** — two and four a head a week, in advance, no term, for two hundred and fifty-two people in a building with no number — and two children lost in October are still lost, and nothing in this batch touches either of those facts.
- **What she did in this batch, and it is a working day and not a speech.** On the Monday morning during the ninth crossing she had **about nine people at the trestle table and about forty more since the Friday**, and she gave them tea out of a Thermos and told them, at about nine o'clock, in about eleven words, what had happened at the top of Cardew Street on Saturday, **as a fact about a length of track and a gang and not as a thing that is going to be a miracle.** And to a man who came from Ferry Road on a bicycle with a coat open and said *I have a daughter on that run*: *You are on the list from Friday and you are on it because you came in on Friday and not because of your daughter, and I would like you to know that I have put them in that order myself so that I cannot be talked into putting them the other way.*
- **What she still refuses:** to be the one who chooses what the district gets; to apologise to anybody for a department; to put a name on a district sheet; and to be thanked.

## Tomas Rusk

- **Where he is:** his front room, sixty-one, a bad knee, four years of a district works pension, and a taxi from the low end at three and six a trip.
- **His condition, stated once on the twentieth of November and not asked about twice.** It is not for him and it is not for his oath, and he will not swear. **The road is not the lane.** A true sentence about a place that does not exist can be used against a man who did not know it did not exist, and the frontage has a name in the district's own drainage book and in the works' own schedule and has been a maintained frontage since before he was at the pump house: **Bridge Reach.** He wanted the record put in before the sixth. **What it cost him is his name** — a page with his name at the foot of it, in the reopened order file, as a mechanic of sixty-one who sat on a dead man's next-of-kin line for six years and has asked for a road. *Once a road is in the district's book with a name and a maintenance schedule and a man attached to it, there is a road, and there is no version of this that ends with me being a man in a kitchen saying a true thing to about nine people.*
- **And it worked.** On the Thursday morning of the ninth the district drainage section came out to the low end and unstruck the 1904 entry and wrote the date and *maintained frontage, 140 ft, reverting on the suspension of CO/6/BELL*, and said four words to Rue: **Requested by a mechanic.** The works maintenance schedule carries the same reach with a gang and a fortnightly inspection.
- **The instrument, again and again, from a telephone at seven in the morning with a clock on the table:** on the second of that June the crew came off the frontage to the pump house, and that is the whole of it, and **from the moment the crew came off, that length of ground was not a road, it was a gap, and a gap is not a road and it is not a hazard and it is not a street, it is a place where a road used to be, and the difference between those is a crew.** And then, on the sixth of December, in a trench, with a crew on the reach at ten to two: *that is exactly the condition I have been describing to you since June six years ago, and paragraph six says the crew change is sounded from the gantry and the paragraph above the one that was taken out says the frontage is at all times a service access and not a public route.* He said it once, refused to say it again, and hung up. **He still will not swear. Nobody argued him out of it and nobody tried.**
- **What he asked for and did not get:** to be at a door. He is on the round's sheet with **I cannot get there** in his own hand, and he is walked to the door by whoever is going.


### Tomas Rusk — Volume 02 Batch 0001

- **He is sixty-one and he still will not swear, and nothing in this batch argues him out of it and nothing in this batch asks him to.** He has one page with his name at the foot of it and no oath on it and a drainage record and a works maintenance schedule and a name on a tram diversion panel, and all of it is his and none of it is a street.
- **What he did, and it is the hinge of the whole batch's method, and he said it standing at the top of a market that closed nineteen years ago.** *A road is not a line. That is all of it. A road is a place and a crew keeps a road. A gap is a place where a road stopped being a place and a crew does not keep a gap. **And that length out there is not a road and it is not a gap and that is why it has got a car on it. It is the third thing and nobody has a word for the third thing because nobody has ever needed one.*** And then, on the Sunday night, to about nine people in a yard, refusing to sit down: ***a length has got a crew on a sheet and a wall at the end of it… The writing down is the crew. The crew is the writing down. Take the writing down away on the tenth of October and you have not removed a road. You have removed the crew's reason for being there on Tuesday morning, and the gang still came on Tuesday, and the gang's reason is a piece of paper, and nobody has ever taken a piece of paper away from a gang in this city, and that is the only reason your car is still going round.***
- **He said the word at three of the four places on the length** — the down loop, the wall, and the water stairs end — and did not say it at the north end, which is a box and not a turn and which Jo Lask says so.
- **His condition, asked for in advance and honoured:** *I am not going to be asked afterwards why I was there and I am not going to be thanked, and I want both of those written down before anybody asks me for either of them.* Nobody asked. Nobody thanked him. It is written down.
- **He cannot get to a door.** He was walked to the depot on the Thursday morning and to Saltmarket Row on the Saturday morning by Doreen Sankey, both times before that man had done his own eleven, and neither time on a sheet, and the first time he asked for it to be entered in Hettie Brann's list as one true small thing, and the second time he said he was not going to talk about it again.

## Doreen Sankey

- **Who he is:** forty-one, two rooms behind a boatyard on the Ferry Road side, nine years, paid nobody anything, on no rate book, no index, no tenancy and no schedule, and never has been. One of the thirty-three rooms on no list.
- **What he did in this batch, and it is the most reliable labour anybody did in it:** **walked Milo Rook in and out of the reach on the seventh**, in about eleven words of terms, *You have a man on a platform and I have no place to be, and you want somebody who is not your chalk, and I am the only one in this district who can turn round and come back up this and I do it eleven times a day and I will do it in front of four hundred people and then nobody can say a man from a boatyard stood in a hole.* **Ran the way in and out for the passage on the seventh and the eighth.** **Took the check at a door on the twenty-eighth of November, the first with no named person, and told Rue the terms in his own hand.** **Stood at the foot of the made reach at eleven feet in front of a woman for ninety minutes** so that nobody touched her arm. **Sat on a step in the rain on Sunday the fourteenth and would not come past the first room in nine years**, and said so, because he has not been asked and he is not going.
- **What he says, and it is the argument for the whole batch:** the count has to be a person saying a number to a person, and a photograph of him is a document and a document can be produced, *and I would rather the document be one I chose and stood in deliberately and where there is a foreman's name on it as well as mine.*
- **What he refused, in the last chapter, and it is a refusal with a reason:** to write a man's route home. *I have been asked for that route in about four houses this month and I have said no in all four, and one of them was a man with a good coat and one of them was a man with a worse coat, and if I walk it once to write it down then in a month somebody will be able to say the boatyard man keeps the surveyor's way home, and the second it is written down it is a job… Ask a person who has to be there. Not a person who is passing.*
- **What he still wants:** not a street. **Connected.** A way to walk from his door to the tram steps in the rain that about forty people in this building have never heard of, and he now has one, on a works frontage, and he has said that a works frontage is the only kind of street anybody in this district is ever going to get and that he is not complaining.


### Doreen Sankey — Volume 02 Batch 0001

- **Still forty-one, still two rooms behind a boatyard on the Ferry Road side, still on no rate book, no index, no tenancy and no schedule, and never has been, and still would rather be hard to find.** He is not on the new case either: the back length is not his and the tram is not his and he is not the reason a car came back.
- **What he did, and it is the same labour as always and it is the reason the ninth crossing was legal.** He **walked Milo Rook in and out of the back length on the twenty-second of December**, on the same terms as in December: he can stop it, and he is told now that if he says the word it stops and nobody is to argue with him on the way back, including Milo Rook. He **walked a mechanic to the depot on the Thursday and to Saltmarket Row on the Saturday**, both times at a quarter to ten, before he had done his own eleven, and did not put it on a sheet and did not tell anybody, and told Milo Rook about the first one and then would not talk about it again. And he said the sentence about the market that Milo wrote on his card and did not strike out: **it is not a mystery, and if you go into that shed at Cardew Docks and ask the manager he will tell you the same thing I am telling you, and he will tell you it as a nuisance — they come back; the row is not a thing that happened, it is a habit that has got two doors.**
- **What he still refuses:** to be on a sheet about the second walk; to be the reason a document is made; to be thanked; and to be legible.

## The woman of sixty from the second landing of Cedar Court

- **Not named, by the same rule and for the same reason.** Rue did not tell Milo Rook her name after the count of the seventeenth and he did not ask.
- **What she holds: a street.** And what she got: **a contested community and memorial name** on a register that is not the street index, capable of being produced against any person who uses it, and she has read that sentence four times and said, in a hall, *I asked for a street for nine years and I have been given a thing that can be produced against me, and I would like the register to know that I know that, and I am going to use it anyway, and I am not going to be one of the women who waits for the perfect thing.* **And she asked for it to be noted on the entry, and it is noted.**
- **What she did:** took the hours of the passage and wrote the word **clear** on Rue's round in her own handwriting; **marked the tenth of Milo's ten lost routes, at Rue's insistence and his not asking, and did not go on a list to do it**; made the department read the contested entry out loud in a hall; and said the sentence that made the room say *good* and go home: *Then there is no way in and I would like somebody to say so out loud, because I have lived on a landing for nine years and I have been told there is no way in by five different people in five different coats and I have never once been told it by anybody who had to.*
- **What she will not do:** be the door-check. She said it and she is right and nobody argued with her. She is not on the rota and did not go on the round's floor sheet.
- **She is one of the five who went out through the ground-floor door to Cedar Court.**

## The woman at the door of the Board school

- **Who she is:** she keeps a list, the list closes at noon, there are **nine children on it, two of them lost for good, and seven of them come back in the spring because the intake opened** — on a Continuity referral, on the fifteenth of December, **in Lena Marr's own hand, in the same minute and with the same signature that puts the levy on her**, naming her as the responsible person for a temporary accommodation household. Advocate Quill's own hand is on the head count, not on the referral.
- **What she said and did not say:** she has not been thanked and did not ask. **She has not been the door-check, still is not the door-check, and is right.** What she read two names off her list to a letting agent in November is still on the page and still hers in that order, and it has not been softened.
- **What is now on the page because of her:** a copy of the turn sheet came into her letter box on the fifteenth of November addressed to *the person keeping the street*, and there is another in a fourth-floor flat in Wragg Street, and **she has not given either name and is not going to.**

## Hettie Brann

- **Who she is:** the water clinic counter, nineteen years on it, formerly a laundry hand at Ada Fenn's laundry. She holds the turn sheet on alternate weeks with Mara Quill and reads neither.
- **What she did, and it was the last thing the seventh crossing needed:** **she said no three times and then said a different yes**, and held the count on the seventh of December from the path head with a stopwatch on the back of her hand, and would not read the sheet, would not come in, would not be asked about anybody who came out, and told the yard why: **the day this district needs a count and there is nobody in it to keep one, that is the day it stops being a district, and I have been on that counter nineteen years and I have watched it stop twice and I am not watching it stop a third time while I am standing up.** She took the tally in her apron pocket before anybody asked her to, and her tally now has **eight lines** on it and is the record of his crossings.


### Hettie Brann — Volume 02 Batch 0001

- **Where she is:** the water clinic counter, nineteen years on it, **whose tally it is and not whose pocket it is** — a clinic counter form in her own hand on the back of a form — **and it is in Dr Sen's apron pocket, which is where Chapter 68 of this batch put it on the twenty-ninth of December, and it now has nine lines on it and is the record of his crossings.** **She made him read the ninth crossing out for the seventh time and made him say the number out loud.**
- **What she did, and it is the reason the district behaves the way it does.** She stopped the thirty-one names being written down in a yard: *A list of names on a piece of paper in this district is a piece of paper about people… The list of the people who have not come home is in a public building, in a drawer, in a depot office, where anybody can ask to see it and where a department keeps it and cannot lose it. **It belongs in the depot. It does not belong in a yard in the rain.*** And she said the sentence the batch ends on, in a yard on the Tuesday morning, with no tally and no form: **a document is the only thing in this city that bites** — *I have watched this city eat four people in six years over a document, and it was not the four people who did it, it was a piece of paper in a drawer… there is not one now. The car came back and the people came back and the one thing that could have been said about the ninth of December is on a form about a drain in a rack in an office, and in about four years that rack will be cleared, because racks are cleared, and when it is cleared the thing will not have happened. **And it will be done again. Not by a villain. By a box. And the next one will not be a tram. The next one will be something with children in it. And I would like somebody in this yard to write that down who is not me, and nobody has written it down, and I am not going to be the woman who has it on a form.***
- **What she has not been asked to be and will not be:** the door-check, the reader, or the woman who holds a mark overnight.

## Nell Crale

- **Who she is:** sixty-three, Bridge Street, four rooms over a shop with a cellar under it that is the only cold store within four streets, twenty-two years on a milk-and-ice round.
- **What she did in this batch:** **held the seat on the wall on the north path from nine until it stopped, with a whistle, from Saturday the sixth until it was not needed, and is not on Orrin Sable's payroll and does not want to be.** **Read both questions Lena Marr wrote down that morning on the step, and said she would come on Wednesday if either was wrong.** Lost a hundredweight of ice and four inches of water in a cellar in four hours on the sixth and got the ice out alone and told nobody until the Monday. Made a man look at a line about the reach on the seventh of December, *and I am not in a yard and I am not the face of anything and I have a cellar to run.*
- **What she still refuses:** to talk about a man who has not asked her, and to cross the reach, and to be a symbol. **Her position, in a yard of about twenty-nine people, and nobody argued with her:** *never.*


### Nell Crale — Volume 02 Batch 0001

- **She is sixty-three, she has four rooms over a shop on Bridge Street with the only cold store within four streets, and she appears once in Chapters 61–70.** On the Monday morning of the ninth crossing she **sat on the wall on the north path from nine until about eleven with nothing in her hands**, next to a man with a spade across his knees, and **the two of them did not speak to each other at any point in two hours**, and about four people noticed and about two of them have said since that it was the most sensible two hours anybody has spent in a yard since the seventh of December. **She was not asked to do anything, she was not made a symbol, and she is still not.**

## Verrick Assal

- **Where he is:** the works compound, mending chain, fifty-eight, on a wage, with a spade he brings to the reach and has still never used for anything.
- **What he did:** **sat on the wall on the north path from nine until it stopped with the spade across his knees and counted heads going out, and gave the number to nobody and has not been asked for it.** He was the one who said *ask it again* when Rue needed somebody, twice, and it is still the only thing he has got and he has had it since the sixth of Sazur.
- **His record still says: asked three times, said nothing three times, not a witness,** and **the fourth position in the yard — nobody goes in, and the reason gets written down — is his, and he gave it in a yard and was not thanked for it.**


### Verrick Assal — Volume 02 Batch 0001

- **He is fifty-eight, on a wage, at the works compound, and he appears once in Chapters 61–70.** On the Monday morning of the ninth crossing he **sat on the same wall on the north path from nine until about eleven with a spade across his knees, next to Nell Crale, and said nothing to her and nothing to anybody, and gave no count to nobody.** **His record still says asked three times, said nothing three times, not a witness, and he has still never been asked about a bell since the sixth of Sazur, and nobody asked him on this either.**

## Dr Iona Sen

- **Where she is:** the water clinic, the dispensary counter, and the edge of an awning in the rain.
- **What she did in this batch:** **gave him the number that is the batch's whole argument, in a dispensary with four people in the queue: there are eight remembered routes and there is a page, and they are not one thing, and the difference is the only interesting thing about either of them** — and told him on the twenty-fifth of November that somebody else is going to have to write the line, from their own feet, in their own hand, on his page, so that he cannot say afterwards that nobody told him it was coming. **Treated eleven people hurt on the sixth of December and four through the room by half past two, and nine on the eighth, and said the thing that is the point of it: a person going into a place that is not safe is not an injury, it is a decision, and I will not have that turned into an argument about a seam.** **Said the sentence that is the batch's whole warning, in a yard of about nine on the Monday the eighth: it is not fixed, it is quiet, and a fixed thing is one you stop watching, and the day somebody in this district stops watching it is the day it starts again, and it will not start with a noise, it will start with a schedule.** **Made him read the eighth crossing out loud and made him say the number out loud, because in a year he will have to say it in a room.** And she corrected her own October finding out loud in a yard: the condition is not *do not be alone with a document about a person*, it never helped him once and it is not going to; **the condition is that he gets walked to a door and walked out of a door, every time, by a person who is not you, and says so in advance, and if they do not come you do not go** — and that it took a line going out of a book to make a man ask for it, and that that is not a magic cost and not a doctor's opinion, it is just what happened.
- **Unchanged:** she has said nothing about the Atlas, nothing about her own past, and nothing about the man who taught Milo's hand. The fifth finding is not cured and is not signed and cannot be signed, and she said on the twelfth of November that writing it down had made it worse and she would do it again.


### Dr Iona Sen — Volume 02 Batch 0001

- **Where she is:** the water clinic, and she has said nothing about the Atlas, nothing about her own past, and nothing about the man who taught Milo's hand. The fifth finding is not cured and is not signed and cannot be signed.
- **What she did in this batch.** **She made him read the ninth crossing out for the seventh time and made him say the number out loud, and wrote the ninth line on the tally, and the tally has nine lines on it and is the record.** She said the useful new thing, and it is about the three weeks he did not ask anybody to fill in his route home: **I want you to notice that you did it quietly and that a thing you do quietly is a thing that is going to get done.** She also told a yard of about nine people on the Monday that she is not the door-check and never was, and that she wants a number of injuries in a yard to mean what it means. She treated the district on the Monday, and Milo Rook was not in her queue, and **she says that if he had come through her door on the Monday morning she would have sent him home.**

## Orrin Sable

- **Where he is:** suspended from duty in this district from ten o'clock on Friday the twelfth of December, required to give account, in a room in Crown Heights with a caution on him and a solicitor he chose himself. **This is not a finding and does not imply one. It is made because an officer may not investigate his own order.** He is not the final antagonist and he is not softened.
- **What he did, and each of it was right:** **announced the test date and the timetable three weeks early and out loud, and said he was doing it early so that nobody could say afterwards that he did it quietly** — the same move as the plate and the same reason, and the second time in eleven weeks he has been right about something with nothing to do with engineering. **Announced the culvert's true state** — throttled to eighteen inches of sill since the sixth of Sazur, never sealed — and told the district that a man had been making his case easier. **Announced the four-day window, the soft lens stopping on the first, and the trench under the only made path.** **Refused to answer a crossing, changed his mind in public inside four minutes, and said the sentence that stopped it: close the crossing and the reach becomes a road, and a road is kept by somebody, and a road with forty people on it at ten to two is a crew, and I would be putting the district's own sentence into the reach against the order of the district, and I have spent six years watching a man do that to about four hundred people and I am not going to be the second one.** Put a woman of sixty-three on a wall who is not on his payroll. **Said in a yard, at about ten on a Sunday morning, that on the sixth of December the district goes into a room and he is going to be the man in that room who does not argue — and that on Saturday he had said the opposite about a fence, and that he would like both recorded.**
- **His three sentences of account, out of nine, on the evening of the twelfth, to about nine people in a yard that had emptied:** **the safety need was real and is still real and the wall behind this district did not fail on the sixth of December and that is the only thing he has ever wanted to be true**; **the concealment was not required by the safety problem, he has known that since the first week, he did it with a document and not with a lie, which is worse, and he put the true sentence in the box that only takes it**; and **there is a file in the district he has never been able to get at and has stopped trying for, and he is telling nine people about it in a yard because there is a dead man in it and the name is on a ledger with the word pending against it — and he is not going to say the rest of it in December, and he wanted the two words *not* and *not* noticed, and he was aware of both.** Nobody asked him. **His later testimony will expose how the order was made and will not absolve him, and he is not absolved anywhere in this volume.**


### Orrin Sable — Volume 02 Batch 0001

- **He does not appear in Chapters 61–70 and is not named in them.** He is suspended from duty in this district from ten o'clock on Friday the twelfth of December, required to give account, in a room in Crown Heights with a caution on him and a solicitor he chose himself. **The investigation is still open, the hearing is still not set, and the three sentences he gave in a portacabin on the evening of the twelfth have not been added to**, and nobody in this batch asks him anything and nobody in this batch is in a position to.

## Cass Ardery

- **Who he is:** Survey Enforcement field officer, the man who wrote the classification of the eleventh of the current year — *minor seam, unstable, not a public route; witness none maintained; no rota, schedule, roster or return arrangement is maintained at the site by the district, by the works, or by any party having authority in this matter; persons affected: nil* — which was true on the eleventh and is not true now.
- **What he did:** came to the reach on the seventh of December at a quarter past six in the morning, did not read either instruction on it, asked three questions and wanted six people to answer them and not the surveyor, **crossed out his own paragraph in ink across his own classification, dated it and initialled it, and wrote the word *pinned* into the box that says *nature of hazard*.** He said the department's honest position: *if it is pinned while the order stands, then the only way to unpin it is the order, and there is not one single person in this district who is going to unpin that reach by being right in a yard.* **He wrote the triad accounting into a report, on the back of a works truck, and refused the word *triad*, because he has read it twice in the file and cannot define it and *a term in a report that nobody in the report can define is a term that gets removed by the next man*.** The classification is withdrawn and the department has noted in writing that it was made correctly on the day it was made.
- **He has asked in writing to be second-in-command on the investigation of a thing he classified, and it was granted,** and he has said that a department which cannot have the man who made the mistake in the room is a department that has already decided. He is not a friend and is not an ally and is not a final antagonist.


### Cass Ardery — Volume 02 Batch 0001

- **Where he is:** a corridor in Crown Heights, about forty-four, second-in-command on the investigation of a thing he classified, and **still not the reporting officer and not going to be.**
- **What he said on the twenty-eighth of December, in about ninety seconds, in a corridor with about nine people in it.** The order has been suspended **sixteen days**; the hearing is a hearing as of right and it is not set and will not be set in January, **because a hearing as of right that nobody has set is a thing a department can leave on a shelf.** And his own reason for not being the reporting officer, which is not a rule: **I wrote the classification of the eleventh. It was correct on the day it was made and I withdrew it in my own hand and I would write it again. There is no rule in this department that says a man who made a mistake cannot report on it. There is not one rule that says he can. That silence is the reason, and I would like the silence minuted.**
- **And the thing nobody has said in nine days, in his words: *A tram service amendment is not a continuity order. It was made by a different body, under the same standing instruction, and it removed six hundred yards of a public route from this city's map, and there is no procedure in this Registry that puts the two in a room, and there is not one in anybody else's Registry either, and I have looked for one for nine days.*** He noticed a document in Advocate Quill's bag on about the sixth of December, **did not ask, and asked for that to be minuted**, and she told him in front of nine people that if he had asked she would have taken it out.

## Danner, Rennick, the works clerk, and the works

- **Danner** — a foreman on a district flood levy, thirty years in. **Back on full rate for five days from Monday the first of December, which resets his review for the second time in eight weeks**, and he has said out loud that in five weeks there will be a file in which a foreman of the district flood stage has been reinstated twice for certification work and has never once been certified against anything that was not a certificate. **He certified the last reach on the fourth or the fifth**, inside the four-day window, and on the form, in the *alternative considered* box, in his own hand, he wrote: *I record that the certification is made in the fourteenth day of a standard deviation in the readings at the trench, and I will not be able to say in a room that I stood behind it, and I am certifying it because the level has to be certified by a man and the only man is me.* **The level is not back in Milo's file and may not be put back**, and the second paragraph of the joint finding of the twentieth of October is about him and not about the order. He was right about the lens: it stopped, and he said *there* once and did not say it twice.
- **Rennick** — the contractor's site manager.
