- **Where she is:** the Registry, a corridor, and a flat with a page in a drawer. **A two-year note, an appeal in a line not stayed, no preferred access, twenty-six names in a Crown Heights file that she put there on purpose, a minuted refusal to identify a document in her custody, a nine-minute letter of the sixteenth of January answered and refused in full on the seventeenth of February, and a second letter written on the eighteenth that she has not sent.**
- **What the custody register page did in this batch: nothing, again.** It is in a drawer with a coat in it. **She did not produce it, did not name it, did not confess to taking it, and was not asked. He has not asked, and has written down that he did not ask and that not asking was the correct procedure.** **Her knowledge of the token and the custody column is still unused and undeclared.** **He watched her look at a table for about four seconds on the seventh of January and wrote down something else, and in these ten chapters he has not looked at her for four seconds.**
- **What the letter did.** A company secretary refused her in three correct sentences and, in a fourth, confirmed that the return is filed under its own reference and bears the date of completion while expressly not treating that as a disclosure of its contents. **A reference and a date and no copy, and the sixth line of the fourteenth of January is not on the page and nobody in this batch tells him it is there.** She has written a second letter and has not sent it and has written down in advance that she is not going to and will read it back to herself on the day, **and has said in a corridor, with a clerk within about thirty feet, for the second time in a month and on purpose, that there is now a letter nobody has sent and the only way that stays safe is if about four other people know about it.**
- **What she said in a corridor on the twentieth of February, in about eleven words, and it is the relationship's whole vocabulary this batch:** *in about two months the first weather-driven event in this city that anybody allocated will be on a printed sheet with a time in a man's own hand, and there will be a great deal said in offices about what the Atlas can and cannot do in a season, and **none of it will be true**, and the truest thing about the whole winter happened because two women in this district put seven days into a counter and a satchel on their own signatures, and one of them is a woman of sixty-one at a clinic counter who is not going to be thanked and one of them is going to be leaned on, and I have written those two things down in a book of my own with a date on them and my name at the bottom, and that is all I have done this winter that anybody could call mine.*
- **Nothing romantic has happened and nothing is going to. No kiss, no declaration, no jealousy scene, no rescue. The Lark Street business was not raised in these ten chapters and she neither raised it nor softened it, and he did not either.** What exists between them is procedural: a document she refused to identify, a document he did not know she had, a fourth copy of a card that has been read and will not be described, and two statements made out loud in corridors that neither of them is going to act on.

### Pell Harrow

- **Where he is:** the round, out at eight and in at four, and in at seven in the seven days of the last week of January. Seventeen. **A courier and a Streetkeeper with no power of any kind, and not Milo's assistant.**
- **Tern: still not found, and nothing about the twenty-two days is asked or said in these ten chapters.** He went up the south on **Sunday the twenty-eighth of December** and came back on **Monday the nineteenth of January — twenty-two days, on his own money** — and has said in a yard, on the first of February and again on the second, that **nobody has asked him and nobody is going to**, and that a man is going to read his card one day and it is going to say nothing, and **that he decided that up the south and has not been able to think of anything better since.** The card is blank and has been since the nineteenth of January and will be blank on the second of March. **No chapter in this block rescued anybody and nothing in it found Tern; the list in the tin is not a finding and Ostrow confirms nothing.**
- **The man in the works jacket: a fourth sighting and no more.** At about ten past two on the afternoon of the nineteenth of February, about forty yards off the north end, in a doorway out of the rain, notebook out, **wrote once and did not come down the Row.** Two in December, one on Monday the fifth of January, this one. **Not there on Thursday the fifth of February, a walk day, and Pell was on the round and saw him not be there. Nobody followed him.** The card went face up under the brick, and **there are now three things under that brick: the run-number card with two lines on the back of it, the jacket card, and his own blank card.**
- **The standing refusal, said twice in a yard and not softened:** *four sightings is not a pattern, two cards on one brick is not a pattern, and if I put a line under any of them I will have made a column out of a doorway and a book on a shelf, and a column is a case, and I have watched two men in this district make one and I am not going to be the third.* And: **a card under a brick is a thing and not a job**, which he did not say and which a man of forty-one said, and which he has not contradicted.
- **What he still refuses:** to be the reason a document is made; to be on a sheet; to be thanked; to be legible; to follow a stranger; to be leaned on; **to have his twenty-two days asked about, in public or in private, ever; and to say anything about where he was.**

### Rue

- **Where she is:** the round, the trestle table, the porch, the top of the Row, and the yard.
- **What she did in this batch.** **She refused the seventh column in a yard of about nineteen in about nine words, and the whole of her refusal is that he took her week off a card on a wall that belongs to a nurse.** She then read out a form she did not approve of word for word without changing anything, which is what she does. **She made the second side of the sheet of who does what and on which day**, in about eleven minutes, in a fishmonger's capacity, and **told Milo Rook about it afterwards and that telling him was the last part, and he had no advice to give and was not asked.** She **named the cost of it out loud at the time** — *it is the same box with different words on it and one of the words is how many times* — and kept seven days in the round for the last week of January, unpaid, out at eight and in at seven, having already said the price out loud in January. **And on the evening of the twelfth she said one sentence to a fence and walked on, about the hundred and forty feet of frontage behind it: *it will not start with a noise, it will start with a schedule, and there is not one in this district that I know of and I have been looking for one since the twenty-second of December.***
- **What she refused, again:** to have her round put in a column with a day against it; to be thanked; to be leaned on; to be the one who corrects his sheet; **and to have the second side be a form that counts how many times.** She has put it there anyway and said so.

### Hettie Brann

- **Where she is:** the water clinic counter, nineteen years on it. **The tally is her paper and not her apron pocket: a clinic counter form in her own hand on the back of a form, and it is Dr Sen's in her apron pocket, and it has nine lines on it.**
- **What she did in this batch, and each of it is the relationship's whole vocabulary in an exchange with nobody.** **On the second of February, the first working day of the month, she compared the four copies for version and date and nothing else, found that three said version one and one said version two, asked the person whose hand it is, and was told that the only one of them who writes dates could not say which was right.** At about ten past five, in a corridor, **she asked a man of forty-one who is on no list at all one question and sent him to do a thing nobody sent him to do**, and when he came back and refused to describe one of the four copies, **she read her own rule at the foot of the envelope back and said out loud what it turned out to be for: *it is not for stopping two people disagreeing. It is for not making a person admit where they keep their papers.*** She then wrote the date on the outside of the envelope in her own hand in her own ink, put four copies back in four places, altered nothing, read nothing, copied nothing, **and said in front of about four people: *I have not fixed anything and I want that said… one fact is what a season is made of.*** **And she is not going to find out where the fourth copy is and says that in the same breath, and if she finds out she will not use it.**
- **Two other things she did that are on the page and are not to be improved on.** **She made a piece of paper about a man on purpose** — Doreen Sankey's name on a clinic form, face down under the counter, because there is no box on that machine for a man with no rateable unit — **and said at volume in front of nine people: *I have written your name on a piece of paper… I am not putting that paper into the machine, and it is going face down under this counter, and it is not a record of anything, and it is a piece of paper about a man, and I have made one on purpose, and I am sixty-one and I have a bad hip and I am not going to be the one who is asked about it in four years*** — and then refused to be thanked. **And she said the counter's method out loud to a room of about eleven who did not ask her: *I know which people in this district are not ill. And it is not my job to say so, and I do not say so, and I have never once said so, and that is the reason this counter has never had a fight on it.***
- **And she found the fourteenth of April.** On the twelfth of February, at about ten past two, off a card on a wall, in front of about nine people: **the season ends on the fourteenth of April and there is a hearing in the Hall of Wards on the fourteenth of April, and neither office has told the other that they share a day.** And: *I am the one who does [that] and that is the job, and I would like it minuted that a woman of sixty-one at a water clinic counter has worked out that… and I do not think either of them is going to.*
- **What she has not been asked to be and will not be:** the door-check, the reader, the person who holds a mark overnight, **or the person who knows what is written on four pieces of paper.** *If anybody asks me what is on it I will say it is a piece of paper.*

### Doreen Sankey

- **Where he is:** two rooms behind a boatyard on the Ferry Road side, forty-one, **on no rate book, no index, no tenancy and no schedule, and never has been, and still would rather be hard to find.** He does not appear by name earlier in these ten chapters and is referred to in the third person until the afternoon of the second of February, which is the first time he is in a room with the district in it.
- **What he did in this batch, and it is the recovery and it is not a power and it does not scale.** **On the Friday the thirtieth he came in at about ten past four in a wet coat and there is no box on the clinic machine for a man with no rateable unit**, and a woman of sixty-one wrote his name on a form face down under the counter and told a room of nine people about it. **On the second of February, at about ten past five in a corridor, he was asked one question — how do you know a paper has been read — and gave the answer that is the batch's only usable instrument: *you fold it back wrong. Paper's soft where it's been handled. It's not a mark and it's not a hand and it's not a date.*** **He went and did it himself, on the tide, in about an hour, with nobody sending him, and reported three of the four copies and refused to describe the fourth because nobody had asked him.** **He is not a courier and has no mark and cannot hold a route; the softness of paper is a thing he knows from handling other people's paper since he was nine.**
- **His two standing sentences in this batch.** The wrong shape, from the corridor on the second of February: **the card says how to turn and it does not say whether there is a way to turn** — and it is not to be put on the card, because *a man of fifty-four wrote on that card in January that it is not a route and he is not redrawing anybody's route.* And the rule, on the twentieth of February, about a card under a brick: *about four times a day somebody looks at a job and decides it is not theirs, and four times a day is a very different instrument from once.* **He has told this district the standing condition of its own method and he has done it for free and to no schedule.**

### Roy Pryce

- **Where he is:** the quay section, four rooms above a fitter's shop, fifty-four, thirty-one years, four lengths.
- **What he did in this batch, and it is three sentences and a cover.** He found a cast-iron cover up out of its frame with the ballast washed from under it, wrote **four words in the condition column and nothing in the reason column**, and said **nine words about it that have not been improved on**: *It filled. There was rain in the night… and I did not come down here, because it is a drain and it is not a road and nobody was on a car.* **Nobody was on a car. Nobody was hurt. Nothing collapsed.** His gang has walked past a cover that rocks twice a week since the second of February and he has made a private joke about it — *the cover rocks and the sheet does not, and the sheet is the truer of the two and always has been* — and **he has still not written one word about a person on a form, and his rule in grease pencil about not taking a line off is now the reason a surveyor's page is in a tin and the reason he does not write down what he saw at a scale on the nineteenth of February.** **In about four months a man in an office is still going to ask him what he saw.**
- **What he still refuses:** a reference on his schedule; a name in any box; being the officer who answers for persons; being redrawn by a surveyor; **and being thanked for *I am the record*.**

### The man of about fifty in the drainage section

- **Where he is:** a room in Crown Heights, **about fifty, fifty-one next month, nineteen years in it, no name on the page, and a later batch may not invent one.** He is not a character and he is not a face and he is not on anybody's ladder: **everything that goes wrong in the allocation half of this batch is attributable to a form, a machine, a list a hundred and forty years old, and a box he refused to fill in, and not to him.**
- **What he did in this batch.** **He transferred the four pages' own figures into the *events forecast* column of a printed allocation sheet by hand, with a pen, on the Monday, and did not like doing it and did it anyway.** **He entered the first event in the city in five words, a time and two initials in his own hand.** **He filled four of five *officer responsible* boxes off a wall board and refused the fifth, correctly, in about nine words, and paid for it with a query that came back with a sentence in it.** He typed a **0** against Mireward and said the sentence the whole season rests on. And he refused, in nine words, to write a row of frontages into a district row, and gave the reason that has nothing to do with caution: *that is a man who has one child and a mortgage and about eleven years to go.*
- **What he said that nobody argued with, twice:** ***nothing about Mireward is a record of Mireward… a place is entered as nil because there has been nobody to write down what is in it, and the two things look identical on a form and they are not the same thing, and the second one is worse*** — and **a seam event is not a gang going out, and a sheet like that is for having something on the day to give somebody, and it has never once told anybody what happened, and the gap between being told and being looked at is about eleven months and a filing cabinet, and that gap is where this season is going to happen.**

### Ola Petrossian

- **Twenty-three, four months in the job, never been past the tram steps.** In this batch she is the person in the working day, and **she has worked out on her own what a foreman of fifty-four worked out in thirty-one years.** She writes a time, a name and a room on a clinic form and **puts a ruler over the printed column headed *nature of attendance* and does not use it again for the rest of the day, and does not tear it off, because a sentence about a person is a record of a person**: *I have not torn it off. It is still there and anybody can lift the ruler and see that I did not fill it in, and that is better than tearing it off… a time is not a person.* **She was at the back of the counter on the Tuesday afternoon in January when a sheet was read out in a room with nine people in it, and has not been able to get it out of her ears since, and says so in about thirty words and would like it noticed that she has explained it as far as she has.** **She has still never been thanked for the card with a number on it she carried in December, has still not been asked to walk him to anything, and has now been the person who kept a counter open for ten hours a day for six of seven days.**

### Iona Sen

- **Where she is:** the water clinic, the dispensary counter, and a doorway on the evening of Sunday the first of February.
- **What she did in this batch.** **She opened the counter eight until six for seven days on a four-page forecast that cannot be entered anywhere, and on the Friday morning wrote the clinic's closing time and the event's hour in the same line of the counter's own day book with no mark between them, and said at the counter: *The clinic was shut four hours before it and I am writing both of those down together on purpose… a water clinic is not a thing that is open at two o'clock in the morning, and the day is the whole of what a person needs.*** **She said it six times in a doorway and asked for it to be minuted and nobody minuted it.** **On the first of February, in a yard of about nineteen, in a wind, she asked one question from the back and it is the hinge of the batch: *What happens on the day it does not come.*** And on the third of the batch's three failures she said the standing thing about hiding: *do not tear it up… you have got better at hiding them. Getting better at hiding them is not the same as being right, and it is further from being right than the first one was.* And on the Friday after the hearing's line was read out in a yard: **that a permanent line in a minute can be a protection and a gag at the same time, and a minute will only ever print one of those two words.**
- **The fifth finding is not cured, is not signed, and cannot be signed, and she has said nothing about the Atlas, nothing about her own past, and nothing about the man who taught Milo's hand.**

### The fishmonger of about forty-five

- **She is the correction, and the change of method, and she is not named.** She calls the lot prices from a doorway at about ten past five and has done for nineteen years, and puts her thumb in a crack in the wall at the south end for about half a minute afterwards, and said in January *it is not for anybody*. **In this batch a man of about forty sweeping the Row badly asked her at ten to six on Monday the second of February whether a piece of paper in a yard was right, and she read it, and she brought it up the Row at about ten past ten, and she made the first initials that have ever gone on the column headed *wrong*** — and the correction is that *a thing done somewhere else is not the same thing*, and that the sheet has nothing for it. **She then made the second side of that sheet in about eleven minutes, headed and ruled in her own hand, with a rule at the foot in her own hand** — *Nothing on this side is anybody's fault and nothing on this side is a reason. A side with nothing on it is a day when nobody stood in the weather* — **told Milo Rook about it afterwards, on purpose, as the last part, and he had no advice to give and was not asked.** She also, on the nineteenth of February, told nine people that there was coffee on that Row forty minutes early and that she could not tell where it was coming from. **She is not on any list, has never been asked to be, and has not been thanked and does not want to be.**

### The man of about twenty-six in the quay gang

- **Two years of stopping in the down loop, and in this batch three things.** **On the morning of the twenty-ninth of January he stopped nine feet short of a drain he has put his ear on every Monday and Thursday and got a shovel instead and said nothing** — the first time in two years. **On the second of February he wrote the first line on the second side of a sheet, and put his initials on it, and gave the reason out loud: the first side is about a person and this side is about a day, and if somebody asks him in four years he will say he stood in it and will not have to say why.** He will still never initial the first side. **On the twelfth of February he gave the batch's answer unasked, in about forty words, and then gave its price in nine words and said he was not going to say it twice.** He has no name, no initial on the first side, and has still not given either.

### The woman of sixty on the second landing of a building with no number

- **Sixty, off every list for nine years and on purpose, in an arrangement, and not named on the page and not to be named.** **She is the ninth of the twenty-six names that Mara Quill put into a Crown Heights file on the twelfth of January, and Lena Marr told her first and has not told the other seven.** **She asked three questions — *is it in a file, can it be taken out, can I see it* — and the second and the third are the same no, and the reason they are the same no is the district's one permanent gain.** Her twenty-three words are on the page and are not to be improved on, and they end: **I cannot see it, I cannot correct it, I cannot come off it, and I cannot ask anybody whether my name is spelled right.** And she said the other two things: that Lena Marr is going to come to about nine of these rooms one at a time, **and that she is the only one of the twenty-six who is going to notice that and it is hers**; and that **Lena Marr is the list**, and one day somebody will ask for it and Lena will have to decide then whether she is a person or an office. **Nobody in the district has told her what the hearing cost. She is not thanked and she is not on anybody's side of anything.**

### Lena Marr

- **Where she is:** the river core, the Board school, a second landing, and a yard. About thirty-four. **The named rateable head of a temporary accommodation household for two hundred and fifty-two people in a building with no number, at two and four a head a week, in advance, week by week, with no term.**
- **What she did in this batch, and each of it is the cost.** **She went up one flight on the Thursday afternoon of the fifth of February at about half past two and told a woman of sixty that she is the ninth of twenty-six names in a file, and came out with three questions asked and one answered.** **In a yard on the Friday she said the price out loud and named it: the reporting officer's permanent refusal of any schedule of persons is the reason that woman cannot get a copy of the only paper in the city with her name on it, and it is forbidden because the district asked for it and Mara Quill drafted the ask.** **She repeated the woman's twenty-three words in a yard word for word on the condition that she would repeat them word for word or not at all, and has offered the page she wrote them on once and been refused once.** **And she said in a yard that the seventh column in a surveyor's form is our tram, in about forty seconds, and named the mechanism exactly: a paper that will not know whether it happened and will say it happened.** She is still the only person in eleven streets who can be asked paragraph three's question, and **she is now also the only person in eleven streets who knows all twenty-six names and has deliberately not written them down, and has said so, and has said that one day somebody will ask her for them and she will have to decide then whether she is a person or an office.**
- **Unchanged and paid:** the levy, the seven school places, the two children lost in October who are still lost, and the address trap. **Nothing in Chapters 81–90 touches any of it.**

### The remaining people, and what did not happen to them

- **Cyril Sale**, works clerk, forty-six, **does not appear and is not named in these ten chapters.** The sixth line of the fourteenth of January, **fifty-four words in his own hand, is still in a file in another body's building, there is still no copy, the district still has no standing, and Milo Rook still does not know it and nobody in this batch tells him.**
- **Dilley Farr**, route room, thirty-one years, **does not appear and is not named.** The second tin now holds nine weeks of gang carbon and one page that is not a gang sheet, **and he has still not read the seventh column and never will, and the last line of it is still a name with a line through it.**
- **Ivy Marsden**, seventy-four, thirty-one years, **is not named, does not appear, and is not told anything.** A beam scale at the north end of the Row was found on the nineteenth of February with the poise off nothing, and the man who saw it **did not write it down, went back up the Row, and has not looked twice.**
- **Owen Fitch, Old Mabb, Jo Lask, Tomas Rusk, Nell Crale, Verrick Assal, the woman of fifty-four, Nessa Bray, Hester Coombe as a named speaker, Amos Trench, Bettina Sallow, Jo Lask, Barrow, Ola's colleagues** — **not in these ten chapters and not named in them**, with the single exception of Hettie Brann, who appears because this batch is about the first working day of the month. **Old Mabb is one clause in Chapter 90's list, is not named and is not woken. Owen Fitch's name is still on five copies of a sheet of paper with a contractor's head on it and has not been taken off any of them.** **Ivo Sarratt is not named in these ten chapters** and the adjourned hearing of Tuesday the fourteenth of April appears as a date and not as a proceeding that has happened.
- **Orrin Sable, Halden Vey, Sel Iri, Tarin Kest** do not appear and are not named. **Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–90.** **No new grammar, faction, antagonist, final enemy, or fourth kind of place.**

---

## Volume 02, Batch 0004 (Chapters 91–100) — state after Chapter 100

**Where everyone is on Wednesday the first of April.** A second month of a season that cannot be dated has gone by and **two of the eleven weather-driven seam events have happened and there is still no calendar, and there is now the additional fact that nothing whatever is printed in this city for the fifteenth of April, and a man of fifty-one in a room with a machine in it has been in that room nineteen years and has never seen a second outlook and cannot tell whether the second does not exist or has not been sent.** A cast-iron cover has been rocking for seven weeks and there is no form in the city that will take a sentence about it. A six-line form has come back down to a section office with a box headed *does the section stand behind the entry*. A list of the departmental business of the fourteenth of April has been served on the district as a party, and item six of it says *presented* about six hundred yards of tram route, and the district has a chair and was asked for nothing. **A woman of sixty-eight on nothing.** Two and four a head a week, in advance, week by week, with no term, for a building with no number. **The second of March is done and the first of April is done and there is no fourth of either before the fourteenth.**

### Milo Rook

- **Where he is:** the route room, a yard, a hundred and ten yards of frontage that is not a street, a corridor in Crown Heights, a depot office at Alder Quay, and a clinic counter. **Next thing in the book: the fourteenth of April.**
- **Stage: Stage 2, begun and not complete, and he held nothing in these ten chapters.** He went into nowhere, held nothing, and crossed nothing, **and the reason is on the page twice: that he is not going to make a form about a practice, and that he did not go near the north end of the Row and has not written down why.** The outline places field grammar across Volumes 2–3 and this block deepened the identification — a practice that is not a daily practice, on a side built for days — and did not advance the ability.
- **Crossings: nine. He crossed nothing in Chapters 91–100.** Iona Sen's tally is unchanged at nine lines, the paper is Hettie Brann's and the pocket is Dr Sen's, and he has not looked at it.
- **The orientation ledger, and it did not move, and one thing did.** The remembered-route list **stands at TEN**, the original eight intact, none removed, all ten marked by somebody who is not him, **nothing added and nothing lost in these ten chapters**, and the ninth crossing's failure to cost one is still unexplained and is not explained by anything here. **The written line home is the hundred and twenty-ninth day of it on the first of April and the ten figures printed in this block are 92, 103, 108, 112, 115, 119, 123, 127, 128 and 129**, all on the chapter that owns the day. **And on the first of April it was marked in a fourth hand by a woman of sixty-one at a counter who was not asked, in about four seconds, in front of nine people, and she refused to put it in the envelope — and it is there on the eighth, and on the twelfth, and on the fourteenth, and it does not come back. His own sentence: *it is not a cure and it is not an absence of one. It is a line somebody else can walk me home by, and on the twenty-seventh of December that is all it was, and on the first of April that is all it is.*** **It is not cured and the district's refusal to keep asking remains a refusal and not a kindness.**
- **The Stage 2 cost, its fourth instance, a sound.** A tram gong at the end of Cardew Street on Monday the sixteenth of March at about ten to six in the evening, eleven seconds, and at four seconds into it he could not tell what it was **and he needed it, because a Line 3 car comes down that street at about that time and the coffee man walks against the traffic at half past five, and he wanted to know whether a car was coming so that he could say so.** He used a watch. **A sound and a taste and no face, no place, no person and no name, and he has still decided nothing about whether that is one thing or two. The first personal blank, the seam-scar, the record blank and the authorial blank appear nowhere in Chapters 91–100.**
- **His failure in this block, and it is the seventh column for the fourth time in about ten weeks, and it is the same mistake.** A form headed *SECOND SIDE — DAYS WITH NO LINE*, the columns of the sheet it is about, and a seventh headed **WHY THERE IS NO LINE ON THIS DAY** — *a question about a practice, and there is no such question in this district that has ever been answered by anybody.* He filled in two blanks **and filled them in well**, and both reasons were true and neither was about a day. **Doreen Sankey said four words before he had got to the bottom of the page: *There is a why in it.*** And the fishmonger, from her own doorway: **a rule at the foot of a sheet is for the person whose hand it is going to be asked about, and your seventh column is a rule for nobody** — *and the answer is for a man in an office in about four years, and I have known that since about the tenth of January.* And then **he broke his own written undertaking in the same yard on which he had sworn on the sixth of February that he was not going to be the man who said that in a room again for about a year, said so out loud, and asked for it to be written down, and nobody wrote it down — the eighth time in nine weeks.** **And the resolution is new and is a method: he put it face up on a trestle table in the rain, squared, took his hand off it, and did not take it, did not burn it and did not give it to anybody, and it was gone off the table in a wind by about Wednesday. It is the first of his that has left a district without him.**
- **The recovery he got was somebody else's work twice, and neither of them is his.** **A man with a broom, twenty-eight words under a rule, undated and uninitialled, correcting the district's own instrument about a Wednesday** — *he is not here on a Wednesday and he has not been here on a Wednesday since before the war* — **which established that the second side has been blank on every Wednesday since the second of February and that a blank on a day-side is not a fault, and a thing that is not wrong cannot be struck out, and there is no column on that paper for a man who is somewhere else.** And **Ola Petrossian, twenty-three, four hours in eleven streets in her own time, nine doors and no names** — *and I was right about the list of doors and I was refused by the person it was about, and I have written down that being right is not the same as being the person who decides.*
- **What he refused.** To write a form about the second side's five-days-in-seven fault. To write the word *why* anywhere, and he has caught himself three times in two days starting a sentence with it. To take the sheet away, burn it, or give it to anybody. To reduce the four in the river core or the zero against Mireward. To put a name in the river core *allocated to* box, or to ask the drainage man to. To put a name in a *risk to passengers* box, or to answer a query on a section's behalf. **To read a crew's number twice.** To put the crew's non-working week in his own book. To ask Hettie Brann about a version number she has not asked him about, **and to ask Mara Quill about the season, and he has written that not telling her was not the correct procedure and was not a courtesy, and that if he goes on not telling her after the fourteenth then it is not a rule.** To go to Mireward. To go near the north end of the Row. To look at the scale a second time. To ask a woman of sixty-three about her mother's boy. **To say the seventh-column thing in a room again for about a year, and he said it in a room on the fifteenth of March, which is the failure and the cost and is not cured.**
- **The Lark token: cold throughout, not out of the coat, and is the seventy-eighth, eighty-ninth, ninety-fourth, ninety-eighth, hundred and first, hundred and fifth, hundred and ninth, hundred and thirteenth, hundred and fourteenth and hundred and fifteenth day since the seventh of December.** It was not warm once in this block and produced nothing.
- **The Field Book: TWENTY dated pairs — one six, SEVEN threes, one nine, one two, two fives, one eight, one twenty-five, two sevens, and FOUR fours, all four of them in March, and there is no thirteen-day pair and there never was.** **The four threes that were here in December are the four that were here in December and are not collapsed; the fifth was added in the first week of February, the sixth on the twenty-third of February and the seventh on the eighteenth of March, and none of the original four is one of the other three.** The single-day interval from the tenth of December is still looked at and is still not written, and there are **no one-day gaps in this block**: the runs of nothing are two, eleven, three, two, three, three and three days, and the two single-day intervals at the end of the block are silences he has declined to pair. **There is no thirty-six, no eleventh pair, no thirteenth pair, no twenty-first pair and no twenty-second pair; the figure is twenty and it is a claim to be checked against the chapter that owns each interval and not an authority.** Atlas residue was used **once in ten chapters, in Chapter 95, in six words: *A habit is not a calendar.*** **Ten entries in ten chapters, on ten dates, and four days in February have no entry at all (the twenty-first and twenty-second, the sixteenth and the seventeenth of March), and he has written down that the two-day run of nothing is a two-day run of nothing and not a pair.**
- **What he has now written down that he had not.** **The seventh column is not a mistake. The seventh column is the only thing he is good at, and he gets to be good at it about four times a year, and every time he gets to be good at it somebody else has to hold the paper** — *and I have been very pleased with myself about it three times and that is the actual finding of the last ten weeks.* And: **a blank on the second side is a day when nobody stood in the weather, and a woman of about forty-five is right that a number that cannot get better is the only kind of number worth writing, and a man of about forty is right that grit in an axle is a low place and is not a roof and is not on any form and cannot be bought, and he is worse at this than either of them and has written that down.** And: **the writer of the map is not the maintainer of anything, and a length is still a third kind of place, and a row of frontages is still a Keepsake remnant, and the two are still two kinds of place and are not merged.**
- **The clue chain: not touched and not advanced.** Nothing in Chapters 91–100 joins any document to any other. **The three Volume 01 clues appear in no chapter of this block as a document. The second tin is not a fourth instance. Two documents still carry a pair of marks and not three; three references exist and are references and not pairs; Kest has not matched the strings; and Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–100.** No fifth sighting of the works-jacket man and **no fifth refusal of it by anybody**. A crease was not used as a date; **Hettie Brann's count is a count of versions and not a date of reading, and the instrument that reads four copies by softness was not used again.**
- **The Lark Street grievance is not named in Chapters 91–100, and is not resolved and not softened and not referred to.**
- **How he ends the block, in his own hand:** *the writer of the map is not the maintainer of anything, and the fourteenth of April is a fortnight away and I have not got a use for that and I am not going to invent one, and I am going to be in a hall or I am not and I have not been asked and the yard is not asking me* — **and it is a list of other people, and he is not cured, and the seventh column is the same mistake every time.**

### Mara Quill

- **Where she is:** the Registry, and a corridor in Crown Heights on Tuesday the thirty-first of March. A two-year note, an appeal in a line not stayed, no preferred access, twenty-six names in a Crown Heights file, a minuted refusal to identify a document in her custody, a nine-minute letter answered and refused in full, and **a second letter written on the eighteenth of February and not sent, which is not mentioned in these ten chapters and remains unsent.**
- **What the custody register page did in this block: nothing, for the third time.** It is in a drawer with a coat in it. **She did not produce it, did not name it, did not confess to taking it, and was not asked, and it is not mentioned in dialogue in any of the ten chapters.** **He did not ask, and has written in his own book for the third time that not asking was the correct procedure and not a courtesy, and that the not-asking has now stopped being procedure and become something else, and that he has not decided what.** **Her knowledge of the token and the custody column is still unused and undeclared and he still does not know she refused anything, and this block spends none of it.**
- **What happened in the one scene she is in.** She received a departmental list as the district's advocate, and a corridor, and about eleven seconds each way. **She told him, unasked, that she cannot tell which sections are on item four and is not going to find out, and he did not ask her anything.** And then she said the sentence the chapter turns on: ***this sheet is the only piece of paper in five months that has this district's name on it, and it has our name on it in the first line, and it has asked us for nothing, and it has given us a chair. A hearing is a thing somebody asks questions in. A list is a thing nobody asks anything in, and a list goes on a wall and gets read by about nine people and then goes in a file for eleven years.*** And the answer to *and what is item one about* is not a document: **somebody has to be in a room on the fourteenth and say it out loud.**
- **What she was not told, which is the new thing in this block.** **He did not tell her about the season.** He has written down, in his own book, that a four-page document from that office is not evidence in a hearing about a sentence in a report, and that if the word weather goes into a file by Friday then on the fourteenth of April a man will ask what season, and that **if he goes on not telling her after the fourteenth then it is not a rule and he will write that down in April in the same hand as everything else in the book.** **Neither of them is named as knowing and neither is named as not knowing, and the reader is given the fact and not the interpretation.**
- **Nothing romantic has happened and nothing is going to. No kiss, no declaration, no jealousy scene, no rescue. The Lark Street business was not raised in these ten chapters and she neither raised it nor softened it, and he did not either.** What exists between them is procedural: a list, a chair, a withheld document, a season not mentioned, and nine lines of his own route home written on a counter by a third person in front of nine people, and she is not the third person.

### Nell Crale

- **Where she is:** four rooms over a shop on Bridge Street low end, and six stops, and a cellar. Sixty-three, **twenty-two years of a milk-and-ice round in two baskets on a hand barrow, forty-one pounds, six stops, and no machine on that street.**
- **What she did in this block, and it is the batch's working day with a person in it and it was not in a yard.** She walked a surveyor round her round for two hours and a half and **she was right about the roof and right about the grit in the axle and right about the Friday**, and he has written that she is better at reading a place than he is. Two of her sentences are the chapter and are not to be improved on: **grit in an axle is a low place and it is not a roof and it is not a measurement and it is not on any form and you cannot buy one**; and **I have a week in my head that nobody in this district has ever written down, and it is not a schedule and it is not on a rota and it is not a column, and I have never had to say it to anybody, and I have been doing it since the second year.** She has refused a machine twice and the reason is not dignity: **the day I am on a schedule one of these six stops becomes an appointment, and an appointment can be moved, and nobody in the world needs an appointment to be moved.**
- **What she will not be asked about, and the standing cost.** A board outside a portacabin with four questions in a clerk's hand and the fourth one *subsidence observed*, a tick and a time and no column for anything else, **is what her mother's boy died of, and she is not in a yard, and he did not ask her one question about it, and she is not going to be asked.**
- **What she has not been refunded:** a cellar with six inches of silt and salt in it and a frost that has not gone out in nine years, **the works' answer is November and nobody came in the March either, and she has stopped saying so and still goes round with half the ice she carried in the December.**

### Hettie Brann

- **Where she is:** the water clinic counter, nineteen years on it. **The tally is her paper and not her apron pocket, and it has nine lines on it and she has not read it.**
- **What she did in this block, and each of it is the volume's whole argument in an exchange with nobody.** **She wrote a number on the outside of the envelope in pencil in her own hand for the first time — *1 of 4 differs* — on the sixth of March, because a count of houses goes up and a count of people goes up or down and a count of documents that do not agree with each other is not a complaint about the world, and it cannot be got better by anybody working harder.** **On the first of April she did it a third and last time before the fourteenth, the number is identical, and the fact behind it is different, because the odd one out is a different piece of paper in a different building and she cannot tell which** — *if anybody in four years reads *1 of 4* on this corner they are going to think they know which month it was written in, and they will be wrong* — **and she refuses to write *and it is a different one*, in these words: *a number is the one thing about four people that I am allowed to have, and it is the one thing I am going to have.*** **She made the second piece of paper of her life on purpose, said so out loud in front of about eleven people, promised not to make a third without saying so, and said the sentence the volume is about: *I want it to be wrong-shaped. The moment a thing can be used it is in use.*** And at the end of the batch **she wrote nine lines of Milo's own route home down on a counter in front of nine people, unasked, and refused to put it in the envelope** — *the envelope is four copies with three hands and a disagreement that started in January over a gate, and this is a different thing and it does not go in there.* And she asked the question nobody has asked, in the ordinary way, because asking it in the ordinary way was the only way it worked: **what do you print in the second week of May?**
- **What she has not been asked to be and will not be:** the door-check, the reader, the person who holds a mark overnight, the person who knows what is written on four pieces of paper, **and the person who writes a route home in a book of somebody else's.** *If anybody asks me what is in it I will say it is a piece of paper.*

### Ola Petrossian

- **Twenty-three, four months in the job, never been past the tram steps — and on Sunday the twenty-second of March she went.** **Four hours in eleven streets, in her own time, unpaid, and she came back with nine doors and no names, on the back of a clinic form, in a bag, and it is not in the machine.** The reason, in eleven words in a yard: *then what is a water clinic for on a Tuesday morning, and who is standing in the kitchen?* The rule she set on the doorstep is four words — **doors and not people** — and the reason is that she has been taking the sheets since the middle of January and *a sentence about a person is a record of a person, and a door is not a person.* **The ninth door is a side door on a store that is shut, because a store is not a dwelling and nobody in this district has ever counted a store.**
- **What happened to it:** Milo Rook said the true thing about rate books and **was refused by the person the objection was about**, and she said *I am not going to be the woman who holds the fourth copy of a card and does not know what is in it. I have watched that happen and I do not know how anybody lives with it*, and he wrote down that he was right and that being right is not the same as being the person who decides. **Hettie Brann made it the second paper under her counter and said so out loud. It is going to happen again on the seventh of April and nobody is to stop her and nobody has said so out loud.**
- **She is not a device and she has no power and she is frightened of what she has done and has said so.** She has still never been thanked and is not going to be asked to stop.

### Doreen Sankey

- **Where he is:** two rooms behind a boatyard on the Ferry Road side, forty-one, **on no list at all, and it did not change in this block.**
- **What he did in this block, and it is the batch's diagnosis and it is four words.** On Sunday the fifteenth of March, at a trestle table, with the sheet in the other man's hand: **"There is a why in it."** And the reason: *you are still holding it, and you have not got to the bottom of the page and you have already said the word.* And then the arithmetic, which is not February's arithmetic: **you have written down, in your own hand, with a heading, that the round does not go out on a Saturday, and that is true, and nobody has to be asked, and it does not matter, because the asking has already happened — in a kitchen, in advance, under a heading — and in about four years six people are going to remember that a man of thirty-one stood in a yard in March and held a piece of paper about their Saturdays.** And: *if you take it off the table then it is a man who had a form about other people's week and put it away, and that is a different sentence and it is not a better one.*
- **And on the thirty-first of March, in a yard, his other half of the fortnight: *a list is a thing you read and a minute is a thing you are in.*** And he named the standing rule about the card under the brick, which is his and which Pell Harrow has not contradicted, and which is a man of thirty-one who is not the one doing the looking.
- **He has no power, no mark, no name on any list, and he has told this district the standing condition of its own method three times now, for free and to no schedule.** He has not been thanked and does not want to be.

### The man of about fifty in the drainage section

- **Where he is:** a room in Crown Heights, **about fifty, fifty-one next month, nineteen years in it, no name on the page, and a later batch may not invent one.**
- **What he did in this block, and it is the batch's institutional centre.** He **left a printed allocation sheet face down on his own desk over a weekend for the first time in nineteen years and cannot afterwards have told anybody why he decided to.** He **filled the *allocated to* column for the first time in nineteen years, three names off the wall board, and refused Mireward in twenty words**, and the refusal is a *different* refusal from January's and he says so and says it is better: *there is nobody to hand it to… and I have just done the January thing to him in a different column and got further with it.* He **left the river core box blank for a reason that is not a principle**, because a man he has watched for nineteen years walks past at about eleven for a cup of tea and there is no day on which to say he was told anything. And **the machine's answer, seven days later, with a sentence in the reason column where it has always had a dash**, and his sentence about it: **a machine is a much worse thing than a man about being difficult, because a man stops and a machine does not.**
- **And he answered the batch's question.** Put his pen down for about four minutes and said **there is nothing** — *I have been in this room nineteen years and I have seen the one that came in December and I have never seen another and I have never asked for one and I have never known anybody who has, and I am telling you that as a fact about a room and not as a finding, because I have never once in nineteen years been in a position to know whether a thing does not exist or whether it exists and has not been sent.*
- **Everything that goes wrong through him in this block is attributable to a form, a machine, a list a hundred and forty years old, a column nobody has ever filled in and a week he left a sheet face down, and not to him. He has not reduced the four and he has not reduced the zero, and the river core row still carries one hand-entered line, and Mireward carries no new nil.**

### Cyril Sale

- **Where he is:** the quay section office, four rooms above a fitter's shop, forty-six, four years in the job.
- **What he did in this block, twice, and both times about a box.** *One.* **A defect report could not be made and the four refusals were correct**, and the reason is nine words of his: *the box wants a place it can send a man to, and that box is a man with a barrow on a Tuesday, and he cannot find six hundred yards of track because it has not got a name and he will not find it on a diagram either, because the diagram took it off in October and the diagram was right to take it off.* And **he refused to put nil in the risk box**, because a nil there is a lie about the twenty-second of December, and **wrote on the back of the form that the box is left empty and why, and put it in the correspondence tray and not in a drawer, and said he had done that before in January and had noticed himself doing it again.** *Two.* **A form came back down with a box headed *does the section stand behind the entry*, on line six, which is his fifty-four words, and which does not ask what he meant.** **He left the box empty, ruled a line through it, dated it, and did not tell the foreman that the line was his — and when the foreman said *read it to me* he said no, in one word.** And the other half, which is the finding: **nobody has ever been asked about line five**, and a nil on a box headed *persons observed on the length during the year* has now been read by a man with a pencil in a corridor and has not been queried, **and that is the second time in six months this district has been protected by a clerk being fast, and he has said that he could have been that pencil.**
- **The sixth line of the fourteenth of January is still in a file in another body's building, there is still no copy, the district still has no standing, and Milo Rook still does not know what is in it and nobody in this block tells him.** The return will be filled in again in January and in every January after this one and the only true answer to the fifth box is still nil.

### Roy Pryce

- **Where he is:** the quay section, fifty-four, thirty-one years, four lengths, and a cover that is still rocking.
- **What he did in this block.** He **instructed a defect report that could not be made** and said the sentence that is the whole of the business: **the cover rocks and the sheet does not and the sheet is the truer of the two** — and *I have got eleven men walking past it twice a week and I have not got one box in this city that will take a sentence about it, and you are the fourth person in four months to come at that with a piece of stationery.* And he **was asked to sign a query on a line he had not read, and was told the line was the clerk's, and said *read it to me*, and was told no**, and did not have it read out to him and has said it will come back in a fortnight and probably will and probably will carry a sentence instead of a dash.
- **He still has not written one word about a person on a form, and he still refuses to sign a line he has not read, and in about four months a man in an office is still going to ask him what he saw.** The nine words in the hours column are still nine words and are not re-counted in this block.

### The man of about forty who sweeps the Row

- **He has swept that row at about ten past six every morning since he was twenty-one and nobody had ever asked him anything, and on Thursday the nineteenth of March at ten past six in the morning he put a broom down and said nine words and they are the whole of the batch's changed method: *he is not here on a Wednesday, and he has not been here on a Wednesday since before the war, because there is a stall at Cardew Docks that opens at six and that is the day it is busy.***
- **The correction he made is twenty-eight words written on the second side under the rule, in his own hand, undated and uninitialled**, and the reason for not initialing it is the first side of the same sheet. **He is not on any list, is not on a rota, has not been thanked, and does not want to be, and he has not told the coffee man and is not going to** — *I have swept this row since I was twenty-one and I have never once told him what time I am out here and I am not going to start by telling him about a piece of paper.* **He has no name on the page and a later batch may not make him one.**

### The fishmonger of about forty-five

- **She is the diagnosis of the batch, twice, and neither of them is a courtesy.** *One*, on Sunday the fifteenth of March, from her own doorway, on what a rule at the foot of a sheet is for: **a rule at the foot of a sheet is for the person whose hand it is going to be asked about, and your seventh column is a rule for nobody, because there is not one person in eleven streets whose hand is going to be in the answer** — *and the answer is for a man in an office in about four years, and I have known that since about the tenth of January, and I have had a fish stall to run.* *Two*, on Wednesday the nineteenth, on the twenty-eight words a man with a broom wrote under her rule: **I am not adding a paragraph to my own rule. The moment there is a second paragraph there is an instruction in it, and a person can say no to an instruction, and nobody can say no to a line.** She is still not named, still not on any list, has not given her name in six chapters of this volume, and **has said in this block that she is not for a man in an office either, and that a man sweeping asked her a question once and she answered it, and that is the only reason any of it happened.**

### The coffee man of about sixty

- **He is not on the Row on a Wednesday and has not been since before the war; on Thursday the nineteenth of March at ten past six in the morning he said so out loud, unasked, for the first time, and about four people in this district know it and he does not know that they know.** He **has not been told that a piece of paper exists and is not going to be**, and the man who found it out has not told him and has said why. **His hand went on the corner at four seconds on Monday the sixteenth of March and the surveyor wanted to shout to warn him that a car was coming and could not tell whether one was, and the man was not in the road.** He has still said twice, and this block did not add to the two, that it is not his and it is not for anybody.

### Pell Harrow

- **Where he is:** the round, out at eight and in at four. Seventeen. **A courier and a Streetkeeper with no power of any kind, and not Milo's assistant.**
- **Tern: still not found, and nothing about the twenty-two days is asked or said in these ten chapters.** He is named once, in a yard on the thirty-first of March, **and his card under the brick is blank and has been since the nineteenth of January and he has not taken it and nobody has.** **The three things under that brick are still three: the run-number card with two lines on the back of it, the works-jacket card, and his own blank card.** **The man in the works jacket is not seen once in these ten chapters and nobody was asked to look, and no fifth sighting and no fifth refusal was made by anybody**, and Pell's standing sentence about sightings and cards and columns is not repeated.
- **What he said in this block, which is about a document and not about a sighting: *a list is a thing you read and a minute is a thing you are in*** — and then he named the standing rule about the card under the brick, which is a man of forty-one's, and which he has not contradicted. He is on no form and he is not in this block's recovery and has not been asked to be.

### Rue

- **Where she is:** the round, the trestle table, the yard, and the top of the Row.
- **What she did in this block.** **She read out a form she did not approve of word for word without changing anything, which is what she does, and she then said the sentence that convicted him: *I did not think about it, and I have been thinking about it for nine days and I did not think about it in eleven minutes, and I would like it noticed that I did not, and I would like it noticed more that you have been sitting on it since Saturday.*** She **refused to take a column in a yard and said *I am going to walk off*, and he did not say it.** She **stopped him in about four seconds with *that is the seventh column*, and then made him say out loud that he had promised on the sixth of February and had done it, and nobody wrote it down.** And at the end of the block she **refused the batch's answer and refused the batch's good sentence**, unasked for: *you have just said a good thing and it is going to be used… you have been saying nothing in a yard for four months and it has been the best thing anybody has done for this district, and you have just stopped.*
- **What she refused, again:** to take a column; to be thanked; to be leaned on; **to have a second paragraph added to her own rule on that side of the sheet** — *the moment there is a second paragraph there is an instruction in it*; and to have a blank be filled in or a fault be struck. **She did not repeat the sentence about the fence and no new face said it.**

### Hester Coombe

- **Depot clerk, Alder Quay, fifty-one, twenty-two years.** She appears once, at about eleven in the morning on Wednesday the first of April, **asked by nobody**, and did it on a Wednesday rather than the Monday because *a person who is going to do a thing nobody asked her to do should do it on a day when she has a half hour, and the fifteenth is off and the third is a Saturday.* She had said in January that she would not and then had, and she has said that this time she had not said she would not, and that those are different. **The finding, in her own mouth: the same crew's number is entered in the working timetable for the week commencing Sunday the twenty-first of December and is not worked on any day of it, and there is no column on that page for a week in which a crew does not work, because a crew that does not work does not happen, and the row of a thing that did not happen is the same blank as the row of a thing that did.** **She told him not to come back, and that if he wants to know anything else he should ask Barrow, and that if Barrow will not tell him he should not come back here either.** Milo Rook did not write it down and left a card under a brick.

### Dilley Farr

- **Route room, thirty-one years. Named twice in this block and neither time is his doing**: once as the officer responsible in a box that is not even true, and once, by a man of about fifty, as the reason the river core *allocated to* box was left blank — *Dilley Farr is in this building and walks to about eleven o'clock for a cup of tea and I have watched that for nineteen years.* He does not appear, is not named in dialogue, does not read the page in the tin, and the second tin now holds **fifteen weeks of gang carbon and one page that is not a gang sheet** on Friday the thirty-first of March, and will hold sixteen on Friday the third of April.

### The remaining people, and what did not happen to them

- **Lena Marr, Iona Sen as a named speaker, Jo Lask, Tomas Rusk, Owen Fitch, old Mabb, Nessa Bray, Verrick Assal, the woman of fifty-four, Verrick's line, Amos Trench, Bettina Sallow, Barrow, Ivy Marsden** — **none of them is named in these ten chapters.** Iona Sen is referred to twice, as *Dr Sen* and as *the doctor*, in the reasoning of a nurse of twenty-three and in a counter's account of her, and is not present. **Lena Marr does not appear by name at all, and the rule 14 proceeding is not staged again, and she is not put in a second room in this block, and the levy, the seven school places, the two children lost in October and the address trap are carried and none of them moved.** **Owen Fitch's name is still on five copies of a sheet of paper with a contractor's head on it and has not been taken off any of them. Old Mabb is a clause in Chapter 100's list and is not named and is not woken. Ivy Marsden is not named, does not appear and is not told anything; the poise on the beam scale at the north end was not looked at a second time, and Milo Rook did not go near the north end of the Row in this block.** **Ivo Sarratt is named once in these ten chapters, on a printed departmental list in Chapter 99, as *Reporting officer: I. Sarratt, Survey Enforcement*, and that is the first time his name has been on a document in front of this district since the twelfth of January; and nothing in this block has him learning that the season ends on the same day, and nothing has either office learning of the other.**
- **Orrin Sable, Halden Vey, Sel Iri and Tarin Kest** do not appear and are not named. **Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–100. The man in the works jacket is not seen once in this block. No new grammar, faction, antagonist, final enemy, or fourth kind of place.**

---

## Volume 02, Batch 0005 (Chapters 101–110) — state after Chapter 110

**Where everyone is on Saturday the twenty-fifth of April.** The hearing sat on Tuesday the fourteenth of April as a **continuation of an adjourned matter** and **determined nothing and adjourned nothing and set no date.** The order is suspended and is not discharged. The public name is not on the street name index. The classification of the eleventh is withdrawn and stays withdrawn. The removed sentence cannot be restored and the department cannot account for its removal and has now been asked twice and not answered twice. **On Thursday the twenty-third of April a second departmental list came to this district by hand and the matter was not on it, and the board in the corridor changed three days before the envelope arrived, and there is no form in this city for a party being told that it is not on a list.** A cast-iron cover has been flush since the sixth of April and there is no column in this city that will record a fault that stopped. A settlement plate with a name and a date on it has been in the grass behind a fence at the low end of Bridge Street since the sixth of April and nobody has claimed it and nobody has told the works. About nine people stood in the weather on Wednesday the fifteenth of April for nothing at all. **A woman of sixty-eight on nothing.** Two and four a head a week, in advance, week by week, with no term, for a building with no number. **The next comparison of the four return-route copies is Monday the third of May and there is no fourth before it and no fourth before that.**

### Milo Rook

- **Where he is:** a yard, a corridor in Crown Heights, the Hall of Wards, thirty-four steps down into the Survey School's repository, the quay section office four rooms above a fitter's shop, a room above a canteen on Parcel Street, a counter, a hundred and ten yards of frontage that is not a street, and a front room. **Next thing in the book: nothing with a date on it.**
- **Stage: Stage 2, begun and not complete, and he held nothing in these ten chapters.** He went into nowhere, held nothing, and crossed nothing, and on the fourteenth he stood up in a room for about two minutes and the token was in the coat on his own front door and it was cold and it did nothing.
- **Crossings: nine. He crossed nothing in Chapters 101–110.** Iona Sen's tally is unchanged at nine lines, the paper is Hettie Brann's and the pocket is Dr Sen's, and **he has not looked at it since the thirteenth of January.**
- **The orientation ledger, and it did not move, and one thing did.** The remembered-route list **stands at TEN**, the original eight intact, none removed, all ten marked by somebody who is not him, **nothing added and nothing lost in these ten chapters**, and the ninth crossing's failure to cost one is still unexplained and is not explained by anything here. **The written line home is the hundred and fifty-third day of it on the twenty-fifth of April. The eight Field Book figures printed in this block are 138, 142, 144, 148, 149, 150, 151 and 153**, on the tenth, fourteenth, sixteenth, twentieth, twenty-first, twenty-second, twenty-third and twenty-fifth of April, **and one further figure, 136 on the eighth of April, is written in the prose of Chapter 101.** Blank throughout, with three marks in three hands until the first of April and a fourth in a fourth hand on that day, written at a counter in four seconds by a woman of sixty-one who was not asked and who refused to put it in the envelope. **It is there on the eighth, and on the twelfth, and on the fourteenth, and on the fifteenth, and it is not a cure and it is not an absence of one — it is a line somebody else can walk me home by, and on the twenty-seventh of December that is all it was, and on the first of April that is all it is, and he has stopped trying to improve that sentence because a woman of forty-five has told him that a rule with a second paragraph in it is an instruction.** The district's refusal to keep asking is still a refusal and not a kindness.
- **The Stage 2 cost did not recur in these ten chapters, and that is a fact and not a withdrawal.** It is a sound and a taste and no face, no place, no person and no name, and he has still decided nothing about whether that is one thing or two. **The first personal blank, the seam-scar, the record blank and the authorial blank appear nowhere in Chapters 101–110.**
- **The Lark token: cold throughout, not out of the coat, and the eight Field Book figures in this block are 124, 128, 130, 134, 135, 136, 137 and 139.** It went to a corridor in Crown Heights on Thursday the twenty-third with him in the coat and it was cold and it did nothing, and he has written that down six times and it is still the truest thing he has got about it. **The coat was very nearly the reason for one of the things that are not in his book on Monday the nineteenth, and he has written that it got close and has not written down which.**
- **He spoke on the fourteenth, and the reason is statable out loud, and he gave it himself in a corridor and she turned it into three conditions.** *In January the district had nothing to say that a minute could hold… there is one thing in that room this morning that is not a document, and I am the only person in it who can say the shape of the thing that is not there.* He said it for about two minutes, offered nothing as evidence and nothing as a submission, handed nothing in, put no number on it and no reference on it, and has written that he said it in about eleven seconds before he had finished the second sentence of it.
- **His failure in this block is the seventh column for the fifth time in thirteen weeks, and it is the same mistake, and a works clerk of forty-six named the shape of it before he had got to the bottom of the page** — *you have got a seventh one… I have watched four men in this city fill one in, and every single one of them did it in a kitchen, and you are the fifth* — and then the finding: **a column that wants to know why wants a person to put their name against the why, and in about four years the name on that page is the name of a woman of thirty-one who drafted the paragraph that caused the third matter, and he would have made her the officer responsible for a sentence nobody can produce.** **The resolution is new and it is not a resolution: he did not finish it.** A half-filled box, which is the opposite of an exception because an exception has to be cleared. **The five failures are now on the page across five chapters: the *BACK LENGTH (unnumbered) — MAINTENANCE* page in a tin on a bottom shelf; the column headed *scheduled* in his own coat; the yard in which he made the argument for a zero against his own district; the *SECOND SIDE — DAYS WITH NO LINE* form left face up in the rain and gone in a wind; and the *CO/6/BELL — MATTERS NOT DETERMINED* form.** **He is not cured and the page is not a form about a person.**
- **The recovery and the changed method are the same person and they are not his: Hettie Brann, and the method is the second half of a conjunction she wrote in January.** *Ask the person whose hand it is and nobody else.* He read it in March, took the first half and left the second. **Custody is a thing a person holds. Not a tin, not a coat, not a table in the rain, not an office, not a drawer in his own office, not under a brick. A drawer under a counter is wrong-shaped and cannot be chased, cannot be produced and cannot be given a reference, because a counter is not a repository.**
- **What he refused.** To write a why against any of the four things that have happened in this district in five months. To reduce the four in the river core or the zero against Mireward, or to ask the man of fifty to. To say that the plate was one of the eleven. To be in a yard on Wednesday the twenty-second when the third paper went under a counter. To stand in the weather on Wednesday the fifteenth, **because he was the only one of the nine who would have had a reason.** To ask Hettie Brann about a version number, about the third of May, or about what is in the seventh column. To tell a reader of fifty-four that he had heard the same sentence said in other words by the index clerk of her own room in December, in front of her, and never joined the two. To ask the man with the broom about the Wednesday, or the fishmonger for her name, or the coffee man anything. To go near the north end of Saltmarket Row, and to look at the scale a second time. To go on the far side of the fence. To take a page out of the tin, read it, or tell the woman whose name is on it. **To ask Mara Quill about the seventh of January — five times in a fortnight — and to tell her about the season, which was his own test and which he failed and wrote down that he failed.** To come back to a depot, to send the man from the round to a timetable division, or to write down what a clerk of nineteen years said to him. To improve what he said in a room afterwards, in a yard, ever.
- **The Field Book: TWENTY-SEVEN dated pairs — two sixes, seven threes, one nine, four twos, three fives, one eight, one twenty-five, two sevens, and six fours, four of them in March and two of them in April.** The seven new intervals in this block are **6 (30 March to 5 April), 5 (5 to 10 April), 4 (10 to 14 April), 2 (14 to 16 April), 4 (16 to 20 April), 2 (21 to 23 April) and 2 (23 to 25 April)**, and **the one-day intervals of 20 to 21, 21 to 22 and 23 to 24 April are looked at and are not written and are not pairs.** The four threes that were here in December are the four that were here in December and are not collapsed. There is one eight. **There is no thirty-six, no eleventh pair, no thirteenth pair, no twenty-first pair and no twenty-second pair; the figure is twenty-seven and it is a claim to be checked against the chapter that owns each interval and not an authority, and Chapter 101's list, dated the tenth of April, correctly reads twenty-two and must not be read against the closing list.** Atlas residue was used **once in ten chapters, in Chapter 108, in seven words: *It is dated before it is read.*** He did not use it in the Survey School's repository in Chapter 104 and has written that down.
- **What he has now written down that he had not.** **A forecast does not come down because a thing happened**, and it took six months and a plate in a field and about two minutes in a room with a machine in it. **The not-saying is now a practice and not an undertaking**, seventy-seven days after he swore it in a yard, and he has noticed that a man of about twenty-six put *practising* next to *collecting* in February and that he is not going to improve on it with his own arithmetic. **A half-filled box is the opposite of an exception.** **Custody is a thing a person holds.** **A day with nothing in it because a man said a list is a thing you read is not a pair.** **The asking has already happened the moment you write the sentence down**, restated, and he is not writing it a fifth time.
- **The clue chain: not touched and not advanced, and no fifth refusal.** The three Volume 01 clues appear in no chapter of this block as a document and are joined to nothing. The second tin is not a fourth instance. Two documents still carry a pair of marks and not three; three references exist and are references and not pairs; Kest has not matched the strings; and a fold is still not a chronology. **Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–110.** No fifth sighting of the works-jacket man and **no fifth refusal of it by anybody**, and there are still three things under the brick. The run number is one step further and no further.
- **The Lark Street grievance is not named in Chapters 101–110, and is not resolved and not softened and not referred to.**
- **How he ends the block, in his own hand:** *I am not going to be the author of the back length's maintenance, and I have not been for seven months, and the writer of the map is not the maintainer of anything, and that is a list of other people and there are about fourteen of them and not one of them has been asked to be on it, and the reason I have it is that I could not think of a way to do anything else.*

### Mara Quill

- **Where she is:** a corridor, a room, and a corridor. A two-year note, an appeal in a line not stayed, no preferred access, twenty-six names in a Crown Heights file, a minuted refusal to identify a document in her custody, a nine-minute letter answered and refused in full, and **a second letter written on the eighteenth of February and not sent, which is not mentioned in these ten chapters and remains unsent.**
- **What the custody register page did in this block: nothing, for the fourth time, and for the fourth consecutive block.** It is in a drawer with a coat in it. **She did not produce it, did not name it, did not confess to taking it, and it is not mentioned in dialogue in any of the ten chapters. He did not ask, and he has now not asked her about the seventh of January five times in a fortnight, and he has written down that he has stopped keeping a column of it and started keeping a number, and that a number is a column.**
- **What she did in this block, and it is all work, and it is the whole of her.** *One.* In a corridor at ten past ten she asked him whether he was going to speak, and he said no, and she did not argue, and she gave him three conditions — **do not put a number on it, do not put a reference on it, and do not improve it afterwards in a yard, because I will be in that yard** — and he said she would not be able to, and she said **I would be able to, and that is not a threat, it is a statement about what an advocate is, and I have watched three good sentences ruined by a man who could not leave them alone, and I am not going to be the fourth thing that happens to yours.** *Two.* She let the reporting officer ask the party the question and did not answer it for the party, and the party answered it, and **the whole of what six months bought is in paragraph four of a six-paragraph minute.** *Three.* In a corridor on the twenty-third, in about eleven seconds, she found the escalation: **a matter is adjourned by a chair in a room on a minute, and a thing does not lose its date by being left off a list**, and then the finding about the board and the envelope and three days.
- **What she was not told, and the test he set himself, and the fact that he failed it.** **He did not tell her about the season before the hearing, and he wrote in a book in a coat that if he went on not telling her after the fourteenth then it was not a rule. On the fourteenth he did not tell her in a corridor at ten past ten, or in a room at about eleven, or at about half past one when a line with a condition in it was read out.
