He wrote it down at the top of the entry of the twenty-first of April, in the imperative, and has written in Chapter 110 that a thing you do quietly is a thing that is going to get done, which is a woman of about fifty-four's sentence from a yard in this city in the middle of the winter, and that being right about it four times is not the same as being a person somebody can plan a Tuesday with.** **She is not named as knowing and he is not named as not knowing, and the reader is given the fact and not the interpretation.**
- **Nothing romantic has happened and nothing is going to. No kiss, no declaration, no jealousy scene, no rescue.** The Lark Street business was not raised in these ten chapters and she neither raised it nor softened it, and he did not either. What exists between them is procedural: three conditions in a corridor, a question asked of a party and not answered by her, and eleven seconds about a board.

### Hettie Brann

- **Where she is:** the water clinic counter, nineteen years on it. **The tally is her paper and not her apron pocket, and it has nine lines on it and she has not read it.**
- **What she did in this block, and it is the changed method and the recovery and it is the third piece of paper.** **She said the count out loud, in a yard, on a Wednesday morning at half past six, in front of about nine people: *It is three.*** Under the water clinic counter there is a January paper about a man with no rateable unit, a March sheet of nine doors that belongs to a nurse of twenty-three, and **a form about a hearing that was not determined, with a seventh column with a heading in it and two lines in it, one of which has a heading in the seventh column and one of which does not** — put there face down on Monday the nineteenth by a works clerk who did not ask. She refused to take it to the works; she said **if a surveyor of about thirty-one is standing in a yard when a woman of sixty-one puts a third piece of paper under a counter then that piece of paper is now about a surveyor, and it will be in a yard in about four years, and somebody will ask a question about it that is a question about him** — and she said she would hand it back in front of everybody if he came. **She chose a Wednesday to say it, because there is no coffee and about nine of them are there anyway.** She gave back Ola Petrossian's second list of nine doors and said **a thing somebody else made is not mine to keep, and I have worked that out in about four seconds and I would like it noticed that I did not have to work it out at all, and that is what a counter is.** And on Tuesday the twenty-first she read the second half of her own rule out loud for the first time — **it is not about four people, it is about a hand** — and told him what the token in his coat is, and what the sentence about the coat means, and he has written that he has not written down what he did about it.
- **What she has not been asked to be and will not be:** the door-check, the reader, the person who holds a mark overnight, the person who knows what is written on four pieces of paper, **and the person who writes a route home in a book of somebody else's.** She promised in front of about eleven people on the thirty-first of March not to make a third paper without saying so out loud and **she said so out loud, twice, and a later batch may not make a fourth without her saying so out loud first.**

### Nell Crale

- **Where she is:** four rooms over a shop on Bridge Street low end, six stops, and a cellar. Sixty-three, twenty-two years of a milk-and-ice round in two baskets on a hand barrow.
- **What she did in this block.** **She found a settlement plate in the grass, in passing, on Monday the sixth of April at about ten past ten, while a man with a chain was putting a peg back in a boundary because the March returns had not been signed.** She said *that plate is in the grass* and then, nine words that are the finding: **then nobody ever knows it was off, which is what a plate is for.** And then, after about two minutes of thinking: **you have got a chain and I have got a barrow and neither of us can get over that fence, and there is no ladder in this street that anybody has a key to, and that is not a reason, that is a Tuesday.** She is not a surveyor, she was not looking for it, and it did not occur to her that it might be one of the eleven.
- **What has not been refunded:** a cellar with six inches of silt and a frost that has not gone out in nine years, and the works' answer is November.

### Ola Petrossian

- **Twenty-three, four months in the job.** **On Sunday the fifth of April she went into eleven streets for the second time, four hours, in her own time, unpaid, and came back with nine doors and no names, in a bag, on a clinic form.** **The ninth door is still the side door on a store that is shut, and she said in advance that if she ever writes it a third time somebody is to stop her, and nobody has been told she is going to and that is on purpose.** She handed the list back to the counter and it stayed in her bag, because **a thing somebody else made is not mine to keep.** She has still never been thanked and is not going to be asked to stop.
- **She is not a device and she has no power and she is not a device for this block's answer** — the answer came from the fishmonger, in nine words, in a doorway.

### Cyril Sale

- **Where he is:** the quay section office, four rooms above a fitter's shop, forty-six, four years in the job.
- **What he did in this block, twice, and both times about a box.** *One.* **A FORM 4 query came back down on Thursday the ninth of April, a fortnight after the twenty-sixth of March, exactly as he predicted it would** — and it had **stopped asking about the sentence and started asking about the box**: *Return received. Item 4 not completed. Section to complete.* **It went up empty with a second ruled line and a second date, because the honest answer is yes, the answer that keeps eleven men out of a room is no, and there is no column on that form for what a sentence meant, and in about four years somebody will read two ruled lines on a return and will not be able to tell whether the clerk refused twice or whether the section ran out of answers.** And the standing finding: **a machine is a much worse thing than a man about being difficult, because a man stops and a machine does not.** *Two.* **He diagnosed the surveyor's fifth seventh column before he got to the bottom of the page** and gave him the finding that the volume turns on: **a column that wants to know why wants a person to put their name against the why, and there is no name on the page, and in about four years that name is a woman of thirty-one who drafted the paragraph that caused the third matter.** *Three.* He **wrote a sentence on the back of his own form on the sixth of April, with his name and the date, about an empty condition column, and put it in the correspondence tray and not a drawer, and said out loud that he has now done that three times since the middle of January and that he has a rule about it which is a number and not a sentence and which he will not write down, and which Milo Rook did not ask him for and did not push.**
- **The sixth line of the fourteenth of January is still in a file in another body's building, there is still no copy, the district still has no standing, and Milo Rook still does not know what is in it and nobody in this block tells him.** **Nobody has ever been asked about line five**, and the nil has now survived being read, a query on the line above it, a second query, and a defect report that could not be made.

### Roy Pryce

- **Where he is:** the quay section, fifty-four, thirty-one years, four lengths, and a cover that has stopped rocking and cannot be recorded as having stopped.
- **What he did in this block.** **He found that the condition column was empty on the sixth of April, wrote the three words he has written since the second of February, and then took them out with the flat of his thumb and squared the sheet on his knee** — and his reason is four sentences and it is not about a drain: **I did not do it… a foreman of thirty-one years walked past a thing that had stopped happening for four weeks and did not notice, and that is what the difference is between a hazard and a place.** And: **the column is empty and that is the correct entry and there is no column on this sheet that wants to know why a column is empty.** **The cover is flush, nobody reset it, and in about eleven years the rack is cleared and a person reading the sheet will see a length with a fault on the second of February and a fault on the twelfth and then nothing at all, and they will have to guess, and there is no right answer, because the honest answer is that it stopped and nobody wrote down when or why or who was there.**
- **He still has not written one word about a person on a form, and he still refuses to sign a line he has not read, and in about four months a man in an office is still going to ask him what he saw.** The nine words in the hours column are still nine words and are not re-counted in this block.

### Doreen Sankey

- **Where he is:** two rooms behind a boatyard on the Ferry Road side, forty-one, **on no list at all, and it did not change in this block.**
- **What he did in this block, and it is the batch's institutional centre and it is two sentences.** *One*, in a yard on the twenty-third: **in March that sentence was the best thing anybody had said about a piece of paper in five months and it was about a list giving us a chair; in April the same sentence is about a list taking the chair away, and nobody has sent for the chair, and nobody has taken it, and there is no form on which a chair goes back** — and **it was never ours, it was a number in a column in a hearing, and the thing I did not know on the thirty-first of March is that a number in a column can go down as well as up, and nobody has to do anything for it to go down, and the department has not done anything and has not had to.** And: **we did not use the nine words; we used them to work out what the chair was for, and it took us four months.** *Two*, in a room above a canteen on Parcel Street on the twenty-fourth: **he asked a question nobody sent him, because about four times a day somebody looks at a job and decides it is not theirs, and the rule about the card under a brick worked, and the person who acted on it was him and not the surveyor.** And the sentence that is the standing condition of the whole method: **I want it recorded, in about four years, by somebody who was not here, that a list gave this district a chair on the thirty-first of March and took it away on the twenty-third of April, and that nobody did anything, and that the reason nobody did anything is that there is no form for it. I have no power, no mark, and no name on any list, and I would like the four times I have told this district the condition of its own method to have added up to a sentence that somebody else has to read.**
- **He has no power, no mark, no name on any list, and he has told this district the standing condition of its own method four times now, for free and to no schedule.** He has not been thanked and does not want to be.

### The man of about fifty in the drainage section

- **Where he is:** a room in Crown Heights, **about fifty, fifty-one next month, nineteen years in it, no name on the page, and a later batch may not invent one.**
- **What he did in this block.** **He did not reduce the four in the river core and he did not reduce the zero against Mireward, and there is not one line in the event column in any of the five rows of the sheet for the week commencing Monday the twentieth of April, which came off the machine on Friday the seventeenth.** **And a surveyor of about thirty said the first correct thing anybody in that room has said about the second column since the twenty-sixth of January, and he asked that it be written down that the man did nothing with it, and nothing was written down and about four people noticed.** His sentence, which is the batch's finding about a column: **that is a forecast, and a forecast does not come down because a thing happened, and the four is a forecast.** The line under *allocated to* that he filled in three times in March was not filled in again. The line under *officer responsible* is still empty after nineteen years. **Mireward carries no new nil and there is not going to be a fourth.**
- **Everything that goes wrong through him in this block is attributable to a form, a machine, a list a hundred and forty years old, a column nobody has ever filled in, a forecast and a week — and not to him.**

### The fishmonger of about forty-five

- **She said the answer to the volume on Wednesday the twenty-second of April at about ten past six in the morning, in a doorway, in about nine seconds, and it is nine words: *Nobody keeps this row.*** And the rest of it: **that is what it is; if somebody kept it there would be a book and a column and her name in it and the other four of them a line under hers, and then somebody could ask her what she did and she would have to say why, and there is no why in it, and there has never been a why in it, and she does it because she does it and has never once in nineteen years thought about doing it** — and **it is kept by nine people doing it and not writing it down, and you cannot have a book of that, because the moment you have a book of it somebody is keeping it, and then it is not nine people, it is one person with a book and nine people who have been asked.** And: *I have said it is not for anybody five times. It is not for a man in an office. It is not for a form. It is not for anybody and that is not modesty, it is the arrangement.*
- **She is still not named, still on no list, has not given her name to anybody, and has not been thanked. She said *it is not for anybody* five times and this is the fifth and the fullest. Milo Rook has written down four times that he has not asked for her name and in Chapter 110 states that he is not going to ask in April or in May or in the fourth year.** She also said out loud on Wednesday the nineteenth of March that twenty-eight words in a row at the foot of her side is a line and nothing under it and nothing over it and it does not tell anybody they have to do anything — and she refused again to add a second paragraph to her own rule.

### The man of about forty who sweeps the Row

- **He swept a hundred and ten yards of frontage that is not a street at about ten past six on Wednesday the twenty-second of April, as he has since he was twenty-one, and there was no coffee on that row and there never is on a Wednesday, and about nine people were standing on it anyway.** He has not been thanked, is not on any list, and has not been given an initial. **He has still not told the coffee man anything and has still said why, and his sentence from March stands and was not repeated: he has never once told him what time he is out there and he is not going to start by telling him about a piece of paper.** In this block he is a person who was there and said nothing and is named in no document. **A later batch may not give him a name and may not give him an initial.**

### Bettina Sallow

- **Where he is:** the ninth desk on the left in the Survey School's repository beneath Crown Heights, fifty-four, four years, a stair of thirty-four steps, nine desks on the left, a plan chest, a window that does not open and a chair under it.
- **What she did in this block, and it is the batch's working day and it is not in a yard.** **She walked a surveyor round about nine requests, and the ordinary thing she does four times a day, and has never been thanked for, is that she will not tell you a document does not exist unless she has read the whole card, and she will tell you what she looked at, how far she went and what she did not open.** And the volume's structural thesis in a reader's mouth: **every card in this room is a place, and a hand index is built out of one card for one named thing, and the named things in it are rateable things with a street on them, and there is no card in it for a drain, a cover, a length, a row of shops, a fence, a woman, a market, or a hole, and the building is two hundred years old and the street list is a hundred and forty** — and **the index is not a record of what happened, it is a record of what is where**, and a card in an index is a claim and not the document. **And the same sentence in the mouth of the index clerk of that same room, said in December in other words, standing up, to nine people, with her one of the nine, and the word *claim* in both: the two of them have never said a word to the other one, and their one exchange was nine words she wrote on a finding aid on the eighteenth of December and turned round to him and he read it and said nothing. Milo Rook has heard both halves, has not joined them, and did not tell her.** And the broom: **nobody sweeps this room; there is a broom in that office since the twelfth of March and she has not used it, because the day she sweeps under the plan chest she is the person who sweeps that room, and then somebody in about four years can ask her what she did in there, and she will have to say why, and there is no why in it.** **She is registered from Volume 02 Batch 0001, has no power, is not a device, has not been thanked, and Milo Rook has not asked her for a document since December, and has been in that room four times since the seventeenth of December, and told her nothing and been told nothing.**

### Barrow

- **Timetable division clerk, nineteen years in the job, a list of nine items in front of him, no first name and not going to get one. Registered from Volume 01.**
- **What he did in this block: he was asked one question on Friday the twenty-fourth of April, by a man of forty-one on no list at all, and he asked whether he had been sent, and was told that nobody sends anybody, and he then said one thing in about forty seconds and went back to his nine items.** *A column for run number and a column for depot move, and they have never been the same column; the occurrence in the run column is the 07:52 booked working off Crown Heights on the morning of Tuesday the sixteenth of December, which is the car that went round; the other occurrence is a light engine and a two-car set going to a gate, and it is not it, and I am telling you which one so that you can stop.* And the standing limit: **in nineteen years he has never once seen a crew entered in the run column for a morning when the car did not go out, and if a car does not go out the column is empty and the row is not there, because a crew that does not work does not happen.** And the price he named for being asked: **you are asking me about a morning in December and I am a clerk and there is a thing in this city that does that to people and I have watched it happen to about four of them since 1971, and the four of them were not in this building, and I am not going to be a fifth of anything** — and the standing refusal, which is a rule for later batches: **ask nobody, and do not come here, and do not send the man from the round, and if you do any of those three things then I will say that the timetable division does not discuss working timetables outside the department, and it will be true, and it will be in a book, and it will keep.**

### Cass Ardery, Ivo Sarratt, Lena Marr, and the woman of sixty on the second landing

- **Ivo Sarratt** — Survey Enforcement, fifty-one, twelve years, **the reporting officer on the fourteenth and the person in the room.** He read the list of the business of the day aloud because no document in the city would say what was being heard; **he named the condition in his own line and said it is not his and that it is a thing a department should not have to say in a room; he said he has no further date to give, that it is not a decision, that he has not got the standing to make it one, and that a department does not set a date for a matter it cannot hear; and he asked for the distinction between *stood over* and *adjourned* to be minuted, and the minute will not distinguish them, and he asked anyway.** He is not a friend, not an ally, and nobody was added above him.
- **Cass Ardery** — second-in-command, **not the reporting officer**, and he says one nine-word sentence in a corridor: *You asked for a date. There is not one. I am sorry.* He asked for the adjournment date to be minuted in January and it was, and the price of asking for it was the chair and no date.
- **Lena Marr** — the only person in eleven streets who can be asked rule 14's question. **On the fourteenth she said *yes* in about four seconds, twice, and said the price out loud in the record, and asked for it to be minuted and said she knows exactly what the minute will do with it. She is the person who drafted the prohibition that is stopping a woman of sixty from her own name, and she said so in a room.** On Wednesday the eighth she went up one flight and **could not repeat the twenty-three words word for word, and the condition she set was word for word or not at all, so she did not do it.** The levy is unchanged: two and four a head a week, in advance, week by week, with no term, charged to her name and not to the district, for two hundred and fifty-two people in a building with no number.
- **The woman of sixty on the second landing** — the ninth of twenty-six names in a Crown Heights file, unnamed, **not in the room, not asked, and the ninth of twenty-six is the one Lena Marr has told the fewest people about and told first.** She cannot see the file, correct it, come off it, or ask whether her name is spelled right, and **nobody went up three flights of stairs about her and nobody is going to.**
- **Lena Marr is not Ada Fenn and the two sixty-eights in this district are not the same person.** **A woman of sixty-eight on nothing is referred to twice in this batch, in Chapter 103 and in Chapter 110, both declared, and the two are the seventh and the eighth across eight chapters of this volume, and the state layer records the count.** The woman on the fourth floor of Cedar Court is not mentioned in these ten chapters at all, and the woman of sixty on the second landing of a building with no number is a different and unnamed resident, and Ada Fenn is on the third floor, and **her name is not written once in Chapters 101–110 and she is not thanked, argued back, put on anything or solved.**

### The remaining people, and what did not happen to them

- **Pell Harrow** — **on the round, out at eight and in at four, and not named in any of these ten chapters. Tern is still not found, the list in the tin is not a finding, Ostrow confirms nothing, and no chapter in this block rescued anybody or asked anything about the twenty-two days up the south.** His card under the brick is blank and has been since the nineteenth of January and he has not taken it and nobody has. **The three things under that brick are still three.** His mark still holds for a working shift and not overnight and was not used and is not going to be. He is not Milo's assistant and he has no power.
- **Iona Sen as a named speaker, Jo Lask, Tomas Rusk, Owen Fitch, old Mabb, Nessa Bray, Verrick Assal, the woman of fifty-four, Ivy Marsden, Hester Coombe, Corin Mabey, Percy Doak, Fenwick, Amos Trench, Dilley Farr, the boatyard owner, the woman at the door of the Board school, and the man of about fifty in the works jacket** — **none of them is named in these ten chapters except Dilley Farr's non-appearance, and the man in the works jacket is not seen once and nobody has been asked to look and there is no fifth sighting and no fifth refusal.** **Ivy Marsden is not told anything, the page with her name struck through it is not read, not removed and not mentioned, and Milo Rook did not go near the north end of Saltmarket Row in this block and has written down that he did not and has not written down why.** **Hester Coombe is not named and not seen in these ten chapters; the card under the brick carries a third line and its writer is a clerk in a timetable division and not her and not him.** **Owen Fitch's name is still on five copies of a sheet of paper with a floor that is not his and has not been taken off any of them.** Old Mabb is a clause in Chapter 110's list and is not named and is not woken. **A foreman of fifty-four is still going to be asked in about four months what he saw, and a query box on the sixth line of a return is now open twice and has been asked twice and the box is empty.**
- **Orrin Sable, Halden Vey, Sel Iri and Tarin Kest** do not appear and are not named. **No new character has walked into a room in this batch, and the two people who had their scenes — Bettina Sallow and Barrow — were both registered before Volume 02 Batch 0001, and Nell Crale was registered in Volume 01.** **Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–110. No new grammar, faction, antagonist, final enemy, or fourth kind of place.**

# Volume 02, Batch 0006 — people whose standing changed on the page (Chapters 111–120)

**These are updates to the standing of people already registered. Nobody new is added to the series bible, no character acquires a device, and the cast list above is unchanged.** The full prose is in `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0111.md` … `chapter-0120.md`, and the batch's facts are in `state/batch-summaries.md` and `state/continuity.md`.

- **Milo Rook** — **Stage 2, begun and not complete, and he held nothing in Chapters 111–120.** He crossed nothing, so the tally is unchanged at **nine lines**, the remembered-route list is unchanged at **TEN**, none removed, all ten marked by somebody who is not him, and **it is ten and not eleven because he did not cross, and that is the first time in his working life he has been glad of a number and he does not trust being glad of a number.** **The written line home went on the twenty-fourth of November, which is day one, and is the hundred and seventy-ninth day of it on the twenty-first of May, blank, with FOUR marks in FOUR hands and none of them his, and the fourth was written on the first of April at a counter in about four seconds by a woman who was not asked. IT DID NOT MOVE. It did not come back and it did not not come back, nobody has been asked to fill it in, and on the eighteenth of May he deliberately stopped writing down the sentence that says it is there, having written it in seven of the previous eight entries, and he has written down that he stopped and has told nobody. The Lark token was cold throughout, was not taken out, and is the hundred and sixty-fifth day on the twenty-first of May; it was in a doorway two feet from a woman of thirty-four who did not know it was there, in a cabin, on a ramp, in a corridor about forty feet from a hearing room, on a length of reserved tram route and in a yard, and it did nothing every one of those times.** **His failure in this block is the sixth, and it is the same one, and the person who named the shape of it is not a clerk and is the person the form is about: Lena Marr, in a doorway at half past eight on the thirtieth of April, in nine seconds, off one sentence — *you have written a seventh column.* The object was a form headed OCCUPANCY — RESIDENTS, TEMPORARY ACCOMMODATION with a seventh column headed *why this person is not on the district's schedule* and one line in it, about a hundred and ten words, every word of it true, about a woman of sixty on a second landing whose name he does not know and whom he cannot name because the file cannot and only one person in the city can.** The resolution is new and it is the worst of the six: **he did not give it to a counter and he did not put it in a coat and he did not put it on a table in the rain; he folded it in at the back of his own Field Book with the thirtieth of April written on the fold in his own hand, and a woman of thirty-four told him in a doorway to do exactly that and not to do it again, and there are now SEVEN resolutions, and the seventh, written on the twenty-first of May, is the first one he has made on purpose about a thing that was going to happen.** The six resolutions in order are registered at `state/continuity.md` item 8. **The changed method and the recovery are both somebody else's work and neither of them is a word: Corin Mabey's, and the method is one sentence — the route is the doors, and the sheet is a print of the man who has the route, and a print cannot go out and come back — and the recovery is that a man who cannot talk is the only person in this district he can sit next to for a whole working day without putting anybody in a column, and it cost him nothing and he does not trust it.** **He is not cured, the page is not a form about a person, and the diagnosis stands: the seventh column is the only thing he is good at, he gets to be good at it about four times a year, and every time he gets to be good at it somebody else has to hold the paper.** **And the batch's other arc, which is new and is the first time in seven months he has done anything on purpose: he wrote an entry in his own book on every one of the twenty-six nights from the twenty-sixth of April to the twenty-first of May, so that every interval since the twenty-sixth is a single day, so that not one of them is a pair, so that the list has not moved from TWENTY-SEVEN in twenty-six days — and he has written down that a book with a night in it is one night closer to a record, and that the district's own second side has five correct blanks in it, and that the two instruments cannot see each other, and that he has not drawn a line between them and has not written down why.**

- **Mara Quill** — **milestone 2, and the step is the line, used for the first time, and neither of them says what it is.** She appears twice, both work: **a corridor at half past ten on Monday the fourth of May, nine words and then six minutes of not saying any more about it — *nobody has to answer that, and nobody has to not answer it either, and that is the new part… in January the protection was ours, we asked for it, we knew what it was for, and it cost a woman of sixty from her own name and we paid that on the fourteenth in front of thirty people. A letter about a car does not know we exist… this is the first time anybody has used it and I do not know yet whether it is the shield or the gag*** — and then *I think it has always been both and that January was the first time it cost anything, and I have never said that to you before and I am saying it now, and I would like you to notice that I am saying it in a corridor on a Monday morning about a letter and not in a room about a paragraph, and that is the whole difference between the two.* And then: **he told her he had not told her about the season, and she said *I am not going to ask you about it,* and it is three weeks since the fourteenth of April and it is the sixth consecutive block of not telling her, and she knows. And she said the thing that is on the page: *I have a page in a drawer with a coat in it and I have not produced it for four consecutive months and you have not asked, and I have noticed the count from my side and I have not mentioned it, and we are two people keeping a number about each other and the number is the same and neither of us has said it out loud, and that is the most intimate thing that has happened between us and it is a document neither of us has mentioned. Do not improve on that in a yard.*** **The custody register page is still in a drawer with a coat in it, is not produced, not named, not confessed to, and not mentioned in dialogue in any of the ten chapters, and he did not ask, and he is forbidden to ask. Her second letter remains unsent and is not mentioned. Nothing romantic has happened and nothing is going to. No kiss, no declaration, no jealousy scene, no rescue. The Lark Street grievance is not named, not resolved, not softened and not referred to in any of these ten chapters.**

- **Hettie Brann** — **nineteen years on a counter, and this block is hers in four ways, and one of them is a refusal to name a thing.** **She said the second half of her own rule out loud for the first time and the whole of it is the sixth column's problem: *the second half is the whole of it, and the second half is the only part of it that is about this envelope, and I have watched you take the first half of it four times and leave the second half four times.*** She ran the **fourth and last comparison of the four return-route copies on Monday the fourth of May, which was the first working day of the month, because the third of May is a Sunday**, and three of the four say version one and one says version two and the fourth has been read and will not be described, and **the number on the outside of the envelope still reads 1 of 4. — 1.iv. in pencil in her own hand and the fact that changed this month is that she did not rewrite it — she has changed it every month since the nineteenth of January and on the fourth of May she did not, and the corner of that envelope is four weeks old and says the first of April on a piece of paper that was last handled on Monday** — and she is not going to say why and is not going to write anything next to it and has now said *I wanted to write something else next to it* three times and will not say it a fourth. And she told him in nine words that the number is the only part of it that is the same and the rest of it is a different piece of paper every month. And she refused to name the thing he has been doing for six months: **you have spent a day not asking him, and six months not asking about four people whose names you do not know, and four months not asking a question about a plate in a field, and three weeks not asking me what is in an envelope, and five months not asking a man of sixty whether he is at a shed on a Wednesday… you have got very good at it in six months and you have not once written down what it is, and I am not going to give it a name, because the moment I give it a name it is a thing in a book and I have got four returns and one counter.** She is still not going to be thanked, she reads none of it, and **the three pieces of paper under her counter are still three and no fourth has been made and the promise of the thirty-first of March stands.**

- **Lena Marr** — **the district's standing position, and she is the person who names the shape of the block's failure, and it is the sixth resolution's origin, and she is not a clerk and has never been one.** **On Thursday the thirtieth of April, at about half past eight, in a doorway, in about nine seconds, off one sentence — *you have written a seventh column* — she told a man of thirty-one four things: that she knows what it is about and is not going to ask; that he has twenty-six names and has not written them down, on purpose, for about four years; that he has spent seven months finding that out about a plate in a field and has never once applied it to a piece of paper in his own coat; and that the only custody in this city that cannot be produced is a head.** The sentence is hers and it is the largest single idea in this batch and it is the only one of the batch's ideas that came from a party: **a head is the only repository in this city that cannot be produced against anybody — you can send for a tin, a counter, a coat and a man in it; you cannot send for what I am thinking about at eleven o'clock at night, and that is not because it is hidden, it is because it was never written, and a thing that was never written is the only kind of thing in this city that nobody can take off me, including the twenty-six, including the woman on the second landing, including the one I drafted the paragraph for on the fourth of December past.** And the price, said once and not improved: **she has been on paper since the fifteenth of December and not in anybody's head since the day before that, and that is the whole of the price.** And she gave him his resolution and told him it was the last time: **fold it in at the back of your own book, put the date on the fold in your own hand, and do not take it out again, and that is the last time anybody in this district is going to be able to say a thing to you about a piece of paper and be right, because I am not going to do it again either.** She is still the only person who can be asked rule 14's question, still the only person who knows all twenty-six names, and still deliberately not writing them down. **She did not go up the stairs in these ten chapters, the woman of sixty was not in a room and was not asked, the levy is unchanged at two and four a head a week in advance week by week with no term, and nothing in this block was paid again by either of them.**

- **Pell Harrow** — **seventeen, a courier, on no list, and in this block he refuses correctly and says why, and the cost that is not curable is his.** At half past three on Monday the fourth of May, at a gate in a high wall, **he refused to carry a four-page letter from a tram authority to Crown Heights, in about nine seconds, and he was right: the moment a courier is on a thing, the courier is the record… in about four years there is a piece of paper that says a boy carried it, and the boy is on the piece of paper, and the boy is seventeen now and he will be forty in that year, and the boy cannot get out from under a piece of paper about a morning by being forty.** And: **I would say it, and then it would be true, and it would be the first true thing anybody has said about that morning since the Friday in December and it would be about me.** And he named the third of the three things in the timetable division's standing rule: **somebody wrote that rule for a reason and the reason is a boy with a satchel, and I have been the boy with a satchel for two years, and I am not going to be the reason a rule holds in a different direction in the year I am nineteen.** **The standing rule held and was not tested: nobody sent him, nobody sent anybody, ask nobody and do not come here and do not send the man from the round, and a rule holds by not being tested, and that sentence is new in these ten chapters and it is his adjacent.** **THE COST, AND IT IS NOT CURABLE: the round is one man short and it has been one man short since the twenty-eighth of December, and there are two reasons and they are not joined. The first is the twenty-two days up the south on his own money and nobody asked and nobody is going to, and that is a refusal made by a man of forty-one in about four seconds in January and it has held four months. The second is that a round is carried by a postman and a postman is on a form and the form wants a depot and a depot is an address, and a district with no rateable unit and no address cannot have a depot, and has not had one since the eleventh of October six years ago when a street name came off an index — so a boy of seventeen does seven stops in eleven streets on a Monday and a Thursday and takes a handcart through a gate in a high wall, and there is nobody to send with him, and there will be nobody in June and none in the fourth year, and it cannot be cured from inside the district, and it is not an injustice and nobody has done anything wrong, and it is a design.** **Tern is still not found. The list in the tin is not a finding, Ostrow confirms nothing, and the twenty-two days are still unasked and unsaid and no batch has made anybody ask him in order to report them, and a later batch may not. His card under the brick is still blank and has been since the nineteenth of January, it is one of three things under that brick, and nobody has taken it and nobody is going to. He has no power, his mark still holds for a working shift and not overnight, and it was not used and is not going to be.**

- **Nessa Bray** — **Line 3 conductor, thirty-eight, sixteen years on the run, and this block is her chapter and it is the first one she has had, and she refused the chapter before it started.** She said on a ramp at ten past seven in the morning, before anybody had a pen out: **I am not a symbol and I am not a survivor and I am not going to be called that. I am a conductor on line three and I have done it for sixteen years and there are about nine hundred people a day who get on my car and go somewhere because of a thing I say out loud in a particular order, and that is the whole of what I am and it is a job and it is a good one and I would like it left as a job.** **And the three asks she made in December still stand: the shed shut, the carbon in the drawer, the names not read out in a yard in the rain. All three were got in about nine seconds on a day in December and she has never thanked anybody for them and is not going to start, because a person who has thanked somebody is a person who has asked.** And she is the one who warned a man of thirty-one about a man of fifty-one: **do not try to make him talk, not today, not tomorrow, not because you have been in a car with him all day and it would be a kindness — he is working… and if you go in there looking for a thing to write down you will find it and it will not be his.** And she is the reason the batch's answer to the volume's third question exists: **the punch roll, about nine inches of paper in a wooden case, a hole through it for every person that gets on, the only thing in this city that knows how many people were on that car and it does not know who one of them is, and that is not a defect, and she said so before he could and he would have got it wrong.** And: **the instrument a person cannot produce is the thing a person produces about nine hundred times a day, and nobody in this city has ever asked her to make it because they already have it.** And the twentieth call is a siding, and there is no form in this city for a place a car goes to be looked at. **She has not been asked about the sixteenth of December, or the thirty-one, or anything, and she said in December that she is going to be asked by nine people whether it was the best day's work this district has ever done and she is going to say no, and nobody has asked her in these ten chapters.**

- **Corin Mabey** — **Line 3 driver, fifty-one, twenty-nine years, and this block is his chapter, and it is eleven hours and eleven words and none of them are his.** He came back with the car on the twenty-second of December and **has not been able to say a sentence since the Friday and is at home when he is not driving and is not hurt**, and that sentence is now on the page in a chapter and nobody has said it in a yard and nobody has needed to. He worked a figure of three out of Crown Heights on Friday the first of May, out at about ten past seven and in at about twenty past six, with a man of thirty-one in the off side of the second seat from the front, and **he looked at him once at the bottom of the ramp and once at the top of it and on neither occasion appeared to see anything remarkable.** He was not asked anything, about a printed sheet or about anything else, and nobody in this district sent anybody to ask him anything. **And the batch's recovery is his and it is not a fact and not a document: a man who cannot talk is the only person in this district a surveyor can sit next to for a whole working day without putting anybody in a column, and it cost nothing and it is not trusted.** **The seventh call is before the eighth on a Tuesday and after it on a Friday, and he has had the run for twenty-nine years, and he did not consult a printed sheet once and was not once wrong about anything that could be seen from the second seat.**

- **Verrick Assal** — **fifty-eight, on a wage, at the works compound, mending chain, with a spade he has still never used, and in this block he is the only person who gets anything right about the future, and he gets it right twice, unasked, from a paper he has not read.** **On Sunday the third of May, in a yard, unshown the letter, about ten past one: *you have got a date on it… The twenty-first of May. Then you have got eleven working days and you have not written anything and you are going to write something on about the nineteenth, and I am telling you now that you are going to write something on about the nineteenth, and I have told you a thing about yourself and I would like you to notice that I got it from nine words on a piece of paper I have not read.*** And the reason he is right: **a man who cannot answer rings the office and says it cannot be answered and writes three words, and the three words are a figure and not a lie, because the return asks for a number of persons carried and a number of persons carried is not a sentence, and *not stated* is not the same as *no response*, and there is about four words between them and I have had about eleven years to learn it and I have never had a use for it.** And then, unasked, the diagnosis of the block's failure in a sentence: **you are going to let a rule about one column stop me answering a different one.** **And he was wrong. On the twenty-first the man of thirty-one wrote nothing on the letter, and the item stands open, and the man of fifty-eight's prediction did not come true, and Milo Rook has written down that he was wrong and has not written down whether he is glad about it.** And on the seventh of May, in his own book, Milo Rook recorded that **a man of fifty-eight with a spade helped him and he does not know he helped me and he has never heard of either of them** — the recognition that Verrick's two predictions came from nine words about a piece of paper and that they were right about the shape of the thing and wrong about the date.

- **Cyril Sale** — **works clerk, forty-six, and in this block he says the sixth true sentence anybody in this district has said to him in seven months and he does not know it was the sixth.** **On Friday the eighth of May, in a corridor, four rooms above a fitter's shop, about eleven o'clock: *that column is the only one in this building that is allowed to be empty… it is the condition column. The other five columns are about what we did. That one is about what it was like. And nobody in this city has ever asked a gang what a length was like, and the day one of them does there is going to be a seventh column on that sheet with a heading in it and a man's name against it, and it will be my name or Roy's, and in about four years somebody will read it and be entitled to say we knew and did not say.*** He said it once and said he was not going to say it again. And then, on the same morning: **nobody has ever asked about line five in nine weeks and a thing that has never been asked about for nine weeks is not protected, it is just not yet asked about, and the only reason it has not been asked about is that a man in another building has been asking about a box on a different form, and that is all that is holding it, and it is not holding it, it is just in the way.** And the prediction, on the seventh: **you are going to write something on Monday the nineteenth, and it is going to be the best thing you have ever written, and it is not going to be a number of persons and it is not going to be a why… and I am going to read it in about four years and I am not going to be able to tell whether you refused or ran out of answers.** He was wrong about the best and right about the not-a-number. **The two queries on the annual return are unanswered, the box is empty with two ruled lines and two dates on it, the nil on the line above it has now gone eleven weeks without being asked about, the foreman has not read the sixth line and does not know the second query exists, and Milo Rook still does not know what is in the sixth line and nobody in this block told him.** His rule about writing on the back of a form is still a number and not a sentence and has still not been given to anybody.

- **Roy Pryce** — **foreman, fifty-four, thirty-one years, and in this block he is asked about the condition column for the first time and he is the one who says what an empty column is.** **On Monday the twenty-seventh of April, at about ten past ten, four sentences and a correction: the last walk anything went into that column was the twelfth of February and he has not read it once since, and he did not not-read it, and **reading it and not reading it are two different things and the difference is about a second and a half and the second and a half is not a decision anybody makes.** And then the second half: somebody will fill it in one day on a Tuesday in a wet week and it will be wrong, and in about four years somebody will read a sheet and see a fault on a Tuesday in May and a gap from February to May and be perfectly entitled to say nobody was watching, and **there is no column on this sheet that wants to know why a column is empty and I am not going to build one, and I have not built one in eleven weeks and there is no rule.** On Saturday the second of May, unasked, about a boundary: **the low end floods about twice in four years and the gutter is the only thing on that length that moves, and everything that moves gets set again by whatever moved it, and there is nobody out here to set it, and there has not been since the eleventh of October** — and then the standing condition of the district's own boundary, in nine words: **do not go over the fence, and not because I have told you not to. There is nobody on the far side to stop you and there has not been for six years, and that is exactly why you should not go, and I have not got a better reason than that and I am not going to look for one.** He refused to say how many of the surveyor's seventh columns there were and why, and the reason is that **a man who has been asked how many of them he has written will tell you, and then it is a number, and a number on one of them is worse than nothing on one of them.** **He is still going to be asked in about four months what he saw, and the reason in the hours column is still nine words, and nothing in these ten chapters re-counts it.**

- **The man of about sixty-three with the flag** — **the yard man, and in this block he is on the page in his own voice, unnamed, and he is the fifth thing in this district that cannot be shown to anybody, the only one of the five that is older than the fifth of January, and the first one nobody told the protagonist on purpose.** **He has never looked inside a car in twenty-two years and was not asked to and is not going to, and his book says the time a car came in and nothing else, and it was right at 16.41 and it would have been right at 16.36, and he said so in a sentence: *it is right because the times are the times.*** And the reason, in nine words, unasked, to a man with a pen out: **if I look inside a car then I know something about a car, and what I know about a car is that it came in, and the day I know that about one then somebody will want me to know that about the other kind, and there is no box anywhere in this yard for what I know and I would rather be the man with the flag.** And then the sentence that made the chapter: **there was a day in December that my book has an hour in that has not got anything behind it, and I have not asked about it and I am not going to, and my book is a book about times and not about cars, and the hour is in it and nothing is, and I have had five months to be asked and nobody has.** Nobody asked him. He is not the man of fifty-one with a machine in it, and he is not the man of about fifty in a works jacket, and he must not be confused with either.

- **Doreen Sankey** — **not named in these ten chapters**, and he appears once, unnamed, at about ten past five on Monday the fourth of May, at the gate, **looking at a job that was as nearly his as any job has ever been in this district and deciding in about four seconds that it was not his, and saying so out loud to nobody in nine words, for the fifth time: *a card under a brick is a thing and not a job. And about four times a day somebody in this district looks at a job and decides it is not theirs, and this week it was me, four times, and one of them was this gate, and I have not written any of them down and I am not going to and it is the only thing I am good at and I have had four months to get good at it and I have.*** He has no power, no mark, and no name on any list, and the standing rule about a job that is not yours held without anybody acting on it, and **the run number did not move in this fortnight and the reason is that a rule holds by not being tested, and he is the reason it was not tested and he was not asked to do anything.**

- **Nell Crale** — **not named in these ten chapters.** She is in a yard in Chapter 111 and asks the only question anybody asks there, and gets the answer that nothing is going to happen on Thursday either, and it is the chapter's last spoken line: *you are all talking about a machine and none of you have said what happens on Thursday.* She is registered and her six stops and her week in her head and her refusal of a machine all stand and none of them is refunded.

- **Rue** — **she is in a yard twice in these ten chapters, at a quarter to ten on the twenty-seventh of April and on the Friday the fourth of May, and she is the one who asks what happens on Thursday, and the answer is that this district is now in the position of a thing being maintained by machinery that nobody is maintaining and the honest name for that position is a length, and he has written that down three times and is not going to write it a fourth.** She is not thanked and is not on any list and nothing about her is refunded.

- **Ivo Sarratt** — **not in these ten chapters and not named, and the matter is not on the business of the fifth of May, and nothing in this block has him learning that the season ended on the same day as the hearing, and nothing has either office learning of the other.** His line of the twelfth of January stands, was used once for the first time in five months, and was not removed, not varied, and did not lapse.

- **Cass Ardery** — **not in these ten chapters and not named**, and nobody was added above him, the office of the Prefect, or a hearing panel.

- **Barrow** — **not in these ten chapters, not named, not asked anything, and the timetable division's standing refusal was not tested and not broken.** The three things it says stand exactly where they stood on the twenty-fourth of April: ask nobody, do not come here, do not send the man from the round. **The run number has not moved in this fortnight. The card under a brick carries the same three lines it carried on the twenty-fifth of April, the line on the front has not been taken off, there is still no destination on it and no passenger's name and no thirty-one, and a rule holds by not being tested.**

- **Hester Coombe** — **not named in these ten chapters.** She is not named on the card under the brick, which is not produced and not moved, and the third line on the back of it is still the one a clerk in a timetable division wrote on the twenty-fourth of April.

- **The man in the works jacket** — **not seen once in these ten chapters, nobody was asked to look, there is no fifth sighting and no fifth refusal, and he is not a face and not a faction and not an antagonist and is not going to become one.** He has four sightings, the last on Thursday the nineteenth of February.

- **Ada Fenn** — **not named in Chapters 111–120 and her name is not written once in them, and THIS BATCH ADDS NO REFERENCE TO HER. The phrase is not used, in any register, in any of the ten chapters, and the addition is zero, so the count stands at eight across eight chapters of Volume 02 and is not nine.** She is on nothing, is not to be asked, was not asked, and is not thanked, argued back, put on anything or solved. **The woman on the fourth floor of Cedar Court is not mentioned in these ten chapters at all, so that the two sixty-eights cannot be folded, and the woman of sixty on the second landing is a different and unnamed resident of a building with no number and is the ninth of the twenty-six names and is not named either.** A woman of sixty-eight on nothing is a clause in Chapter 120's list only as a counted absence and the phrase itself is not written.

- **Ivy Marsden** — **not named, does not appear and is not told anything.** The page with her name struck through it is not read, not removed and not mentioned in any of the ten chapters. **Milo Rook did not go near the north end of Saltmarket Row in this fortnight and has not gone since the nineteenth of February, and the sentence about the beam scale with the poise off nothing is not in these chapters and was not written down then and is not written down now.**

- **The others, carried and not refunded** — **Tern is still not found and the list in the tin is not a finding. Owen Fitch's name is still on five copies of a sheet of paper with a floor that is not his. Old Mabb is asleep and is not named and is not woken. Tomas Rusk's name is on a drainage record forever and he will not swear. Jo Lask's hearing still has no date. The woman of fifty-four has a plot number and no bill in six years. The two school places lost in October are still lost. A woman of about thirty-four is still paying two and four a head a week, in advance, week by week, with no term, for two hundred and fifty-two people in a building with no number, and it is not curable. The two money totals, five hundred and fifty-four pounds and forty and forty-three pounds and two, are carried forward unchanged and neither is printed in these ten chapters and the multiplication is still never shown. Roy Pryce's reason in the hours column is still called nine words and is not re-counted anywhere in this batch.**

---

# Volume 02, Batch 0007 — the volume close, and people whose standing changed on the page (Chapters 121–130)

**These are updates to the standing of people already registered. Nobody new is added to the series bible, no character acquires a device, and the cast list above is unchanged.** The full prose is in `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0121.md` … `chapter-0130.md`, the batch's facts are in `state/continuity.md` under "What Chapters 121–130 fixed on the page," and **Volume 02 is CLOSED at Chapter 130.**

- **Milo Rook** — **Stage 2, begun and does not complete, and he held nothing and he crossed nothing in the last ten chapters of the volume.** The tally is unchanged at **nine lines** (the paper is Hettie Brann's and the pocket is Dr Sen's and he has not looked at it since the thirteenth of January and has written six times that he is not going to look at it in June). **The remembered-route list is unchanged at TEN, the original eight intact, none removed, all ten marked by somebody who is not him, and the reason it is ten and not eleven is that he did not cross.** The ninth crossing's failure to cost one is still unexplained and nothing in these ten chapters touches it. **THE BLANK DID NOT MOVE: the written line home went on the twenty-fourth of November and is the hundred and fifth day of it on Tuesday the sixteenth of June, blank, with four marks in four hands and none of them his, and the fourth was written on the first of April at a counter in about four seconds by a woman who was not asked, and it is a line somebody else can walk him home by and that is all it is. HE IS NOT CURED AND NOTHING IN THIS BLOCK CURES HIM, AND NOTHING IS GOING TO.** The Lark token was cold throughout, was never out of the inside pocket of his coat, and did nothing in a clinic and in a depot office at Alder Quay. **The two sensory losses are still two things with nothing on them, and the sound recurred once: on Tuesday the second of June, at about a quarter to ten, at a clinic, he did not hear a counter that has made that noise every day for nineteen years, and it was not there for about four seconds and it was there again and it did not come back, and no face and no place and no person and no name has ever come with it, and he has still not decided whether the two things are one or two.** He is **not** the author of anything in this district and did not become one: the gang's sheet is the gang's, the returns are the authority's, the working timetable is the division's, the carbon is the depot's, the margin is nobody's, the plate is nobody's, and the four characters under the brick are a crew and not a car. **What he refused:** he did not answer a return, twice; he did not tell the works about a plate, and said so once and has not said it again; he did not ask a question in a room in which he could have — nine minutes in a depot office and twenty minutes in the same office — and he has written down that he has not counted how many times he has decided not to be a fifth of anything and is not going to; he did not ask a foreman what he saw; he did not ask a man of about twenty-six anything; he did not join the head and the number and the fact. **What he cost:** a form about a round with eighty words in a seventh column is sitting face up on the top of his own table in his own office where anybody who walks in can pick it up, and that is the first of his seven resolutions that can be produced against him, and he chose it on purpose on the fourth of June, and nobody asked him to; and the decision about the plate is a decision and not a refusal, and a refusal cannot be produced and a decision can, and he is now, on purpose, a thing that can be produced against. **What he got that is new and is not a cure:** he did not count a foreman's words and noticed that he had not; he did not resolve a seventh column by hiding it; he did not write down that he was going to look at a page in a book and then looked at it. **The Field Book's pairs are TWENTY-SEVEN and did not move in fifty-two consecutive nights**, and he has written down that the not-looking for another way is not a method. **THE FINAL IMAGE, IN HIS OWN WORDS, AS HE WOULD PUT IT: *A destination that does not exist, and the passengers' names are real, and both of them are in the same book on the same page, and they got there the same way, which is that a column takes what it is given.*** And then, in full, the eight sentences that keep it from being anything else: **a destination that does not exist is a fact about a place and not about a person; there is nobody in it; nobody came back; the thirty-one went home on the afternoon of the twenty-second of December and they do not know and nobody is going to tell them; the names are real because thirty-one people got on a car and a hole went through nine inches of paper for every one of them and a second hand wrote the names down in the only place open and the two of those match; nobody inherits that page and there is no column in that book for who maintains it; it is not a remnant and not a place and not a length and not a row and not a boundary; the printed part is the false part and the handwritten part is the true part, and they are in the same book on the same page, and there is nobody coming to either of them, and that is the only thing I have got after seven months and it is not a rescue and it is not a comfort and it is not a haunting — it is a column that took what it was given.**

- **Mara Quill** — **milestone 2, one corridor on Monday the fifteenth of June, work only, and nothing romantic happened and nothing is going to.** **WHAT HAPPENED TO THE CUSTODY REGISTER PAGE: NOTHING. It is in a drawer with a coat in it, it has not been produced, it was not named, it was not confessed to, it was not mentioned in dialogue, and he did not ask, and he was not allowed to — and she volunteered, for the first time in six blocks, that she is not going to say anything else about it, not this week and not in June, and that it is not her being cold, and that she has stopped requiring anybody in this district to know things. She did not hand it over and she did not confess and she is not going to in this volume.** **WHETHER HE TOLD HER ABOUT THE SEASON: NO, AND HE TOLD HER HE HAD NOT, AND IT IS THE NINTH WEEK.** *It is the ninth week and you have not told me about the season and you are not going to and I am not going to ask, and I want that in a corridor in June in exactly the words I am using and not in a room and not in a minute.* And: **you are going to keep doing that. I am not going to ask you to stop and you are not going to stop and there is no column for either of us. That is the whole of what this is and it is not going to improve in a year and I have stopped waiting to see whether it does.** **He has not asked her about the seventh of January since the fifth of May and has stopped counting and started dating, which is worse, because a date is a column.** The two returns: he had both and gave her neither, and the reason is that she is a party to a proceeding four hundred yards from that office and **there is no column on a return for a party**; she said that was correct, and a very cold way to be correct, and **the first thing in six months he has done that she would not have done, and she is not going to be warm about it in a corridor, and the warmth is not coming and the not-coming is not a punishment, it is a Tuesday.** **The Lark Street grievance is not named, not resolved, not softened and not referred to in any of the ten chapters. The line of the twelfth of January stands and its condition is not the department's, and she said in a corridor in May that she does not know whether it is the shield or the gag, and she has not softened that, and nothing in this block moved it.** Her knowledge of his private motive — the token and the custody column — is unresolved and unused: he did not tell her about the token, and she did not ask, and he does not know that she knows.

- **Pell Harrow** — **seventeen, a courier, on no list, and he is not in this block and he was not sent anywhere and he was not asked anything.** **TERN IS STILL NOT FOUND. The list in the tin is not a finding. Ostrow confirms nothing. His twenty-two days up the south ended on Monday the nineteenth of January and nobody has asked him and he said nothing and no batch has made anybody ask him in order to report it, and this block did not and may not.** **His own blank card is one of the three things under the brick and it has been blank since the nineteenth of January and nobody has taken it and nobody is going to.** The round is one man short and was one man short on Thursday the eleventh of June, when it was thirty-nine minutes late because a lorry was chained across the quay section gate at one o'clock; the two reasons for the shortage are still unjoined and are not joined in this block; **it cannot be cured from inside this district, and it is not paid by Lena Marr and it is not paid by the woman of sixty, and it is being paid at about ten minutes to five on a Thursday by a boy of seventeen with a handcart, and there is no column for it and the sheet is right.** He has no power of any kind, his mark still holds for a working shift and not overnight, and it was not used.

- **Dr Iona Sen** — **seam physician, the water clinic, and this is her working day and it is done well and it is the volume's method in a third pair of hands.** She reads across: two departments, two documents, both printed lists, both correct, four months apart, and she is the one who notices that they disagree. **THE FINDING IS HERS AND IT IS NOT IMPROVED AND IT IS NOT PUT IN A YARD: a list is not wrong because it disagrees with another list; a list is only wrong when somebody has to act on it and the other one is in a different building.** What she did about it: **one line in her own day book with no mark between the two halves, and she is not going to either of the two printing offices, because if she goes the clinic is a place somebody has asked about, and being asked about is the whole of what this district has been doing for six months.** **And she refused, correctly and without being asked, to ask her patient to find a lost sound, on the stated ground that a person who is asked to find a sound starts looking for it, and looking for it is a thing you do with your hands, and then you have got a routine, and a routine about a sound is the fourth thing.** She did not ask him to describe it, did not ask whether a face or a place was attached to it, and said *sit down, I have four things and one of them is your hand and it is not the hand you think it is.* **She has held the tally in her pocket since the thirteenth of January and has never been asked to look at it, and neither of them looked at it, and that is unchanged and it is not going to change in this volume.**

- **Hettie Brann** — **the clinic woman of sixty-one, nineteen years on a counter, and HER NAME IS HETTIE AND NOT HESTER, and this file said *Hester Brann* from the Batch 0007 state pass onward, which is the fusion CR-033 item 4 recorded and corrected out of Chapter 86 and which came back through the state layer one batch later; the manuscript has said Hettie in every one of Chapters 121–130 and so does this line now, and the two women are not to be folded again. Hester Coombe is a different and live character, fifty-one, at Alder Quay, twenty-two years in a depot office, and the two of them are never in a room. — nineteen years on a counter, and this block is hers in three ways and two of them are refusals that are correct.** **She refused a tram authority's return behind her counter in about nine seconds on Tuesday the ninth of June, and she was right, and the reason is hers: a document behind this counter makes this counter a repository, and a repository is a place, and there are three pieces of paper face down under it that were counted out loud in a yard in April, and if a return lives under that counter then the three are in a place with a return in it, and a thing in a place can be chased, and the second anybody in this city chases a return in this district somebody is going to sweep.** And then the finding, in front of four returns: **you left yours where it can be taken, I am not going to bring mine where it cannot, and those are not the same decision, and one of us is going to be wrong about it in about four years and I do not know which and I am not going to find out in a minute and a half.** She also said the standing thing about a round that was late — **the sheet is who does what and on which day, it is not a clock, and a thing that is not on the sheet is not a fault of the sheet** — and she read four returns out loud, standing up, because a person who has heard a figure read out in a room by somebody who was there when it was wrong can answer, and a person who has only been asked can only be asked. **And she said the thing that ends the return-route arrangement: there is not going to be a fifth comparison, the first working day of June was Monday the first and she did not do it and is not going to, and the number on the outside of the envelope has not moved and is not going to, and she has wanted to write something next to it four times and is not going to, and the fourth time she has stopped saying that out loud is the one she means.** The three pieces of paper under the counter are still three and have not been read.

- **Cyril Sale** — **works clerk, forty-six, and in this block he is the person who names what a form cannot do and the person who stops.** He named the seventh failure's shape in about eleven seconds and it was not about the seventh column: **a round carried by one man and a round carried by two return the same six lines, the return is not asking whether the round was enough, it is asking whether the work was done, and a shortage is not work undone, and in about four years somebody in a depot reads twelve perfect months and sees no shortage anywhere in them and is right and there is no column in that authority that will let him say the other thing.** And: **what is on the return about a boy of seventeen is his name once a week and a route through a gate, and a sentence in your handwriting on a form about a round is a document about a person, and he is seventeen and he is not going to be asked about it and he does not know it is on paper, and if it ever came to him he would not be able to get out from under it by being forty.** He read the two returns, said *it is a return*, said the half of it that is true, and **said out loud that he has had four chances to give a number and none was taken, and asked for that to be noticed by somebody other than him.** **And he is stopping: he has three sentences on the backs of three forms, with his name and the date, in the correspondence tray and not a drawer, and a document is a place a person can be asked about.** His rule about line five stands: **a thing that has never been asked about is not protected, it is just not yet asked about** — and it is now **twelve weeks** on the sixteenth of June, and it is not protected, and nobody has queried it, and nobody in this district is going to.

- **Roy Pryce** — **foreman, fifty-four, thirty-one years, and in this block he is SEVEN WEEKS past the four months and nobody has asked him and nobody is going to. He said it out loud on Thursday the eleventh of June in a yard, not on Wednesday the tenth: the yard scene was moved to the eleventh because the eleventh is a walk day and the round is a Monday and Thursday round and the whole of the block's cost is paid on a Thursday at about ten minutes to five. Four months off the twenty-second of December is about the twenty-second of April, and the eleventh of June is seven weeks after that and not two months, and he has had seven weeks to decide which of the two questions he is going to answer.** and nobody has asked him, and nobody in this district is going to ask him, and that has been done on purpose and it is the yard's doing and not his and not Milo's, and he has not given anybody that silence and it is not his to give.** *A thing that has never been asked about is not protected. It is just not yet asked about. And in about four years a man with a box is going to ask me, and I am going to have to say yes, and the yes is going to be about a length and not about a morning, and I have had two months to think about which of those two I am going to answer and I have not decided and I am not going to be asked about it either.* **And Milo Rook did not count the words.** His condition column is empty on the twentieth walk since the first of April and there is no column on that sheet that wants to know why a column is empty, and the standing reason — reading it and not reading it are two different things and the difference is about a second and a half and the second and a half is not a decision anybody makes — stands and is not improved.

- **Hester Coombe** — **depot clerk, Alder Quay, fifty-one, twenty-two years, and this block is her chapter and she is the person who moved the run number and she was not sent and was not asked.** She counts fares, goes down a carbon with a clerk's pencil, keeps the carbon in the second drawer, and has not got a second copy and says so. She wrote the card under the brick on the twenty-first of January, in her own hand, with a line at the foot: *To be looked up in the working timetable. Not before Friday. — H.C.* **In June she opened a bound working timetable on a shelf four feet from her own desk, in her own office, because a crew is a body of people and a body of people is booked to a working and not to a car, and the working has a destination, and she was booking a crew for a Monday — that was her job, and she said so before he asked, and she said on purpose that nobody has asked her about a morning in December and nobody was going to.** She found the row, read it out, and told him the destination is a place that is not on the diagram and has never been one. **She refused, correctly, four times over: she would not say what she thinks it is, would not look for it, would not answer one question about December, and would not say whose handwriting anything in the margin is — *I am not going to be the one who says so, and neither is the timetable division, and neither is the man from the round*.** **She is not a fifth of anything and has not been asked about a morning in December by anybody, and there is nothing in that room that anybody is entitled to, and there has not been since before he was born.**

- **Lena Marr** — **about thirty-four, the district's standing position, not a clerk and never one, and she is not in this block and none of her prices moved.** **Two and four a head a week, in advance, week by week, with no term, for two hundred and fifty-two people in a building with no number, and it is not curable.** She knows all twenty-six names and has deliberately not written them down for about four years. **The woman of sixty on the second landing is on paper and not in anybody's head and nobody in this district is going to tell her anything and she was not in the room on the fourteenth of April.** The round being one man short, and the condition column being empty for twelve weeks, and the plate, and the sentence about a head — **none of it was paid by her in these ten chapters and none of it was asked of her and she was not in a room.**

- **Nell Crale** — **sixty-three, Bridge Street, and in this block she is the person who gave the volume its shape and the person who took the last thing he gave up.** In a doorway on Monday the first of June, in one sentence, in answer to a question he did not ask: **a thing you will not name is a thing you will not go and look at, and that is the whole of what is wrong with you and it is not your handwriting.** And on Friday the twelfth of June, at about half past five, in a doorway, with a hand barrow: **somebody should tell the works that there is a plate off a fence** — and she said she was not asking him what it was, had not asked, and was not going to, and that this was the one thing she was after him about and it was not what it was, and that she was not going to stand in a doorway in the rain for it. **He did not tell the works. That is the last thing he gave up in six months and he gave it up on purpose, and nobody in this district knows that he has decided it, and the decision is a decision and not a refusal and a decision can be produced against a person.**

- **The man of forty-one on no list at all** — **on no list, no power, no mark, and in this block he is the person who names the shape of the seventh failure before the pencil is out, who declines a job in nine words for the seventh time, who says the whole of the round's cost out loud for the first time, and who is told a thing with no form because he is the only kind of person it can be said to.** *You have written a seventh column, and it is about a boy.* And then: ***seven.*** And: *that is seven of them and I have watched six and I have never once seen you learn it, and nobody in this district has.* He has been in that gate four times a day since the twenty-eighth of December. **He has also said, at a gate in this block, that there is a rule in this district that a rule holds by nobody testing it, and that he has got good at not testing it in about two months, and that he has told nobody and there is nobody to tell — and he did not do the thing at Alder Quay, did not know about it until the Monday night, and has not been told what is in the margin.**
