- **A man of about fifty in a room with a machine in it** — **fifty-one next month, nineteen years in the room, and he is in this block twice and he is unchanged.** He transferred five figures off the allocation sheet with a pen on the twenty-second of May and again in the ordinary Friday of the twelfth of June, and **the fourth column has been filled in three times in nineteen years and the line under the words *officer responsible* has never been filled in at all.** **There is still nothing printed in this city for the fifteenth of April, there has never been a second outlook, nobody has asked for one in writing in six months, and he cannot tell whether the next one does not exist or whether it exists and has not been sent, and he has not been asked and has not been able to say.**

- **Ivo Sarratt, Cass Ardery, the office of the First Surveyor** — **none of them is in these ten chapters and nobody was added above Ardery, the office of the Prefect, or a hearing panel, and the office of the First Surveyor is a working desk in an instrument and did not request, receive or act on anything.**

- **The people who carry Volume 01's prices into the close of the volume** — **and none of them is refunded and not one of them is mentioned in a way that improves any of them.** A mechanic of sixty-one on a drainage record forever who will not swear. A conductor of thirty in a printed rule book whose own hearing still has no date and who nobody has sent anything. A woman of fifty-four with a plot number and no bill in six years. Old Mabb asleep and not woken. **A woman of sixty-eight on nothing, referred to ZERO times in these ten chapters — the phrase is written six times only as a declared count, the count stands at EIGHT, and her name is not written once.** The woman on the fourth floor of Cedar Court, not mentioned, so that the two sixty-eights cannot be folded. Owen Fitch's name still on five copies of a sheet with a floor that is not his. Ivy Marsden, seventy-four, not told, with a page in a tin on a bottom shelf and a beam scale found on the nineteenth of February that was not written down. A woman of fifty-four, a plot number, no bill. Two children lost in October and still lost. Two money totals that disagree at the shilling and are not going to be made to agree. **And the four things in five months that cannot be shown to anybody, of which none became a record in these ten chapters, and there is no fifth.**

---

# Volume 03, Batch 0001 — people whose standing changed on the page (Chapters 131–140)

**These are updates to the standing of people already registered, plus four new working people who are all ordinary, are all named in `bible/characters.md` and `bible/terminology.md`, and none of whom is a device, a faction, an antagonist or a person who is thanked. No character acquires a power. POV is Milo Rook throughout. The batch's facts are in `state/continuity.md` under "What Chapters 131–140 fixed on the page," written from the manuscript.**

- **Milo Rook** — **thirty-one, Stage 2, begun and does not complete, and he stabilised nothing in these ten chapters.** **CROSSINGS NINE → TEN. REMEMBERED ROUTES TEN → ELEVEN.** The tenth was Thursday the twenty-fifth of June, in at 14.20 and out at 15.02, **forty-two minutes**, from the old ford in Mireward, with all four conditions of October said out loud to a drain: **Garn Ollerton walked him in and out and is not him; Sabra Trewin held the count in her head and is not a box; the time was stated before he went; and the clinic in the morning was Ola Petrossian, twenty-three, a nurse, on a bank from half past ten, unasked, on her day off, paid by a clinic with no record of where she went.** It cost one route, as every crossing has. **The ninth crossing's failure to cost one is still unexplained and he wrote in his own book on the twenty-fifth that he is not going to explain it before the end of July.** **The tally is TEN lines**; the paper is Hettie Brann's and the pocket is Dr Sen's; **he has not looked at it since the thirteenth of January and he is not going to in July, and he has now said that in this book nine times and improved it none of the nine.** The written line home is **the two hundred and twenty-first day**, blank, **four marks in four hands and none of them his**, and is not cured; the fourth was written on the first of April at a counter in about four seconds by a woman who was not asked, and it is a line somebody else can walk him home by and that is all it is. The Lark token was cold throughout, was in the inside pocket of the coat on a bank at a ford and in eleven streets, **and it is cold on the two hundred and seventh day and has done nothing since the seventh of December**, and he did not improve the sentence about a street that opens because of something in a man's coat. **Failing sensory detail: the sound recurred once, on Wednesday the twenty-fourth of June at about ten past eleven at a clinic counter, for about four seconds, and it was there again and it did not stay, and no face and no place and no person and no name came with it, and he has decided nothing about whether the two things he has lost are one thing or two, and he wrote in his own book that he is not going to decide in a volume where it happens four times.** **He held nothing, stabilised nothing, and wrote in Chapter 136 that he could have and did not.** **THE FIELD BOOK'S PAIRS ARE TWENTY-EIGHT AND THEY MOVED, for the first time since the twenty-fifth of April, and the reason is on the page: he was asleep for six nights in a place with no address with the book in the bottom of a bag and did not open it once, and a pair is a fact about where he was and not about what happened.** Three sevens, not two. **The run of fifty-two consecutive nights is over and he says so and calls what replaced it a habit, and a habit is not a discipline and not a method.** His failure is the **eighth seventh column**, in about a hundred and ten words, headed *why this district is entered as nil*, **and it is the first of the eight whose answer is not a person: a column that wants a name, and there is nobody to give it one, because a district entered as nil has had no resident since 1969, and for seven months the problem had been that there was a person on the other end who did not want their name on it, and this time there is nobody, and he knew that before he finished the hundred and ten words.** The **ninth resolution is the first one that is not a place: he hands the paper to the person the column is about**, Garn Ollerton refuses and tells him who to ask, Percy refuses in four seconds, **and it did not work, and a person is not a custody, and the keeping is a third thing and the third thing is the one he actually does.** He **gave up the seventh column until the first of September, in four lines of his own hand on the facing page of the Field Book on the first of July**, and wrote the cost out nine minutes later: **what stops on the first of September is me and nothing else stops on the first of September, and a foreman will be eleven weeks past four months on the eleventh of July and is not going to be asked, and that silence is now partly mine and I cannot get it out of my handwriting, and that is the most self-serving sentence I have written in seven months and it is dressed up as a discipline and it is not one.** He is not cured of anything. He is not the author of anything in this district or in Mireward. **And he is now the only person in the story whose attendance in Mireward requires a day off from a desk in Crown Heights, and he wrote in Chapter 139 that this is the first cost in seven months that is about a wage and not about a name.**

- **Mara Quill** — **milestone 2, moved once, procedurally, and it is the first time in six months she has told him a thing.** No kiss, no declaration, no jealousy scene, no rescue. **WHAT HAPPENED TO THE CUSTODY REGISTER PAGE: NOTHING, for the seventh consecutive block.** It is in a drawer with a coat in it; it was not produced, not named, not confessed to, not mentioned in dialogue, and **he did not ask.** **WHETHER HE TOLD HER ABOUT THE SEASON: NO, AND HE TOLD HER HE HAD NOT, AND IT IS THE ELEVENTH WEEK.** What is new is work, and it is a document: **he came to her with the first card and the second card and told her the second one had a reference number on it**, and she said it in about eleven seconds, *a sentence is a thing a person can be sent to look at and a sentence with a reference on it is a thing a person can be asked for, and the asking is done in a form, and the form goes in a file, and the hand is not on the form.* And then, once: **there is a line in a return that I reconcile on the last Thursday of every month which has nothing to do with you and nothing to do with a tram, and I have looked at it, and I am not going to tell you what it says, and I am telling you that I have looked at it.** He asked what kind of line it was. **That is the second time, and I said once.** **He did not ask again, and he wrote that he had not, and that if she tells him he is going to be a person who has been told and that is a different column — and that both his not-asking about the drawer and his not-asking about the line are the same act, and that he has known that since the fifth of May and has done nothing about it.** She is not a party to this and is not going to be softened about it. The Lark Street grievance is not named, not resolved, not softened and not referred to in any of the ten chapters. **The Volume 03 line in a return is the seed of Batch 0002 and it is the only thing in Chapters 131–140 that is about land, and it is not explained here and she will not explain it.**

- **Pell Harrow** — **seventeen, a courier, on no list, and he is not in these ten chapters and was not sent anywhere and was not asked anything.** **TERN IS STILL NOT FOUND.** His twenty-two days up the south ended on Monday the nineteenth of January and nobody has asked him and no batch has made anybody ask him in order to report it. **His own blank card is one of the three things under the brick and is blank, and it has been blank since the nineteenth of January, and nobody has taken it and nobody is going to.** The round is one man short and was one man short on the Monday the twenty-ninth of June as it has been since the twenty-eighth of December. His mark holds for a working shift and not overnight and was not used.

- **Percy** — **fifty-eight, registered since Volume 02 as a man who goes out into Mireward on the first dry week with a rod and a chain; THIS IS THE BLOCK IN WHICH HE IS PRESENT, and he is the batch's first working day and its law.** He has gone out since 1974 and writes **four lines** — a date, about nine feet of drain, a water mark, and **a name that is not his own and is the same name in the fourth line for forty-one years, and nobody has ever asked him who it is and he is not going to say.** The pad is in a drawer in Crown Heights from this year back to about 1969: **one of it, not copied, no schedule, no shelf number, and a man of about forty-one who has the key, has been there eleven years, has never asked, and retires in about four years**, and after which somebody will clear the drawer out because a drawer is in a room. He refused, correctly, in four seconds, **and his refusal is the batch's finding about the seventh column: *if you put me in a column I have to have a deputy*** — a thing that happens once a year cannot be handed to a man who is sixty-eight, the second one will not have been out in a February, he will not know which mark is which year, and in about eleven years there will be two names in a pad and no way of telling which line belongs to which man. **He refused a copy of the pad for the same reason and would not have it made.** He named the eighth seventh column in about eleven seconds. He gave nine words that were not a favour and then corrected the day. He refused, correctly, in nine words, to be talked at: **I leave at six. You have had the conversation.** He is not an office and does not become one. **He has not been thanked, has not been sent anywhere, and has not written down that the sentence he said on a raised path on the eighteenth of June has also been said on a blackboard in a drainage section in February by a different man, and Milo Rook is not going to tell him.**

- **Garn Ollerton** — **thirty-seven, ferry pilot, four landings, twenty-six years. NEW, and she is not a device.** She did not offer her name and made him ask for it on the Sunday. **She walked Milo Rook in and out of the tenth crossing and was not asked and said she would have said yes and was glad he did not, and she said those are two different things and she is not going to mix them up in a yard.** She said the six words about the terminus column in April's register — no: she said, *a ferryman does not navigate to a place, a ferryman navigates from a place* — and **that is the first route instrument in this series written by somebody who was not going to be on it, and there are about nine of them on that water and not one piece of paper about any of them, and a route on paper is a route somebody maintains and a route somebody maintains is a route somebody can be asked about.** She refused, correctly, to put the seventh-column card in her keeping, and told him who to ask so that he could not come back and say she had not. **She refused to let the marks-and-bell route be said in a yard, and she was right.** She refused, in nine words, to take him again this week, and said it was not a rule and she had no reason he would print. She has not been thanked and is not going to be.

- **Coll Sarrowe** — **forty-four, ditch keeper, thirty-one years, at a sluice that belongs to a body abolished in 1968. NEW, and he is the other half of the man of fifty-eight and neither of them knows the other exists.** He is paid by a farm by the hour for ditching and **two days a month of what he does is a sluice, and the farm does not know and the water authority does not know and the sluice does not know.** **A man with no address cannot be a ratepayer, because a ratepayer is a person at an address, and a man with no address is not a person to a form, so the water authority cannot bill him, cannot employ him and cannot write him a letter that would arrive.** The cost that is not curable in the whole block, and it is his and it is not a hardship: **if he is somewhere else on a Tuesday in a wet week then about eleven houses have a bad morning, and nobody will ever write that down, and in about four years somebody will say the dyke failed, and the dyke did not fail, and there is a bar in it.** One rescue in nine years, two hours, a chain, a tractor that is not insured to be in that water. **He refuses, correctly, to be named on anything: there have been four of us named in this district in thirty-one years and three of them left inside two years, and the one who is still here is the one who is not.** His name is not written in his own Field Book and he asked for nothing and is owed nothing.

- **Sabra Trewin** — **thirty-four, nine years teaching in the schoolroom at Cobb Lock. NEW.** **Nineteen children on a Tuesday and twenty-one on a Monday and the roll exists only in her head, and there is nowhere else to put it, and that is not a system of hers, it is the absence of a system.** She is not on the education roll, the relief list or any clinic's records, **and if there was a flood tomorrow they would be counted on the back of a bus by a man with a pen, and that would be the first paper ever made about them and it would be made on the day by somebody who does not know their names — and if she is on that bus then it is gone.** **She held the count of the tenth crossing in her head and is not writing it down either, and she said so before he could, and she said twenty minutes' worth is a pair and she is not going to.** She has nineteen marks cut in a post and **put four of them in on the Wednesday and will not tell a man in a coat which four.** She said the refused address and it is the sentence this district has been standing in for seven months with a different name on it: **a village that has no address is not a place that has been forgotten; a place that has been forgotten was on something and came off it; this was never on anything, and nobody did this to us, and we have been un-takeable for about sixty years by accident, and I am not going to be a woman who asks a city to do us a favour.**

- **Ola Petrossian** — **twenty-three, a nurse, and she came out on her day off, unasked, and walked four miles from a yard at the north end, and she is the fourth condition of the tenth crossing, and she is paid by a clinic that has no record of where she went.** The man of fifty-eight told her in about nine words on the Saturday. She was not asked and the standing condition of the crossings — that the fourth person is never asked — held. **She has no power, she is not a device, and she has not been thanked.**

- **The man of about fifty in a room with a machine in it** — **in this block twice and unchanged, and the only new thing he did was the whole of the escalation.** He put a card down face up, which he has not done in nineteen years, and he said **if you want to argue with me about it do it at half past six and not in this room**, and he said he would like to go back to about 1981, and the second run of the machine put a reference number on a sentence in a hand and **the hand is not on the form, and the machine took the hand over, and there is nothing in this city that can make a machine give a hand back.** He has not said a word about whose hand it is and will not.

- **The man of forty-one on no list at all** — **on no list, no power, no mark, and in this block he is the person who names the shape of the eighth seventh column's *absence* and the person who refuses to be the instrument of a going, and the count of his nine words is now TEN and is not written down anywhere but his own book.** Ninth, on the Wednesday, a refusal: *You have to go yourself, and you know it* — and the rest of it, that he has been the one not testing it since April, and that if anybody is ever sent from that gate then it becomes a place somebody is sent from and he will not be the reason that happens before he is forty, and **that Milo Rook is the fourth person in this district to tell him a thing about his own handwriting.** Tenth, on the Friday, at a gate, **and it is not a refusal: *I was not there. I am not going there*** — and he said the other half before Milo could get to the second sentence of his own, **which is that he has not been south of that bridge since he was a boy and he is not going to start at thirty-eight.** He has not been sent, has not been thanked, has no power, and **in Chapter 137 Milo Rook notices that he could not have said what proportion of the ten were refusals, and that this is the difference between a number and a column, and that he has gone six months without noticing it.**

- **The man of about fifty with a mower** — **new, in one scene, in the city, on the near side of a bridge, for one morning.** He has cut eleven yards on the city side for nine years, has never been over, and there is a line in the middle of the deck that is not painted, it is just where the mowing stops, **and there is a different grass because there was a man used to cut that side and he stopped, and nobody has ever put a line anywhere, and he has never once gone round.** He asked nothing of anybody and was asked nothing that mattered.

- **The woman of forty-five at the plan chest** — **new, in one scene, in Crown Heights, twenty-one years.** She gave him a bridge file's number without standing up, would not say one word about the other half of the bridge **because it is not my file and I do not want it in my file**, told him that was the correct thing and had taken him four months longer than it should have, and has not been thanked.

- **Hettie Brann, Dr Iona Sen, Cyril Sale, Roy Pryce, Nell Crale, Doreen Sankey, Nessa Bray, Corin Mabey, Hester Coombe, Bettina Sallow, Barrow, Lena Marr, Ivy Marsden, Ivo Sarratt, Cass Ardery, Rue, Ada Fenn** — **not in these ten chapters, with two exceptions, and none of their prices moved.** Dr Iona Sen is in Chapter 136 in four lines: **she did not ask him to describe the sound and did not ask him to find it and said the reason again, that a routine about a sound is the fourth thing, and she made him say the number out loud in a yard on the Friday, for the eighth time, and the tally has ten lines and is the record and he has still not looked at it.** Hettie Brann's three pieces of paper are still face down under her counter and unread, and the envelope with *1 of 4. — 1.iv.* on it has not moved and will not. **Roy Pryce is not in these ten chapters and the condition column was empty on the twenty-second, twenty-third, twenty-fourth, twenty-fifth and twenty-sixth walks and Milo Rook was not on two of them and nobody telephoned either time, and on the first of July a man of thirty-one put the foreman's silence partly into his own handwriting for the first time.** **Ada Fenn is not named in any of the ten chapters and is not referred to in any of them: the phrase *a woman of sixty-eight on nothing* is used only as a declared count in the Field Book entries, and the count stands at EIGHT and this block adds nothing to it.** The woman on the fourth floor of Cedar Court is not mentioned, so that the two sixty-eights cannot be folded, and the woman of sixty on the second landing is a different and unnamed resident and is the ninth of twenty-six names.

- **Orrin Sable, Halden Vey, Sel Iri, Tarin Kest** — **do not appear and are not named. Sel Iri is a Volume 4–10 character and does not appear before Volume 4. The office of the First Surveyor is not named in these ten chapters and did not request, receive or act on anything. Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–140.** Nobody was added above Cass Ardery, the office of the Prefect, or a hearing panel, and nothing that went wrong in this block is attributable to a new face.

---

## Volume 03, Batch 0002 (Chapters 141–150) — state after Chapter 150

**Updates to the standing of people already registered, plus one new minor figure who is ordinary, unnamed, has refused something and is not thanked. No new character, no new faction, no new antagonist, no new final enemy. POV is Milo Rook throughout. The batch's facts are in `state/continuity.md` under "What Chapters 141–150 fixed on the page," written from the manuscript after the writer's own pass (CR-046).**

- **Milo Rook** — **thirty, Stage 2, begun and does not complete, and he stabilised nothing in these ten chapters. NO CROSSING. THE REMEMBERED-ROUTE LIST IS ELEVEN, THE CROSSINGS ARE TEN, AND THE TALLY IS TEN LINES, and the page home, the token and the tally did not move.** The ninth crossing's failure to cost one is still unexplained and **he is holding to the promise he made in his own book on the twenty-fifth of June not to explain it before the end of July, and it is the twentieth, and a promise about the end of a month is the only date in this book he has written down twice.** The page home is the **two hundred and thirty-ninth day**, blank, **four marks in four hands and none of them his**; the token is cold on the **two hundred and twenty-fifth day** and has done nothing since the seventh of December and has been in eleven streets, in a room above a kitchen in a village, and in a records room about a mile from where a form was drafted. **Failing sensory detail did not recur in nineteen days, and he has written down that he does not know whether a thing that has not come for nineteen days is a thing that has stopped or a thing that has not come, and he has written that before and improved it none of the times. The first personal blank, the seam-scar, the record blank and the authorial blank appear nowhere in Chapters 141–150.** **THE FIELD BOOK'S PAIRS ARE TWENTY-NINE AND THEY MOVED ONCE, on the night of the sixteenth, and the interval is seven days from the ninth to the sixteenth, and there is no entry for the tenth to the fifteenth, and the tenth of July was a decision — he had the form in the bag and took it out and read it and put it back and did not open the book — and he cannot now remember whether he decided not to or simply did not. Four sevens, not three. The list must not be read as twenty-one, twenty-six, twenty-eight, thirty or thirty-one. The run of fifty-two consecutive nights is over and there is a habit and a habit is not a discipline and a habit is not a method, and two sevens in a fortnight has not turned the habit into a run and he has checked twice.** His failure is the **NINTH SEVENTH COLUMN** — a heading and about a hundred and ten words headed *why this district's works are not of record*, every word true, no name in it because there is no name to give one — and **he did not write it, and the reason is a line in his own handwriting on the facing page of the first of July, and a stop that a man wrote about himself is not a stop, and he said that out loud in a village on a Saturday to nobody.** **THE THING HE DID WRITE, in ninety seconds, in pencil, on the back of their own schedule, eleven miles from the district it was about, on the day after the day it was due, was a true sentence for column two of the eleven streets' row, and if it had gone in the check would not have found nil and no key would have been written down and a gang of eleven men would still have a contract — and it went in a bag and it did not go anywhere, and the cost fell on a foreman of fifty-four and not on him.** **HE IS THE FIFTH OF HIS OWN FIVE AND THE FIRST OF THE FIVE THAT IS ABOUT A PERSON AND THE FIRST HE HAS PAID FOR: on Sunday the nineteenth of July at about twenty-five past four he wrote a paragraph and a signature in the two inches of ruled space at the bottom of a Mireward district returns card, face down, and did not put it in the machine, and the reason is the only thing that is any use — a face-up card is re-run once a week on a line headed *re-run — face up only* and comes back with a reference number, so the hand stays on the paper, and that is the first time in eight months he has wanted a machine NOT to take a hand over. The nil is still a nil, the column is still hand-transferred and not typed, the three nils are three, nobody has been frightened, and a card with a thing struck through it is a card with a mark on it, and he cannot get the name off it.** **He is not cured of anything and he is not the author of anything, and he knows it: he is now the only name on any paper in Mireward and the only thing in this block that is his, and it cost four seconds and nobody asked him to.** The **Atlas residue, once in ten chapters, six words, in Chapter 149, given to nobody: *A printed ground holds nobody up.*** — **and he is not going to hand it to a woman of sixty-one at a counter, and not to a woman of thirty-four in a village who would understand it, and not to a man of fifty-four on a gate who would argue with it, and there are about nine people in this district and none of them has it.**

- **Mara Quill** — **milestone 2, moved once, and it was a DISAGREEMENT, and it is milestone 3 set up and not repaired, and she did not describe the line in the return. No kiss, no declaration, no jealousy scene, no rescue.** **WHAT HAPPENED TO THE CUSTODY REGISTER PAGE: NOTHING, for the EIGHTH consecutive block.** It is in a drawer with a coat in it; it was not produced, not named, not confessed to, not mentioned in dialogue, **he did not ask, and he was not allowed to.** She said it once, in a records room on a Saturday, and did not improve it: **I am not going to say anything about it, this week or in July, and that is not me being cold, and you have known that since May.** **WHETHER HE TOLD HER ABOUT THE SEASON: NO, AND HE TOLD HER HE HAD NOT, AND THE NUMBER OF WEEKS IS NOT PRINTED IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS — he said *twelve* out loud on the Saturday, and he has stopped counting and started dating, and dating it is a column, and a column is worse than a count.** **WHETHER SHE DESCRIBED THE LINE IN THE RETURN: NO. She gave four facts and no sentence — she reconciles it on the last Thursday of every month, the last Thursday was the sixteenth, she has done so for about two years before that, and it has never come up in a conversation with anybody — and he asked once in June and did not ask a second time in June and did not ask a third time in July, and she noticed that he did not, and he noticed her noticing, and neither of those is one of the three Volume 01 clues and he has put it in the book anyway because it is on the page.** What she did, and it is the volume's land thread, and it is hers and not his: **she found the second use of the instrument in about nine seconds on a Saturday morning with two people waiting and did not go into a room** — *column two, stability of district, is the column a land schedule wants* — and then read the whole thing out loud, standing up, in the order the print run went, and gave him the row, and the nine plots, and the book of shares in a different building, **and then said: you did not write it down, and it is on a printed side since the middle of May, and it was on a printed side before you put a hand on it on Friday, and the fact that you put a hand on it on Friday has changed nothing at all and I want that written down somewhere, because I have watched a man put a hand on something and I have watched what it did last time.** The disagreement, correct on both sides, unrepaired: *you should put the district's answer on the record* — **it is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing that exists** — against *it is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing a man with a box can ask for in about four years*, and she answers with his own sentence. **We are two people who have both said that and neither of us has moved and neither of us is going to, and I am not going to improve it in a records room on a Saturday with two people waiting.** The Lark Street grievance is not named, not resolved, not softened and not referred to in any of the ten chapters.

- **Pell Harrow** — **seventeen, a courier, on no list, and he is not in these ten chapters, was not sent anywhere, was not asked anything, and nobody has made anybody ask him.** **TERN IS STILL NOT FOUND.** The twenty-two days up the south ended on Monday the nineteenth of January and nobody has asked him and no batch has made anybody ask him in order to report it. **His own blank card is one of the three things under the brick, blank, and it has been blank since the nineteenth of January, and nobody has taken it and nobody is going to.** The round is one man short and ran on the sixth, ninth, thirteenth, sixteenth and twentieth of July and was one man short on all five. His mark holds for a working shift and not overnight and was not used.

- **Roy Pryce** — **fifty-four, foreman, thirty-one years, and this is the block's working day, and it is the best answer anybody hears in the fortnight, and it is not enough.** He came into the quay section office on Tuesday the seventh of July, put his hat on the counter, and said he was the person responsible for maintenance in the district and asked which of the four grounds is the one that is true. **He took three of the four apart himself, standing up, in about forty seconds, and he was right about all three: (a) is not true, because there is a level of record in a file with a date on it under his signature which has not moved since the fourth of December; (b) is not a lie, because there are two hundred and fifty-two people in a building with no number and not one of them is on a list, and (b) does not mean nobody is here; (c) is not true, because the sheet comes Monday to Sunday and goes into the rack on the Friday and there has not been one Monday in nine months without a sheet in it; and (d) is a question about money and is not his.** And then, in about four seconds, unasked: **he went into the corridor, came back with the weekly sheet for the week ending Sunday the fifth of July, put it on the counter squared, and said: that is the state of the works. There is nothing wrong with it. And there is no box on that form, or on the sheet, or in this building, or in this city, for *there is nothing wrong with it*.** He said the shape of the form in one sentence: **you have printed four ways for a district to lose its works and you have not printed one way for a district to keep them** — and then, when told the foot of the form is the way: **there is one way to keep them and it is on the Friday, and it is not printed as a way to keep them, it is printed as a way to complete them, and both of those are the same sentence with a different Friday in it.** He refused, correctly, to be asked what a length was like, twice: **if you put a question in that column then the sheet stops being a record of a length and starts being a record of a man, and a man can be asked what he wrote, and I have got eleven men and I have got a contract to the thirty-first and I am not spending any of it on that. The column is for a hole.** **HE WROTE THE CONDITION COLUMN'S FIRST ENTRY IN A HUNDRED AND THIRTY-SEVEN DAYS ON MONDAY THE THIRTEENTH OF JULY AND HIS SECOND ON MONDAY THE TWENTIETH, both in pencil, both the same four words and a figure, and neither of them is a fault.** He said **I will have something for Friday** and **I know you do not need me to have something for Friday. I am going to have something for Friday** — **and a district with no rateable unit has no stationery and the post goes to a rateable unit and the satchel is the only thing that comes to eleven streets, so it went out in the satchel on the Friday morning and came back in it on the Friday afternoon and has been in it every day since, and nobody has asked him what it was and nobody is going to.** In the yard at half past six on the seventeenth: **I would like it noted that I did not stand there on the Friday morning with it in my hand and ask anybody, because I did not think there was anywhere to ask** — and, after eleven seconds, **that condition column has been empty for a hundred and fifty-five days, and a man in a building a mile away has written a sentence about it, and it is the first sentence anybody has ever written about that column, and it is not mine, and I am not going to pretend it is.** **HE THINKS MILO ROOK DID NOT FILL IN THE COLUMN BECAUSE OF HIM, AND MILO ROOK DID NOT TELL HIM OTHERWISE, AND NOBODY HAS ASKED HIM ABOUT DECEMBER AND NOBODY IS GOING TO. He was on a gate on Saturday the eleventh of July, which is eleven weeks and three days past the four months, and nobody asked him, and a Saturday is the day a foreman of thirty-one years is on a gate and not in a building.** He has not been thanked and is not going to be.

- **Cyril Sale** — **works clerk, forty-six, and this is his block, and the refusal is his and it is correct and it changed nothing.** He read the district schedule out on the back length on Monday the sixth of July, all of it, including the key and the line at the foot, **in the voice he reads a sheet in, which is not a voice anybody argues with.** On Friday the seventeenth, in about twenty minutes, he said **they complete them on the Thursday; it is a Thursday thing and it has been a Thursday thing for about eleven years and it is not a punishment and it is not a meeting**, and **the check is done by reading the district's own file** — and then he put the pen down and did not pick it up again: **I am not signing column six, and you cannot make me… because if I sign that, I have put a sentence about the sixteenth of December into a file, and there is not one sentence about the sixteenth of December that is both true and mine. There is a true one and it is not mine. There is one that is mine and it is a form about a length of road and it is not about the sixteenth of December.** And: **I am a works clerk of forty-six with three sentences on the backs of three forms in the correspondence tray and not in a drawer, and I have stopped putting them there, and this is the same thing a different size.** On Monday the twentieth, at a gate, in about nine words and once: **that column is the only one in this building that is allowed to be empty… and it has now been asked about twice in eight months by two men who are not me, and it is answered twice, and it is on a sheet for the first time in five months and a half, and it is seventeen weeks late for a district that is not going to be maintained after the thirty-first of August.**

- **Sabra Trewin** — **thirty-four, nine years teaching, and the refusal in this block is hers and it is correct.** On Saturday the eleventh of July, in a room above a kitchen, refusing to keep the schedule for a week, in one long sentence: **if the district has an address then the first thing that arrives at the address is a demand, and if a schoolroom has a form in the drawer then the schoolroom is a place, and I have got nineteen children in it and not one line of them is on any paper in this city, and the pencils are in that drawer** — and **I have watched four years of this from a chair with nineteen children in it and I am not going to be the woman who hands a district its first piece of stationery.** And **I would rather say it once to somebody who has come from four miles away than say no to four people**, and **I do not know what is on it and I have not asked and I am not going to, and I would like it noticed that nobody has had to ask me twice.** And then, on her way to the door, the block's cost in one sentence: **your foreman sent something on Friday. I do not know where and he did not tell me and I did not ask… and he is a man who has said nine words to me three times in eight months and he said that one twice.** She has not been thanked.

- **Coll Sarrowe** — **forty-four, ditch keeper, thirty-one years, and this is his block, and the changed method and the recovery are both his, and neither of them is a word.** **There are five of them on ninety feet of bank and he did not introduce the other four and said, when asked how long they have been doing it, *longer than you have been asking* — nine years. One of them lives at the north end. One of them is about nineteen and cannot read the water at all and can lift.** The changed method, without stopping work and without a word: **he walked the nineteen-year-old up and down ninety feet four times, by the arm, and put him on the other end and went back to the bar** — **he is the Tuesday.** And then one sentence, the only one that was not about water or a bar: **I have been getting them ready for nine years and I have never once told them I was getting them ready, and I have never once written it down, and there is going to be a day when I am not able to come and there is going to be five of us here and not one of us will be on a piece of paper about it, and that is not a fault and it is the only thing I have got.** And then, before anything else could be said: **you are not going to thank me for that. If you thank me for that then in about four years somebody is going to ask you what I said, and then I am a man who said something, and then I am on a piece of paper, and there have been four of us named in this district in thirty-one years and three of them left inside two years and the one who is still here is the one who is not.** Milo Rook said no, he said good, and **he was not thanked and has not been thanked and his name is not written in the Field Book and he asked for nothing.**

- **The man of forty-one on no list at all** — **in these ten chapters twice, and the count of his nine words is now ELEVEN, and it is still not written down anywhere but Milo's own book.** In eleven streets on the third of July, about the empty row: **it is empty because the eleven streets sends a works sheet and not this, and I have been in this office four times a day since the twenty-eighth of December and I have never once seen one of these go out and I never once saw one come in. And the row is empty, and empty is the only thing this form can read.** And on Friday the seventeenth, to a request, in nine words: ***Standing in a room is standing in a file.*** He has not been sent, has not been thanked, has no power, and has not been south of that bridge since he was a boy.

- **The man of about twenty-six in a high-visibility jacket** — **in these ten chapters twice, and the second time he did the best piece of work in the block and nobody asked him to and nobody thanked him.** On the back length on Monday the twentieth, at half past eight, with a hard pencil, **he copied the four words and the figure onto the duplicate carbon, letter for letter, in about four minutes, so that the sentence would be legible on the copy** — because a foreman's pencil comes through as a grey smear and a smudge is not a record and cannot be produced. He also said, unprompted and to nobody in particular, **I have walked this length for about four years and I have never once had anything to write in it and I did not know that was unusual**, and was told **it is not unusual, it is what it is for.** He has been on the back length three times in two volumes and was asked nothing useful any of the three times, and **he is not a fifth instance of anything and he is not a clue and a later block may not thank him or ask him what he copied or why.**

- **The man of about fifty in a room with a machine in it** — **not in these ten chapters, and nothing of his moved.** The allocation sheet still comes off the machine on a Wednesday at nine, five rows, five figures transferred with a pen, the fourth column still empty and filled in three times in nineteen years, the line under *officer responsible* still never filled in, and the zero in the fen row still a zero. **He has still never been told that the sentence on the face-up card was copied onto a district schedule and typed on a line on the fourth of July, and he is not going to be.**

- **Percy, Garn Ollerton, Dr Iona Sen, Hettie Brann, Ola Petrossian, Nell Crale, Doreen Sankey, Nessa Bray, Bettina Sallow, Barrow, Hester Coombe, Lena Marr, Ivy Marsden, Ivo Sarratt, Cass Ardery, Rue, Verrick Assal, Jo Lask, Tomas Rusk, Old Mabb, the woman of sixty on the second landing, the woman of fifty-four with a plot number and no bill in six years, the man of about sixty at a shed at Cardew Docks** — **none of them is named in these ten chapters except Percy and Garn by reference, and none of their prices moved.** **The man of about sixty at a shed at Cardew Docks still does not know that a piece of paper exists and is still not going to be told, and the second side of the sheet is still right about every Wednesday because of him.** **Ada Fenn is not named in any of the ten chapters and is not referred to in any of them: the phrase *a woman of sixty-eight on nothing* is used only as a declared count in the Chapter 150 Field Book entry, and the count stands at EIGHT and this block adds nothing to it and it is not nine.** The woman on the fourth floor of Cedar Court is not mentioned, so that the two sixty-eights cannot be folded, and the woman of sixty on the second landing is a different and unnamed resident and is the ninth of twenty-six names.

- **Orrin Sable, Halden Vey, Sel Iri, Tarin Kest, Soren Rook** — **do not appear and are not named as persons. Sel Iri is a Volume 4–10 character and does not appear before Volume 4. THE OFFICE OF THE FIRST SURVEYOR ACTED FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THE SERIES, and it acted as a printed signature block on a printed instrument and not as a person, it requested nothing, received nothing and answered nothing, there is no person behind it, and nobody asked.** Nobody was added above Cass Ardery, the office of the Prefect, or a hearing panel, and **nothing that went wrong in this block is attributable to a face: the escalation is a key of four printed grounds, a row that was empty, an office with a pen, and a Thursday.**

---

## Volume 03, Batch 0003 (Chapters 151–160) — state after Chapter 160

**Updates to the standing of people already registered, plus ONE new minor figure who is ordinary, has refused something, is not thanked, and has no power. No new character, no new faction, no new antagonist, no new final enemy, no new kind of place. POV is Milo Rook in nine chapters and MARA QUILL IN CHAPTER 158, which is the second Mara-POV chapter in the manuscript after Chapter 30 and is recorded in `CR-050`. The batch's facts are in `state/continuity.md` under "What Chapters 151–160 fixed on the page," written from the manuscript after the writer's own pass.**

- **Milo Rook** — **THIRTY, Stage 2, begun and does not complete. NO CROSSING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS. He held nothing, stabilised nothing, crossed nothing. THE REMEMBERED-ROUTE LIST IS ELEVEN, THE CROSSINGS ARE TEN, THE TALLY IS TEN LINES, and the page home, the token and the tally did not move, and if you do not stage a crossing both figures do not move and that is what happened.** **DID THE BLANK MOVE: YES, ONCE, IN CHAPTER 152, AND IT ACQUIRED NOTHING. At about half past one on Friday the twenty-fourth of July, on a top track, for about four seconds, the sound went the way it has gone twice in eight months, and a taste came with it, and there was no face, no place, no person and no name on either, and it did not acquire one, and it did not become the bell, and it did not become Neris and it did not become a key. On the page, in his own hand, that day: *a lost sound and a bell that rings are two different things and I am not going to write them in the same column* — and that rule is the only rule on that page which is not about a month.** He has not looked at the tally since the thirteenth of January and **has not looked at it in August or in September.** **DID THE PAIRS MOVE: NO. THE LIST IS TWENTY-NINE AND IT DID NOT MOVE ONCE IN FORTY-SIX DAYS, and the reason is on the page six separate times: he has written an entry on most nights since the sixteenth of July, and an interval of one day is not a pair whether or not either of the two days has anything on it, and if the count of pairs in this block is a small number and not a large one, that is correct.** Two sevens in about a fortnight is a coincidence in July and **is still a coincidence on the fourth of September and he has written nothing about it.** The ninth crossing's failure to cost one is **still unexplained, and the promise about the end of July is now broken in writing: on Monday the thirty-first of August, the last day of the month, he wrote in his own book that he is not going to explain it, and he recorded that the end of July was the thirty-first of July, that he did not do it then, and that this is the second time he has said so and the second time has cost a day, and that a promise kept in a book and broken in a book is not a promise and is not a break and is a third thing, and nothing on that page is a device and he is not going to explain it with one.** **DID HE KEEP OR BREAK THE LINE ABOUT THE SEVENTH COLUMN: HE DID NOT BREAK IT, TWICE, AND HE WROTE DOWN WHY BOTH TIMES, AND NOBODY HAS FORGIVEN HIM AND NOBODY IS GOING TO.** The rule is in his own hand on the facing page and says *no seventh column before the first of September*, and on Thursday the twenty-third of July he wanted a column that would take a sound, **had it in his hand in about eleven seconds, and did not draw it**, and wrote in the margin of the page that carries the rule: *I want one. That is the whole of it… I am not going to wait for the first of September either and I want that written down in the same hand as the rule, because the rule is about a month and what I am actually doing is about me.* On Wednesday the twelfth of August a machine in a room about a mile away **would have taken a fifth column and he did not ask for it**, and the reason and the cost are on the page: a man of sixty-one cannot be asked, so he cannot be claimed, so he cannot be paid, so nothing has ever been wrong. And on the thirty-first of August the rule ran out and **he did not draw one on the first of September either**, and the reason, in his own hand, is the worst sentence in the block: *the reason I was not going to wait for the first of September was that I was hoping a month would make it correct. A rule that runs out is not a release and it never was a stop, and I am not going to be improved by anybody in a yard.* **HE IS NOT THE AUTHOR OF ANYTHING IN THIS BLOCK.** **The sixth thing of the shape "got by writing something down in this book and not doing it" is the seventy-eight words he did not say to a foreman of fifty-four on the last morning of a contract (**he called them eleven words on the page and it is seventy-eight; see `CR-051` item 8**), and it cost Roy Pryce, and nobody asked him to, and the five that he has been calling his five are a DIFFERENT LIST of five — the plate, the seventh column on a table in his own office, the not-telling, the rule on the facing page, and the paragraph and signature in two inches of ruled space — and the two lists must not be merged, which is a fact he wrote down in advance of anybody doing it for him.** The plate is **a hundred and fifty-one days in the grass at the low end of Bridge Street on Friday the fourth of September, and still never reported and never reported to, and the decision was made on the first of June and has been told to four people and one of them is him.** The name on the card **is still on it, written face down on Sunday the nineteenth of July so that a machine would not take the hand over, and it is thirty-nine days old on the twenty-seventh of August, and it cannot be taken off, and a card with a thing struck through it is a card with a mark on it, and the re-run is on a Friday at about four, and he is still the only name on any paper in Mireward and a later block may not name him on a second piece of paper and may not make the name useful.** **THE FOUR BELLS ARE FOUR AND HE HAS NOT WRITTEN THEM DOWN AND HE HAS NOT TOLD ANYBODY, and there are four people who have not been asked whether it is four, and he is the only person in the city who knows the number, and he went out on Friday the twenty-eighth of August and did not go to the top track, and he has written that down and has not written down what about, and that is now the fifth time he has not gone and looked at a thing he could have looked at.** The Atlas residue is **not used in these ten chapters and one is not required.**

- **Mara Quill** — **thirty-one. Milestone 2, deepened, and it is milestone 3 set up and NOT repaired, and neither of them won it and neither of them is warm. No kiss, no declaration, no jealousy scene, no rescue.** **WHAT HAPPENED TO THE CUSTODY REGISTER PAGE: NOTHING, for the NINTH consecutive block. It is in a drawer with a coat in it; it was not produced, not named, not confessed to, and NOT MENTIONED IN DIALOGUE, and he did not ask, and he is not allowed to.** In her own interior, unnamed and undescried, once: *there is a page in a drawer in a room she is not in, with a coat in the drawer with it, and it is hers, and it has been hers a long time, and she has not produced it and has not named it and has not said one word about it to anybody since May.* **WHETHER HE TOLD HER ABOUT THE SEASON: NO, AND NO WEEK-COUNT IS PRINTED IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS.** He has not told her since the middle of April; **she has not asked since July**; and in her own chapter she records that she found out he had stopped counting by noticing that the number had stopped going up, and that a number which stops going up is worse than a number that is running, **and that she has not told him she noticed.** **WHETHER SHE DESCRIBED THE LINE IN THE RETURN: NO. She did not describe it, nobody asked her to, and Chapter 158 says so in its own text, and a cell and a line are two different things in a return and paper is not a reason.** What she did, and it is the volume's land thread continuing, and it is hers and not his: **on the last Thursday of the month, in the records room, he was not present and has not been in that room on a last Thursday in about two years, she reconciled the return; the last cell is the week beginning Monday the thirty-first of August and it had not happened yet; a blank in a cell is a query and a query goes down; and she put a dash, with a pen, in about four seconds — and a dash is a departmental statement that nothing was received, which is a statement about the post and not about the district, and nothing was not received, nothing was not sent, and the form has one mark and the mark cannot say which.** Then she opened **the register of correspondents, a list of addresses about four inches thick, made to tell a department where a letter goes and therefore the wrong shape for a district, because a district is not somewhere a letter goes, it is somewhere letters come from**, and **shut it ninety seconds later and entered nothing**, and her reason, once, is the one that puts his own given-up on the table: **a district that can be written to is a district whose returns are held somewhere, and a district whose returns are held somewhere is a district somebody can ask a card from, and there is one card in this city with a hand on it for the fen country and it has a line through it and a man's name under the line.** She went through nine minutes of versions of the register and none of them is safe, **including the one that leaves the eleventh streets on exactly one printed side in the whole of this city as a row that is not going to be maintained.** In a corridor at ten past four she told him she had put a dash, told him why he could not have the sentence instead, told him the second time this year that a true sentence in somebody's hand is the whole mechanism and that the first one did not need a hand at all, **and he did not ask her which Thursday, and she had not said which Thursday and was not going to, and the not-saying was the correct procedure and was also the reason she could say the rest of it.** The disagreement, third time, unrepaired: **it is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing that exists** / **it is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing a man with a box can ask for in about four years** — **Yes. And I have proved both of those in one morning, at the same desk, with the same pen, and you were not in the room, and I am not going to pretend that either of us is going to win it, and I am not going to be warm with you about it in a corridor.** **Nor am I.** The Lark Street grievance is not named, not resolved, not softened and not referred to in any of the ten chapters. **She has not been thanked and has said she is not going to be, and the fifth cell is the only thing she has written this month and it is a dash and it is wrong, and she has left it.**

- **Barnaby Pike (NEW)** — **sixty-one, has dug in this district for about thirty-one years, is on no rate roll because a ratepayer is a person at an address and there is no address, has no employer because the families pay him and pay him in coin out of a jar on a table, and has no wage, no record, no holiday and no name on anything in this city. He is the block's first working person who CANNOT BE ASKED, and therefore cannot be claimed, and therefore has never been paid for thirty-one years, and that is a fact about the same shape as everything else in this volume. He rang a handbell on a nail inside the door of a house that is not made of brick and has no number, twice before he started work and once when he stopped, with his left hand, while digging with his right, and has done so for about thirty-one years.** He told the whole of it while he worked, about four hundred words, and none of it was a document: **the bell goes the day somebody dies, and again the following year on the same date, and again, and it has gone every year since, and nobody arranged it and there is no meeting and there was no first one anybody can point at; he opens the hole up, puts it right, does the grass, takes the weed off it, and goes, and it takes him an hour and a bit and nobody is watching and nobody thanks him and he has never asked to be thanked, because if you thank a man for this then you have to give him something, and there is nothing to give him.** **HE REFUSED THREE THINGS AND GAVE ONE: he will not say who it is for, not because it is a mystery, because if he says a name then the name is a name he knows, and in about four years somebody can come and ask which date goes with it, and the date is the whole of it and the date is in his head. He will not say how many of them there are, in about four words: *I do not know the number either. I know the people I have seen. I have got an idea. An idea is not a number and I am not going to give you an idea dressed up as one, because you will write the idea down and in about four years somebody will ask you for the number.*** He said, unasked, the sentence the whole block turns on: **whatever you write down will be the first time anybody has written down any of it, and that is not a service, that is a door; there has not been a door in this for thirty-one years and there has not needed to be one, and I have never in my life seen a thing get written down and then go back to being how it was.** He said he would not be there on the next Friday and told him not to come. **He has not been thanked, was not thanked, and is not going to be, and his name is not in the Field Book and nobody has asked him anything else and nobody is going to. His nineteen years of dates and the nineteen cuts in a post at a ford are two different numbers and are written next to each other on purpose so that a reader of the book can see him not joining them.**

- **Roy Pryce** — **fifty-four, foreman, thirty-one years, and this block is the cost of the printed date landing on him.** On Monday the twenty-fourth of August, in a yard, with about nine people in it and no coffee, he put a folded piece of paper on the low wall, squared it with his thumb, and said: **it is a notice that the works on the back length are discontinued after the thirty-first of August and it has got my name in a box on the last line and I did not sign it and I have not seen it and nobody asked me, and I am not going to stand here and be the man who complains about it, because if I say I did not agree to it then it is a disagreement, and a disagreement wants two, and there is one, and there is no second one and there is not going to be a second one, because the district has got no address and the office could not write to us and it went to the contract instead, and I am on the contract, and that is the whole of how I got into a box on a Thursday. And if I stand in this yard and say it is wrong, then in about four years there is a piece of paper in a file with a man in a yard on it saying the eleventh street district disputes the discontinuance, and there is no district, and I will have built the first piece of paper there has ever been about this place, and it will be about me, and it will be wrong, because the works are going to stop on the thirty-first whatever I say.** He asked for nothing to be said. **He asked one question in four seconds — *did you put my name on it* — and was told no, and said *right*, and went and washed.** He has been **eighteen weeks and five days past four months on Monday the thirty-first of August** and nobody is going to ask him, and that silence is on the facing page of Milo's Field Book in Milo's own handwriting. **On the last morning of the contract he asked, on the Friday, whether there would be a sheet, and was told there is no week after the contract, and said, at ten past eight with his thumb on the clipboard: *that is not the office being difficult, that is a machine, and the machine is right, and I have got a gang standing on a road at half past seven on a Monday because a contract says to the thirty-first and the contract does not know what day of the week the thirty-first is.*** Nobody answered him. **THE COST OF THIS BLOCK FELL ON HIM AND HE WILL NEVER BE TOLD AND HE DID NOT ASK: the only name of any person on a printed side about this district since the fourteenth of April is his, in a box that says he signed a thing he did not sign; the whole of the record of his last day in charge of that gang is a figure in a tin in a plan room in another building, and a figure in a tin is not a record of a man; and the sentence that could have stood beside the box was said out loud in a yard on the twenty-fourth and it is not in any file and cannot be put in one and he has said he is not going to say it again.** **The two condition-column entries are still the thirteenth and the twentieth of July, both four words and a figure, and no later block may fault them or improve them or put a word in either column about why the days in between were blank.**

- **Sabra Trewin** — **thirty-four, nine years teaching, and the refusal in this block is hers, is correct, and changed nothing.** On Friday the thirty-first of July, on her own step with her sleeves rolled, after standing a man of thirty at the back of her room for an hour and a quarter and then sending him out: **for about nine years, when somebody in this room has had somebody die, I have rung it. Only when somebody in this room. There is no other rule and there has never been a rule and I have never written it down and there is nowhere to write it down, and I have been asked twice this year by two different people whether I would put it in a book and I said no both times and I would like it noted that neither of them had any business asking.** She does it once and not the next year and will not say why to a person in a good coat. On the count: **that is three numbers and not one practice, and if you put them together in a book then you have made a practice out of three men and their memories, and a practice with a number at the end of it is a rule, and a rule can be broken by a person and there is nobody here who is going to be the person who breaks it.** And on the rope: **four children take turns, the rule is not written anywhere, she has watched them give it nine and seven and eleven in one term, she has never once corrected one of them, she could not tell you what the right one is, and if I stand up in front of seventeen children and tell them it is seven then I have taken four old women who are dead off a rope, and I do not know that I am entitled to do that, and I have not gone looking to find out whether I am.** And she volunteered — decided, not extracted — the fact the block is about: **there is a child in this room whose mother's mother died at the end of May, and she has got the date, and she told me the date on the Thursday and she does not know what it is for, and I have not written it down. I know it. I have got it since the Thursday. That is the point and you have not got there yet… and that is why I am not going to let you have the date. You are a man who writes things down. If you have got a date and a bell and a child then in about four years you have got a row, and the row will have a schoolroom on it, and a schoolroom on a piece of paper is a place, and I have spent nine years keeping this room off every piece of paper in this city and I am not going to be the reason it is on one before I am forty.** She told him he would not ask again and would get the same eleven words if he did. **She has not been thanked, he did not thank her, and he has written that down. Her roll is seventeen on a Friday, nineteen on a Tuesday and twenty-one on a Monday, it exists in her head, it is on nothing, and a schoolroom is still not a rateable place and still has no street.**

- **Garn Ollerton** — **thirty-seven, ferry pilot, twenty-six years, four landings, and the block's recovery, which is hers, takes about two seconds, is not a word, and is not thanked.** On Friday the fourteenth of August she told him the vehicle arithmetic he was getting wrong — the Tuesday lorry goes to a yard at the north end and does not go to a landing, the Friday one comes in at half past ten and goes back at four, and he has been out on four of them since the end of June — and then, in answer to a sentence he had not planned to say: **Then that is four. I am not going to tell you where the other two are and I am not going to tell you which of the three you have got wrong, and I have said four and that is one more than I have said to anybody in twenty-six years and I said it because you said the nine, and I have never once in my life wanted somebody else to know how many times something was rung.** And: **I would like it noticed that I have not been thanked and I am not going to be, and I have said that in about nine words before and I am not going to say it again.** She then told him, because she had decided to and not because he got there, about the bell on the wall at the top of the step — **about four inches across the mouth and it came off a boat, and it is for the first one of the day and the last one off and a boat coming in the dark, and it has been all three of those since before she was doing this and she has done all three and has never once written any of it down, and there is no form for it and there is not going to be one** — **every day, at ten past seven and at four in the afternoon, including days when she is not on this water at either of those, and on Sundays, every day since about 1979 and she has not missed one.** And the sentence he wrote down and did not say to her: **a thing that happens every day at the same hour is a timetable, and a timetable in this city is a document with a column on it, and the moment a bell at a landing is rung every day at ten past seven it stops being a thing four people do and becomes a service, and a service is a thing somebody is responsible for, and the moment anybody in this district is responsible for it there is a form with a signature on it, and the four people stop.** **She is right about her twenty-six years and wrong about the other thing and she will not be told and there is nobody in this district who is going to tell her and there is a man in a wet coat who could and is not going to.**

- **The man of about fifty in a room with a machine in it** — **his block, and the refusal is his, and it is correct, and he has told nobody anything.** On Wednesday the twelfth of August a man of thirty stood at his machine for two hours and a quarter and did not ask one question. **They do not set a machine for one district** — he said that unasked in about nine words while transferring the fifth figure — **and a column is set once and it stays, set for nine hundred and not for one, and it is not set again in August, because in about four years somebody is going to want to know the day a card was issued and the day is a column and you cannot take a column out without taking the day out. And I do not know what you would want to put in it, and I have not asked, and I have had a machine that prints a card in this room for nineteen years and I have never once seen a hand in the reason column before the seventeenth of June and I have not asked about that either and I am not going to ask about yours.** He refused to be thanked: **you have said that to about four people and every one of them is a person who had told you something for nothing, and I am the fifth, and I have not told you anything.** The allocation sheet still comes off the machine on a Wednesday at about nine, five rows, five figures transferred with a pen, **the fourth column still empty and filled in three times in nineteen years, the line under *officer responsible* still never filled in, and the zero in the fen row still a zero.** He has still not been told about the typed name in a box, has not connected it to the hand in the reason column, and is not going to be told about either; **and Milo Rook put the lie *are you the one that wants a column* past him in half a second on a Wednesday morning, and the cost of the lie is that a man who has been in a room nineteen years has now been told by his own face that there is nothing here anybody could want, and he had not worked that out before, and he will not forget it, and that was done for a reason that has nothing to do with him.**

- **The man of about fifty with a mower** — **nine years on the city half of one bridge and never over the line, and this block is his.** In August he stopped the machine about four feet short of the unmown line, shut it off, and stood there for half a minute, and gave the reason unasked: **about three hundred yards of path on the far side that eleven men and a foreman walk twice a week, a machine of about two hundred and forty pounds, a rail on one side and a slope on the other, and a load calculation in a drawer from about 1974 with the low end not re-checked, and I am not putting two hundred and forty over that.** He has cut the far half with a sickle for nine years, about ten minutes a morning before he starts on this side, **not on his time sheet, paid for by nobody, and never told to anybody** — and he told Milo Rook only because *you have been standing on this deck since the quarter past eleven and you are the first person in nine years to stand on the far side of it, and if I do not say it now then in about four years somebody will find out that I have been over here and think I did it for a reason.* **He was not thanked and did not expect it and said the ten minutes was not on a time sheet for that reason. He has not been asked about the handrail post and Milo Rook, who was standing eleven feet from the man who cuts both halves of that bridge, did not ask him.**

- **The man of about forty-one on no list at all** — **in these ten chapters three times, and the count of his nine words is now TWELVE OCCASIONS in Volume 03, of which the eleventh is Chapter 144 and the twelfth is Chapter 157, and the number of words is nine and is not the same number. HE IS NOT THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE WHO HOLDS THE KEY TO THE DRAWER IN CROWN HEIGHTS, and the second one is in no book.** In this block: the second tin at thirty-two on Monday the twenty-seventh of July, in a corridor, with a handcart; on Monday the twenty-fourth of August, at ten past seven at a gate, **You have got a man in a box now** / **That is what you said just now and not what I said. I have never been able to work out why it is that a row of a district is a document and one man in one box is a different kind of thing, and I have been at this gate four times a day for two hundred days and I have not been sent and I am not going to be, and I would rather somebody in a good coat who writes things down worked that out than me, because I am not going to be the one who says it and have it in a book**; and on Friday the fourth of September, ten past four, with a handcart, to the only question Milo Rook asked anybody that week, which was a fair one — *Does anybody empty that* / **I have been in this office four times a day since the twenty-eighth of December. I have watched that rack. It has been got from and never got from. I am not going to be the man who says which of those it is.** **He has not been sent, has not been thanked, has no power, has not been south of that bridge since he was a boy, and there is nothing on any paper in this city that says he was asked a fair question in a corridor on the fourth of September and gave a correct answer to it.**

- **The man of about twenty-six in a high-visibility jacket** — **once in these ten chapters, on the last morning, at the north end at about eight, and he did not ask for a sheet and nobody asked him for anything and he stayed half an hour and went off up the length the way he goes.** He has not been thanked, has not been asked what he does here, **is not a fifth of anything, is not a clue, and has not been made a narrator, and a later block may not thank him or ask him anything.**

- **Cyril Sale, Hettie Brann, Dr Iona Sen, Bettina Sallow, Barrow, Doreen Sankey, Ivo Sarratt, Cass Ardery, Percy, Coll Sarrowe, Lena Marr, Ivy Marsden, Jo Lask, Tomas Rusk, Old Mabb, the woman of sixty on the second landing, the woman of fifty-four with a plot number, the man of about sixty at a shed at Cardew Docks, the man of about forty who sweeps the Row, the fishmonger of about forty-five, the coffee man of about sixty, the man in the works jacket, Rue, Ola Petrossian, Nell Crale, Nessa Bray, Hester Coombe** — **none of them appears in these ten chapters, none of their prices moved, and the fishmonger was not given a name, not thanked, not put on a list, and did not say the standing answer again, and the man of about forty who sweeps the Row has still not been asked for his twenty-eight undated words, and the man of about sixty at Cardew Docks still does not know that a piece of paper exists and is not going to be told and the second side of the sheet is still right about every Wednesday because of him. ADA FENN IS NOT NAMED IN ANY OF THE TEN CHAPTERS AND IS NOT REFERRED TO IN ANY OF THEM; the count of a woman of sixty-eight on nothing stands at EIGHT and this block adds nothing to it and it is not nine.** The woman on the fourth floor of Cedar Court is not mentioned, so the two sixty-eights cannot be folded. **Doreen Sankey did not use the instrument in this batch, or in Batches 4 to 8, or in any batch.**

- **Pell Harrow** — **seventeen, a courier, on no list, not in these ten chapters, not sent anywhere, not asked anything, and nobody has been made to ask him anything. TERN IS STILL NOT FOUND.** The twenty-two days up the south ended on Monday the nineteenth of January and nobody has asked him and no batch has made anybody ask him in order to report it. **His own blank card is one of the three things under the brick, blank, and it has been blank since the nineteenth of January, and nobody has taken it and nobody is going to, and the run-number card's line on the front has not been taken off.** The round is one man short and was one man short on every gang day in this block, **and there is now no post at all, because the works' papers were the post.**

- **Orrin Sable, Halden Vey, Sel Iri, Tarin Kest, Soren Rook** — **do not appear and are not named as persons, and Soren Rook is not named anywhere in Chapters 1–160.** Nobody was added above Cass Ardery, the office of the Prefect, or a hearing panel, and **nothing that went wrong in this batch is attributable to a new face: the escalation is a typed name in a box on a printed side, a contract, a dash in a cell, a sheet that was never printed, and a Thursday.**
