## Volume 03, Batch 0004 (Chapters 161–170) — state after Chapter 170

**Updates to the standing of people already registered. NO NEW CHARACTER, no new faction, no new antagonist, no new final enemy, no new kind of place, and nobody added above Cass Ardery, the office of the Prefect, or a hearing panel. POV is Milo Rook in all ten chapters. The batch's facts are in `state/continuity.md` under "What Chapters 161–170 fixed on the page," written from the manuscript. `CR-052` records what this batch added and `CR-053` records the writer's own verification pass.**

- **Milo Rook** — **THIRTY, Stage 2, begun and does not complete. NO CROSSING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS. He held nothing, stabilised nothing, crossed nothing. THE REMEMBERED-ROUTE LIST IS ELEVEN, THE CROSSINGS ARE TEN, THE TALLY IS TEN LINES, and the page home, the token and the tally did not move, and if you do not stage a crossing both figures do not move and that is what happened.**

  **DID THE BLANK MOVE: YES, ONCE, IN CHAPTER 163, AND IT ACQUIRED NOTHING.** At about the second of four minutes at the third post on Friday the eighteenth of September, **for about four seconds, the sound went the way it has gone twice in eight months and a taste came with it, and there was no face, no place, no person and no name on either, and it did not acquire one, and it did not become the bell, and it did not become the mowing line, the post, the marks, the ford, or the Rainway. HE DID NOT WRITE THE TIME DOWN, which is the first time in eight months he has not done that.** On the page, in his own hand: *a lost sound and a bell that rings are two different things and I am not going to write them in the same column* — the only rule on that page which is not about a month — and *if it ever becomes a thing I will write the name of that thing in this book at the time, and until then it is four seconds and a taste and nothing, and I am not going to be the man who gives a four-second thing a name because a name is the only thing in this story that anybody can be asked for.* **He has not looked at the tally since the thirteenth of January and has not looked at it in September.**

  **DID THE PAIRS MOVE: NO. THE LIST IS TWENTY-NINE AND IT DID NOT MOVE ONCE IN TWENTY-FOUR DAYS, and the reason is on the page nine separate times: he has written an entry on most nights, and an interval of one day is not a pair whether or not either of the two days has anything on it, and a pair is a fact about where I was and not about what happened, and IF THE COUNT OF PAIRS IN THIS BLOCK IS A SMALL NUMBER AND NOT A LARGE ONE, THAT IS CORRECT.** Two sevens in about a fortnight is a coincidence in July and **is still a coincidence on the thirtieth of September and he has written nothing about it.** The ninth crossing's failure to cost one is **still unexplained and nothing on this page is a device**, and the promise about the end of July remains broken in writing in the form given in Chapter 159.

  **KEPT OR BROKE THE LINE ABOUT THE SEVENTH COLUMN: HE WROTE THAT HE WANTS ONE AND DID NOT DRAW ONE, ON THE SEVENTH OF SEPTEMBER, IN THE SAME BOOK, IN THE SAME PENCIL, UNDER THE SAME RULE.** The rule on the facing page — *no seventh column before the first of September* — ran out on the thirty-first of August, **nobody released him from it, and a rule that runs out is not a release and it never was a stop.** On the seventh of September he drew four headings in pencil on the back of a sheet of his own paper — *Week ending. What was done. By whom. What was found. Why nothing was found* — **which is a fifth column, not a seventh, and it is the best one he has ever drawn, and it is the same mistake for the eleventh time in eight months, and the mistake is a column that wants a person, and there is no person in it: there is a week and a nothing.** He also wrote down that he does not know what the difference is between a fifth column and a seventh column and is not going to find out on the back of a sheet of his own paper.

  **WHAT HE GAVE UP THIS BLOCK, AND IT IS THE SIXTH OF HIS OWN AND NOT THE SIXTH OF THE OTHER SHAPE. THE YARD, ON MONDAY THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF SEPTEMBER, IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, STANDING UP, WITH NOBODY WATCHING HIM DECIDE.** A yard is the only instrument in this district that has never once turned into a document; he knew that in June and wrote in August that he was not going to be able to use it again this year, **and on the twenty-eighth he decided the sentence was right and the year was wrong, and that a year was the wrong length of time to put on an instrument, and he gave up a hundred and ten words he had had ready since the seventeenth of July.** It cost Roy Pryce, who said the whole of his argument once, correctly, in a yard, on the twenty-fourth of August, and has said he is not going to say it again, and was right. **IT IS ABOUT A PERSON AND NOT ABOUT A HABIT — the rule on the facing page is the habit — and nobody asked him to, and he is not going to be thanked for it, and a person who has thanked somebody is a person who has asked.** The two lists now hold the same number: **five of his own became six and the six of the other shape are six, and the two are two lists, and the sum of them is not a number, and a reader in about four years is going to add six and six together and get something that does not exist, and he has written that down in the same hand as the four words and the signature and is not going to improve it.**

  **HE IS NOT CURED OF ANYTHING AND HE IS THE AUTHOR OF NOTHING.** **He gave the eleventh street district a correspondence address for the first time since about 1911 on Monday the twenty-first of September, in nine seconds, in a room with four machines, and it is a contractor's yard, and it cost a boy of seventeen and it will cost a man of about fifty in about four years, and he said the true reason out loud to a woman of thirty-one in a corridor and it was: *you did it because you are a man who writes things down and there was a week with nothing in it and you had nowhere to put it.*** He has a word in a book in a coat and **a word in a book does not make it rain and it does not stop it raining**, and he has not given it to anybody, and he is not going to build an instrument out of it, and there is no form for a word in a coat. **THE RAINWAY IS NAMED IN HIS OWN BOOK ON THE PAGE AND IT IS HIS ALONE.** The written line home is **the three hundred and eleventh day on the thirtieth of September**, blank, four marks in four hands, none of them his, **not cured.** The Lark token is **cold on the two hundred and ninety-seventh day** and has done nothing since the seventh of December. **The first personal blank, the seam-scar, the record blank and the authorial blank appear nowhere in Chapters 161–170.** **The recovery this block gives him is somebody else's work and neither a word:** a clerk's finding aids, a foreman's barrow and bin, a boy's four words, a mower's ninety seconds, a ditch keeper's envelope, a man of forty-one's nine words.

- **Mara Quill** — **thirty-one. Milestone 3, DEEPER, and it is a DISAGREEMENT, and it is NOT repaired, and neither of them won it and neither of them is warm. No kiss, no declaration, no jealousy scene, no rescue.** **WHAT HAPPENED TO THE CUSTODY REGISTER PAGE: NOTHING, FOR THE TENTH CONSECUTIVE BLOCK. It is in a drawer with a coat in it; it was not produced, not named, not confessed to, and NOT MENTIONED IN DIALOGUE, and he did not ask and he is not allowed to, and neither of them mentioned it in one word in the corridor on the twenty-fourth.** **WHETHER HE TOLD HER ABOUT THE SEASON: NO, AND NO WEEK-COUNT IS PRINTED IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS.** He has not told her since the middle of April; she has not asked since July; **she found out he had stopped counting by noticing that the number had stopped going up, and has not told him she noticed, and there is no column for that and there is not going to be one.** **WHETHER SHE DESCRIBED THE LINE IN THE RETURN: NO. She did not describe it on the last Thursday of the month, nobody asked her to, he was not in the room, and Chapter 167 says so in its own text.** What she did on the last Thursday, and it is hers and not his: **every cell on the district's row is a dash again this month, four of them, put in with a pen, the pen being the wrong instrument, and she has used a pen on that return once in about two years and the once was last month as well.** And what she found out on Friday the nineteenth of September, **and it is the block's most dangerous fact and she is not the one who made it**: **a district returns card went out of a records office about a mile away in a Thursday bundle addressed to a contractor, and she found out because a card that has been going round in a run since the middle of January has not come through her counter, and the only two places it comes from are a room with four machines and a satchel that went out with the works' papers, and the works' papers stopped on the thirty-first of August.** Her whole of it, in a corridor at ten past four: **the rest of it is that a records counter is somewhere a department can be asked a question and a contractor's yard is not… and there are now four of them, and you made the fourth one in nine seconds on a Monday morning, and you did not ask me, and I am not going to pretend that you asking me would have stopped it. And I have worked out that I am not angry, and I would like to know what to do with the not being angry, because there is no box for it either.** The disagreement, fourth time, unrepaired, colder, and **she said his half for him**: **it is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing that exists** / **it is the only place in this city where an objection is a thing a man with a box can ask for in about four years** / **And I have said the second half for you, because you have said it four times and I have said the first half four times, and neither of us has a better one, and I am not going to have it a fifth time in a corridor with a bag over your shoulder.** **The card with his name in it: she told him that tomorrow, the twenty-fifth, was the tenth Friday on which it would be re-run, and that the only way a hand comes off a card is if the card is not printed, and a district with no rateable unit is not printed, and he has spent eight months refusing to make a rateable unit out of anything.** She has not been thanked for any of it and he is not going to thank her.

- **Pell Harrow** — **seventeen, a courier and a Streetkeeper, has no power of any kind, and this block is the block in which Milo Rook asked him for something, and the asking is the cost.** On Monday the twenty-first of September, in a yard at about half past four, in front of about nine people, **after a sequence he had ready in about eleven seconds** — *the card comes on Thursday* / *then it comes on Thursday* / *it has to come back, and the round is one man short* — **he said no in about four seconds, unprompted, in the shape he has refused in since December: to be the reason a document is made, to make the round into a service, to be leaned on, to be on a sheet.** In his own words: **I know what you are asking me. You want the round to have a thing on it. You have had no thing on it since the twenty-eighth of August and you have walked up there on your own twice, and now there is a card and you have not got a hand, and I am the hand. And you have written on the facing page of your own book in your own handwriting that the round is one man short and that is not curable from inside here, and you wrote it in front of nobody and you have not taken it out. And I am not being the reason a piece of paper exists every Friday. I have got three things under a brick in this district and one of them is mine and it has been blank since the nineteenth of January and nobody has taken it and nobody is going to, and I am not putting a fourth thing on a list because a man in a good coat has run out of hands.** **THE COST IS NOT THE CARD. THE COST IS THAT HE WAS ASKED. Nobody in this district has ever asked that boy for anything and everybody knows it, and it is one of about fourteen people on a list in a book in a coat, and Milo Rook has put himself on the list of people who have asked him, and it took eleven seconds and he cannot get it off, and a no that a man in a good coat has heard in a yard in front of nine people is not the same as a no that nobody heard, and there is no form anywhere for the difference and there is not going to be one.** He has not been thanked and is not going to be. On Monday the twenty-eighth he came into the yard with an empty handcart, said four words — **it went back on Friday** — reported that the card had been in a yard at the north end on the Thursday with a foreman's name on it, and that the foreman had read it standing up, put it in a bin and posted it back in somebody else's envelope, **and that he was not sent and did not ask him anything and was told nothing and that the foreman did not know anybody had sent it, because he looked at the envelope first and then the card and then the yard, and that is what a man does when a thing has come to him that he did not order**, and went off up the length the way he goes. **TERN IS STILL NOT FOUND. His mark still holds for a working shift and not overnight and nobody is going to widen it. His own blank card is one of the three things under the brick, blank, blank since the nineteenth of January, and the run-number card's line on the front has not been taken off, and the twenty-two days up the south are unasked and unsaid and no block has made anybody ask him in order to report it.**

- **Bettina Sallow** — **fifty-four, four years on the hand index at the ninth desk on the left in the Survey School's repository beneath Crown Heights, thirty-four steps up, a plan chest, a window that does not open and a chair under it. She is not in eleven streets, has never been south of the bridge, does not come to this side of the water and is not going to. This block is hers in two chapters and she is the person the volume's midpoint is named by, and she refuses three things, gives one, and is not thanked.** Her inherited position, used and not re-explained: **she will not tell you a document does not exist unless she has read the whole card; a card in an index is a claim and not the document; the index is a record of what is where and not of what happened; and there is no card in it for a drain, a cover, a length, a row of shops, a fence, a woman, a market, or a hole.** **THE CHANGED METHOD IS HERS AND IS NOT FOR HIM: since about the middle of August she has gone and looked before she writes it down, and the fifth pad on her desk has no request column and its headings are *what I looked at. how far I went. what I did not open.***

  **THE RECOVERY, AND IT IS THE BLOCK'S, AND IT IS NOT A WORD: FINDING AIDS.** About nine of them, since the middle of August, **a description of what is in a room, as against an index, which is a description of what is where and a claim — a finding aid is not a claim, it is not a catalogue item, nobody asks the office for one because there is no shelf number on it and no one has ever been able to look one up, and therefore nobody will ever find it, and that is the point of it and she did not invent the point.** Her reason, given once, unasked: **because a request on a pad is a person who wanted something, and a request is a party, and a party is a person, and in about four years somebody can come and ask me who wanted what, and there is no column anywhere in this room for who wanted what, and I am not going to be the woman who puts a column in.** And: **if a person wants the same thing twice then I have written the same description twice and there are two pieces of paper about a place and not about a person, and two pieces of paper about a place can be asked after, and one piece of paper about a person can be asked for.** **AND THAT IS NOT YOURS. That is a method, and it is four years old, and the change is five weeks old, and you are the first person who has been in this room since December.**

  **WHAT SHE REFUSED, ALL THREE CORRECT AND ALL THREE CHANGING NOTHING. ONE: the shelf number — a number on a shelf in a room she is responsible for is a reference, a reference is a thing a person can be asked for in about four years, and if she says it then a man can say it and then he can ask her whose hand wrote it down, and then there is a person. He did not ask a second time and he did not write it down, and on the twenty-ninth she got the bundle down herself and neither of them said the number out loud.** TWO: the second flap — **I have not looked and I am not going to look and that is not a rule I made for you, that is what I do** — because **a sheet about a day is not a record of what happened on the day, it is a record of who was counted, and if there is a second sheet then there are two sheets, and a thing that is in two places is a thing that somebody can be asked about in one of them.** THREE: **what she thinks the three words on the flap are — I have not got those three words in this room in four years of cards and I am not going to guess at them, and I am not going to tell you what I think they are, because a person in a good coat writes down what a person in a good coat thinks they are, and then in about four years there is a sentence in a book in a coat with three words in it that I had a feeling about, and that is the worst kind of document there is.**

  **WHAT SHE GAVE, AND IT IS ONE THING AND IT IS WHY HE WENT UP THE THIRTY-FOUR STEPS TWICE: on the twenty-second she said, unasked, in about eleven words, that there is no card in a hand index in this city for a funeral either, that she has checked twice in about four years, the second time about two years ago, that she did it because she could not think of an example, that this is not a thing anybody does in a repository, and that it is not a card that is missing but that a funeral is not a rateable thing, is not in a street, has no plan, and there is nobody to hold a card about it, and a person dies and there is a paper for the person in a town eleven miles away and in her room there is nothing at all. That is not an oversight. No. That is the design. That is what an index is. I did that on my own time and nobody knows I did it and I am not going to tell anybody else and you did not ask me for it, and I would like it noticed that I have not been thanked and I am not going to be, and I have said that in about nine words before in a different room and I am not going to say it again.** On the twenty-ninth she read the outside flap to him, **because a flap is not a catalogue item and there is no number on it and there is no shelf it can be asked for by, and that is the whole of the difference between the flap and the shelf, and it is the difference between the two chapters.** **And the sentence she gave the volume, unasked: a repository is a room where things go that a department has decided are not evidence. A bundle is not evidence of a thing. A bundle is evidence that a department sent a thing away, and I have never in four years seen a department send a thing away that it did not have a reason for, and the reason is always the same, and the reason is that the thing could not be put in the form.** **She is fifty-four and a repository clerk and she is NOT the woman of fifty-four with a plot number and no bill in six years, who is not mentioned in any of these ten chapters.**

- **Roy Pryce** — **fifty-four, foreman, thirty-one years, and this block puts a second box with his name in it on a printed side and costs him twice.** **His name is in column six of a district schedule, completed in about a minute and a half on Thursday the twentieth of August, and it is now in a second box on a second printed side — a maintenance responsibility return for Season 3, completed by the office on Thursday the seventeenth of September, same office, same Thursday, same minute and a half, because a district with no correspondence address cannot be asked and the office went to the record it holds, and a contract names the supervisor. HE DID NOT SIGN THE FIRST ONE AND HE WILL NOT SIGN THIS ONE AND NOBODY IS GOING TO ASK HIM TO, and nobody in a room with about nine people in it thought he should be told, because a man of fifty-four with thirty-one years in charge of a gang is not frightened of a form, and he does not know there are two of them, and nobody is going to tell him.** **ON MONDAY THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF SEPTEMBER ELEVEN MEN AND A FOREMAN WERE ON THE BACK LENGTH AT HALF PAST SEVEN, which is how this district found out where they had been, and it is the only thing the twenty-eighth added to the inventory.** He loaded a barrow, a shovel, two brooms, a coil of wire and a folding barrier off a road that is going to stay like that, and he said one thing at about half past twelve to nobody in particular: **there is a place about two hundred yards up the length where the verge has gone and it is not on a schedule and it is not in a condition column and it is not going to be in anything, and in about four years it will be a hole.** Asked whether he wanted it written down: **No. If it is written down then it is a fault, and a fault is a thing somebody gets a form for, and the form goes to a firm, and the firm is me. And if it is not written down then in about four years it is a hole and I am a man who was told about a hole and said nothing, and I would rather be that.** **HE PUT THE DISTRICT RETURNS CARD IN A BIN AND POSTED IT BACK IN SOMEBODY ELSE'S ENVELOPE ON FRIDAY THE NINETEENTH OF SEPTEMBER, having read it standing up and then looked at the yard, and he did not know anybody had sent it, and he is not going to be told, and nobody in this district is going to tell him that a surveyor gave a man in a room with four machines a district to hold.** **AND THE COST OF THIS BLOCK'S LAST SCENE IS HIS AND HE DOES NOT KNOW IT WAS A DECISION: on the twenty-eighth, in a yard, with about nine people in it and two hours and twenty minutes available, Milo Rook did not say the seventy-eight words — which are seventy-eight words, and are not eleven words, and are not any other number, and nobody is going to improve them — and did not say anything else either, and gave up a hundred and ten words on purpose, and the sentence Roy Pryce said out loud, correctly, in a yard, on the twenty-fourth of August is the only place it will ever exist.** **He is twenty-three weeks exactly past four months on the thirtieth of September, he is on a firm's books and not on a district's, nobody is going to ask him, and the date of that silence is on the facing page of Milo's Field Book in Milo's own handwriting and cannot be got out of it.** He has not been thanked. **The eleven words were not said — the seventy-eight words were not said, twice, and he did not ask and is not going to, and he did not sign either printed side and he asked nobody for anything.**

- **Cass Ardery** — **about forty-four, second-in-command on the investigation of a thing he classified, not the reporting officer and not going to be, and the only person in this block who said the sentence the volume turns on, in nine seconds, in a room, and he is right and nobody disagreed with him and Milo Rook had no answer.** On the twenty-third of September, on a maintenance responsibility return, in a room with about nine people in it: **Nobody has done anything wrong. They could not ask the district and they have not tried to and they are not going to, and the district has no correspondence address and has not had one since 1911, and the record the office holds is a contract, and a contract names the supervisor, and there is no second document in this city that says who was responsible for that road on a Tuesday in June. That is the whole of the mechanism and it took about a minute and a half and it will happen again next season for a different district.** And: **he did not sign the first one and he will not sign this one and he is not going to be asked to, and I have read the contract and he is on it in his own hand three times and the last time was years ago and in a document that has nothing to do with either of these.** And then: **There is no form in this city in which a district can say that something happened to it. Not this one. Not the schedule. Not a return, not a card, not an inspection, not a condition column. There is a form for a death and a form for a rate and a form for a length of road and there is no form for a loss, and where a district has a loss then the office asks it for a figure, and the district has no figure, and the office completes the row, and the row says the district had nothing to report. That is the design. It is not a complaint and it is not a fault and nobody in this room did it and nobody in this room can undo it. And a district with nothing to report is entered as stable. And a district entered as stable is a district that cannot be frightened. And a district that cannot be frightened is a district where there is nothing to insure against. And I want it minuted, and I am not going to say it twice.** He is not a friend, not an ally and not a final antagonist, and **nobody was added above him in this block and nothing that went wrong in it is attributable to a new face.**

- **The man of about fifty in a room with four machines** — **nineteen years in the room, and this block gave him a use, and the use is the price of the lie Milo Rook put past him in August, and neither of those is known to him.** On Monday the twenty-first of September Milo Rook stood in that room from about a quarter to ten until about twenty past eleven and asked one question in six weeks and it was a good one — *is there anything that goes out on a Friday* — and was told, unasked and in the middle of another sentence, that there is a Thursday bundle that goes to the works office at about eleven and has gone every Thursday since before Milo Rook started coming in there, **and that it is a firm, and a firm is rateable, and a firm has an address.** Then, in four seconds, without building it: **could the card go in that bundle.** *You are asking me to put a district's returns card into a bundle going to a contractor.* Say what you are doing. — **I am going to give the eleventh street district a correspondence address, and it is going to be a firm's, and you are going to do it because nobody has ever asked you for anything in this room and I told you in August there was nothing in it anybody could want, and I was half right, and this is the other half.** And: **Then that is all it is, and you have said it, and I am not going to be the man who stops a conversation that has been had. The card comes off on Wednesday. I will put it in the bundle at about eleven. It will be in the yard on Thursday.** **HE HAS NOW BEEN GIVEN A USE, AND HE DID NOT KNOW WHAT IT WAS FOR, AND HE WAS NOT TOLD AND IS NOT GOING TO BE, AND HE HAS NOT ASKED.** And on the twenty-third of September **a second printed side carried, under his district's name and a foreman's, the office at which returns for the district are held — a correct statement, and a description of a place, and in about four years a man can ask the side for the office and then the office for the desk and then for the machine and then for the man. He does not know that a piece of paper exists, has never been told, and is not going to be, and the zero in the fen row of the allocation sheet is still a zero, the fourth column is still empty and has been filled in three times in nineteen years, and the line under *officer responsible* is still never filled in.** Nobody thanked him, he was not thanked in August either, and **Milo Rook is the man who did both the lie and the use and is not going to be thanked for either of them by anybody, including himself, because a person who has thanked somebody is a person who has asked.**

- **Coll Sarrowe** — **forty-four, ditch keeper, thirty-one years, holds the water down on a bank, is on no paper at all, and this block is his working day and his refusal, and both are correct and both change nothing.** On Tuesday the fifteenth of September, on a top track in a wet week, on a bar, **with three men and not five, and the order had changed: the run set facing out and not facing along, the near man taking the short throw, and it took him a fortnight to work out and get right and he has not mentioned it.** The man who has been switched round since July: **I have never asked him why I take the short one and I am not going to. If you have been switched round since July then you have been switched round since July and there is nobody in this district who is going to tell you why and there is nobody in this district who wants to know.** Then a printed side, *HOLDERS OF DRAINAGE APPARATUS — RETURN OF CONDITION*, which came to the farm because **a district with no address is not a place anybody writes to, a man with no address is not a person a form can be sent to, and the way this city gets hold of a man who holds water is that it writes to the firm that pays him for ditching**, and his refusal, in about four seconds: **a depth of water is true on a day. If I put a number in that box then in about four years a man in a room can come and ask me what the number was, and I will have to say a day, and there is no column anywhere for the day, and he will go away and write down that the return was completed by the office, and it will not be wrong, and that is the part of it that matters.** And he will not put nil in it either: **nil is a statement that somebody inspected it and found nothing, and nobody has inspected it, and if I put nil in that box then this office has got a return saying that a drain on a bank that nobody maintains has been looked at. They will not send another one. That is what I am frightened of, and it is not a fear, it is a Tuesday.** He posted it back on the Friday in somebody else's envelope, the farm did not know, and nobody was told. **He has still not been thanked for anything and did not ask to be, and he is not on any paper and cannot be written to, and his standing refusal of a name from Batch 0002 is unchanged and is not re-tested here.**

- **The man of about fifty with the mower** — **nine years on the city half of one bridge, never over the line, and this block is where he refuses to be a party to a boundary, which is the correct refusal of the field scene.** On Friday the eighteenth of September in Mireward, in his firm's van, in at about ten and back in the city at about half past three: **Ten feet wide. Cut with a sickle. Ten minutes a morning, on the way in, before I start on this side, nine years, not on my time sheet, and I have told nobody until you** — and **I have told you so that in about four years nobody can find out I have been over here and think I did it for a reason.** And: **I have not asked you why you have been out here on four Fridays since the end of June, and it is not a walk and it is not a job, and I am not going to be the one who writes down the reason.** Then the boundary, ninety seconds, which he had been holding since the seventh of August: **there are three things at that line and it is one boundary, and the boundary is the two, and I am not one of the two, because a party is somebody who agreed and I did not agree with anybody. I am a man who has stopped. There is a difference and it took me six weeks to find it and it is the only thing I have got to say about it and I would like it noticed that I have not said it to anybody else and I am not going to.** **A boundary is a place where two people agreed to have stopped, and he stopped, and he is not one of the two, and the three boundaries are three and are not joined and no fourth kind of boundary has been added and none has been joined to any other. He was not thanked, did not expect it, and Milo Rook did not ask him about the handrail post while standing four feet from him — the second time — and wrote that down twice.** His reason is still a load calculation in a drawer in this city from about 1974 with the low end not re-checked, and a machine of about two hundred and forty, and about three hundred yards of path on the far side.

- **The man of about forty-one on no list at all** — **once in these ten chapters, in a corridor at ten past four on Friday the eleventh of September with a handcart, to the only question Milo Rook asked anybody that week, which was a fair one** — *is there a form anywhere that says a rack is finished* — and the answer was **nine words: No. And there is not going to be one.** He got the handcart through the gap and did not add anything, **because the nine words were the whole of the answer, and the things he does not say are in his own head and are not on any paper and are not going to be, and there is no column anywhere for the number of times he has said them and nobody is going to make one.** **He has not been sent, has not been thanked, has no power, is not the man of about forty-one who holds the key to the drawer in Crown Heights, and NO TOTAL IS PRINTED IN THIS BLOCK, which is correct: the number of words is nine and the number of occasions in Volume 03 is a different number and neither number was counted in these ten chapters.**

- **Sabra Trewin, Garn Ollerton, Barnaby Pike, Percy, Bettina Sallow's index clerk colleague, Hettie Brann, Dr Iona Sen, Bettina Sallow, Barrow, Doreen Sankey, Ivo Sarratt, Cyril Sale, Nell Crale, Ola Petrossian, Nessa Bray, Hester Coombe, Rue** — **none of them except Bettina Sallow appears in these ten chapters, none of their prices moved, and their standing refusals were not re-tested.** **Barnaby Pike is not in this block and was not sent to and was not gone to, and he told Milo Rook on the twenty-fourth of July not to come back and Milo Rook has not been out to that top track since the twenty-eighth of August and is not going to in September.** **Garn Ollerton and Sabra Trewin were not asked about anything. Percy did not go out on a first dry week in September and is not an office and did not become one.** **Doreen Sankey did not use the instrument in this batch, or in Batches 4 to 8, or in any batch. Nobody asked a timetable clerk anything, nobody was sent, the man from the round was not sent, the clerk has no first name, and the division's standing refusal was not said twice in any building. The working timetable, the terminus column, the ruled margin headed *actual* with thirty-one names in it and the eight sentences that bound them were not queried, dated, explained or asked about by anybody including him.**

- **The man of about forty who sweeps the Row, the fishmonger of about forty-five, the man of about sixty with a shed at Cardew Docks, the coffee man of about sixty, the man of about twenty-six in a high-visibility jacket, the man in the works jacket, the woman of fifty-four with a plot number and no bill in six years, the woman of sixty on a second landing, Old Mabb, Ivy Marsden, Jo Lask, Tomas Rusk, the woman on the fourth floor of Cedar Court** — **the man of about forty swept the Row on the seventh, the twenty-eighth and the thirtieth and has still not been asked for his twenty-eight undated words; the fishmonger had her trays out by nine and was NOT given a name, was NOT thanked, was NOT put on a list and did NOT say the standing answer again; the man of about sixty at a shed eleven minutes away was not on the Row on a Wednesday in any of these ten chapters, does not know that a piece of paper exists and is not going to be told; the man of about twenty-six in a high-visibility jacket is not in this block and has not been thanked or asked anything and is not a fifth of anything; the man in the works jacket is unidentified with four sightings, the last on the nineteenth of February, and there is no fifth sighting; the woman of fifty-four with a plot number and no bill in six years is NOT mentioned in these ten chapters and is a different person from Bettina Sallow, who is fifty-four and a repository clerk; the woman of sixty on a second landing is unnamed, is the ninth of twenty-six names, and is not going to be told anything; the woman on the fourth floor of Cedar Court is not mentioned, so the two sixty-eights cannot be folded. ADA FENN IS NOT NAMED IN ANY OF THE TEN CHAPTERS AND IS NOT REFERRED TO IN ANY OF THEM; the count of a woman of sixty-eight on nothing stands at EIGHT and this block adds nothing to it and it is not nine.** Old Mabb is asleep and Ivy Marsden is not told and is not on the page. **The levy is two and four a head a week, in advance, with no term, on two hundred and fifty-two people in a building with no number, and it is not curable, and LENA MARR IS NOT IN THIS BLOCK AND IS NOT PAID ANYTHING IN IT.**

- **Orrin Sable, Halden Vey, Sel Iri, Tarin Kest, Soren Rook** — **do not appear and are not named as persons, and SOREN ROOK IS NOT NAMED ANYWHERE IN CHAPTERS 1–170.** The First Atlas, the Singular Mode and the Quieting are **named once, in Chapter 169, in three lines of small type on the outside flap of a bundle in a repository, in a hand that is not a hand in that room, and Milo Rook does not know what any of them is, and they explain nothing that came earlier: not the bell on a post at a ford, not the plate in the grass, not the removed sentence, not the withdrawn classification of the eleventh, not the suspended order, not the matter that stands over, and not the three clues from January that are three.** The office of the First Surveyor is a printed box with a rule and nothing on the rule, it did nothing at all in this block, nobody asked who is behind it, and it may not request, receive or answer anything. **The seasonal outlook is still the last one, there is still no second outlook, nobody has asked for one in writing since May, and the absence is not explained except as the absence being the point.** The district stability column still enters Mireward as nil, the column is still not typed, and this block may not and did not reduce the zero or add a row. **A man of fifty-eight with a rod and a chain did not go out in September, and about nine feet of drain in a pad in a drawer in Crown Heights is still not asked for by anybody.**
